# Malaysia: research findings

Merged from the per-topic notes in `findings-parts/`. Ledger ids like [W0012] point to rows in ledger.csv.


# Findings, part A: recruitment and real costs (practical pass)

Researched 6 Nov 2026. Ledger ids are in `ledger-parts/A.csv` (A0001-A0112). Every row is a news, NGO or research source: status `unofficial` (what happens in practice, to be written as "unofficial, not verified") or `conflict` (3 rows, each naming the official row that is the rule). None of these rows may be the basis of a fee, eligibility, document, processing-time or legal rule. Sources older than 6 Nov 2025 are marked with their year; 2024 items were kept only where nothing newer was found (complaint outcomes, debt survey, 2024 refund process).

Sources: about 60 pages saved to raw/ (59 by curl, 1 by Firecrawl: the NSU policy brief); 50 registered and used (all `--official no`). The other saved pages were duplicates, too old (2011) or off-topic. Key outlets: Daily Star / ANN, Prothom Alo, Dhaka Tribune, TBS, Daily Observer, bdnews24 (Bangla), New Nation, BSS, FMT, Malaysiakini, The Star, Malay Mail, Rakyat Post, ILO, Verité, NSU CMS.

## Where things stand (context for the "Is recruitment open now?" paragraph)

- **The 2026 arrangement.** 338 entities: 25 principal agencies chosen by Malaysia from Bangladesh's list of 423, 312 associate agencies (10 per principal) and BOESL with 62 associates; associates can send workers only with a principal agency's or BOESL's approval [A0005, A0028; matches official W0008]. FWCMS published the 25 on 21 Aug 2026 [A0017]. Reported mechanism: employers holding approvals choose one of the 25; other agencies reportedly pay one of them a Tk 6,000 processing fee [A0019]; employers are expected to nominate agencies of their choice [A0007]. Prothom Alo found 17 of the 25 had never sent a worker to Malaysia [A0027]; the experience, office and training-centre criteria were relaxed at Bangladesh's request [A0010].
- **Zero-cost 10,000.** Per a Ministry release of 28 Aug 2026 (not online), Malaysia agreed to take 10,000 workers at zero cost, including airfare, first batch via BOESL on chartered flights [A0033, A0034]. In Oct 2026 employers had started issuing demand letters and paying levies on FWCMS; the High Commission in KL had begun attesting demands (first example: 28 workers for NSK Trade City under Goodness Services Ltd) [A0035]. BOESL says the employer alone picks among applicants [A0036]. The medical process was "not yet approved" in early Oct 2026 [A0038]; a Paltan centre doing unapproved Malaysia medicals was sealed on 1 Oct 2026 [A0039]. This matches the BOESL DHL circular (official W0001-W0006).
- **Policy statements.** Apr 2026 joint statement: employers bear the full cost, zero cost to workers, under the ILO Employer Pays Principle; Malaysia plans an AI-based / direct-hiring platform (TURAP, by Bestinet, FWCMS's developer) [A0097, A0022, A0104, A0100]. Jun 2026 PMs' meeting: fewer intermediaries, lower costs [A0103, A0105]. The 2021 MoU runs to Dec 2026 and must be renewed [A0018, A0025]. Gradual reopening: about 800 vetted workers entered construction in Dec 2025-Jan 2026 [A0023]; the "last week of August 2026" start date did not happen [A0021].

## Step 1: Find a real job. What workers report

**What workers actually paid in 2022-24 (unofficial, not verified).** Average about Tk 5.44 lakh against the official Tk 78,990 [A0001, A0098 (Tk 5-6 lakh)]; Verité: about USD 5,000, usually through several loans [A0072 (2024)]; NSU/UniSZA: RM17,000-25,000 [A0068]; NSU policy brief: Tk 3-4 lakh, mostly borrowed [A0110]; FMT: up to RM20,000 [A0024]; OKUP case study: every male worker paid above the legal limit, up to six times for Malaysia [A0081]; costs as high as USD 6,600 per a 2024 memo to Malaysia's PM [A0070]. This is consistent with existing rows W0070, W0072, B0124.

**Where the money goes (who gets it).** In 2022-24, job orders were routed only to 101 listed agencies; other agencies had to process through them [A0006], paying the listed agency about Tk 1.07-1.52 lakh per worker as a "syndicate fee" [A0002, A0028] and Tk 1.6-2 lakh to middlemen for the demand letter [A0002]. Bloomberg: a "syndicate fee" of about USD 1,350; workers who did not pay cash allegedly did not get their passports back [A0069]. Agencies paid to join the syndicate and recouped it from workers [A0107 (2024)]. For 2026, BAIRA members allege a RM5,500 (Tk 1.68 lakh) syndicate fee plus RM8,000-9,000 to buy each visa (over Tk 4.3 lakh per worker), with total worker costs of Tk 6-7 lakh [A0026]; others say costs could pass Tk 6 lakh and visas could cost about RM7,000 [A0003, A0004]; an alleged Tk 17 crore entry payment per agency and RM5,000 per worker [A0011]. All allegations, not verified.

**Agency practices to warn about.** Collecting passports before any official go-ahead: a BMET mobile court sealed Triple A Overseas (RL-2630) on 10 Sep 2026, fined it Tk 50,000 and seized three boxes of workers' passports [A0043, A0044]. Workers pressured to sign or record false declarations that they paid only the official fee [A0061]. Extra payments demanded after the fact and jobs changed without consent [A0062]. No contract before departure (75% in OKUP's cases) [A0082] (contrast the official rule W0030). Jobs that do not exist on arrival; employers over-recruit or recruit to subcontract illegally [A0014, A0016, A0071, A0095]. Even BOESL-recruited workers reportedly paid more than five times the official limit (UN experts, Nov 2025) [A0060].

**Ministry warnings (official text not online).** Don't pay, do medicals or hand over passports until an official notice [A0033, A0031]; agencies must not sign contracts, collect passports, arrange medicals or take money [A0040, A0008]. Complaints: Expatriate Call Center 16135 (+880 961-010-2030 from abroad); Malaysia focal point Md. Hedayetul Islam Mondal, +880 241-030-241, dsemployment1@probashi.gov.bd, +880 171-604-8115 [A0041, A0042]; need the official notice before the doc uses these. The HC in KL warned of a fraud ring taking passports and cash for "Sabah" jobs; there is no Sabah agreement [A0102].

**Loans and debt.** 96% of surveyed Malaysia workers took at least one loan for recruitment, 82% two or more, and 73% spent 50-100% of monthly pay on repayments [A0064, A0065 (2024)]. Loans are mostly informal and paid straight to a third party, with no record [A0066]; families sell land or borrow [A0015, A0106]. Recruiters themselves proposed PKB instalment migration loans [A0013]. Nothing found on PKB loan use for Malaysia in 2025-26 (the doc's PKB facts come from official B0013-B0019).

**What helps (evidence).**
- BOESL government channel: zero-cost in principle. Track record: about 1,381-1,500 workers sent at no cost in 2022-24 against demand for 10,000+ [A0059 (2024), A0030]. But the stranded-worker scheme cost Tk 1,62,500 (official W0020), reportedly Tk 1 lakh of it a "visa purchase fee" [A0056]; BOESL sent only 190 in about six months [A0057]; workers complained of harassment [A0055]; UN experts reported BOESL recruits paying more than five times the limit [A0060]. So BOESL's zero-cost circulars (W0001-W0005) are the official route, but there is no outside evidence yet on how the 2026 zero-cost scheme works in practice.
- Fair-recruitment pilots: none found for Malaysia. ILO's only Bangladesh pilot cited is Qatar construction 2018-21 (92% cost drop) [A0092]. No agency in the Malaysia corridor has outside evidence of low fees; **do not name any agency**. The 25 FWCMS agencies are listed (official W0008), but news gives no evidence about their fees.
- Employer fee repayment in Malaysia happens only under pressure: Mediceram agreed in 2025 to repay RM22,500 each after workers went to a rights group [A0090], paid on 27 Oct 2025, then terminated all of them on 31 Oct 2025 and kept passports [A0108, A0109].
- Malaysian labour office claims with an NGO can work: 74 workers won a RM1.54 million labour office order against Meranti Binamas through Tenaganita (Jun 2025); 33 are suing in the Shah Alam High Court for RM1.71 million including their RM25,000 fees [A0086, A0087]. Without representation, the 2024 Pengerang workers settled for about half [A0088, A0089 (2024)]. (Part L may use these for "If you aren't paid".)
- ILO definition (benchmark for what is a recruitment fee): any fee or cost to get the job, wherever and whenever charged [A0096]; under the Employer Pays Principle, workers pay only for their passport [A0099].

**Complaints to BMET / the Ministry: what actually happens.** Official route and 30-working-day timeline are in B0034, B0035, B0041, B0044, B0045. In practice (2023 data, newest found): victims say claims take 4 months to a year (conflict row A0073 vs B0034/B0045); BMET resolved about one-third of 2,380 complaints in 2023, awarding Tk 4.87 crore [A0074]; most get under half their loss [A0075]; in Aug 2023 the average award was about Tk 74,000 against Tk 3.5-5 lakh costs [A0076]. Documents decide the outcome: those who can show proof get more; most paid middlemen with no written agreement [A0077, A0079]. A BMET official says an agency's licence is suspended if a complaint stays unresolved for six months through its fault [A0078]. OKUP: BMET both licenses and arbitrates, and there are no compensation standards [A0083]. NGO routes: Ovibashi Odhikar Forum and WARBE do local arbitration, and refer recruiter cases to BMET; complex cases go to court under the 2013 Act or the 2012 Trafficking Act [A0080]. 2013 Act amended (Nov 2024) to cover sub-agents [A0093]. For workers in Malaysia, the HC and labour department did not help the Mediceram workers [A0091, A0109]; HC services were reported "inaccessible and slow" [A0111].

**Enforcement against agencies.** 49 licences cancelled [A0012]; ACC cases against 64 people from 25 agencies over about Tk 2,288 crore [A0085, A0084].

## The 2024 stranded workers (17,777) and refunds

- Cause: agencies sent workers late, causing ticket shortages; fares jumped from Tk 20,000-25,000 to over Tk 1 lakh; some employers were blacklisted [A0054, A0052 (2024), A0051].
- Refund process (2024): the ministry ordered agencies to refund by 18 Jul 2024; workers sign a receipt only after getting the full amount; BAIRA reports the list to the ministry; agencies that don't refund face legal action [A0045, A0046]. Reported progress: 70% (Jul 2024, unconfirmed) [A0047], 81% of agencies (Jan 2025) [A0048]. Workers without visa or BMET card were told to reclaim passport and money from the agency, with ministry help through local police stations [A0049]. High Court writ: reports every three months, and asked why refunds should not carry interest [A0053]. Official W0036: an agency gets no new permissions until it repays the workers it failed to send.
- Going later: BOESL took 7,873 (official W0018/W0020); the first 60 flew on 25 Nov 2025 [A0058]; about 120-150 were still waiting in Oct 2026 [A0037].

## Suggested new official sources (details in blocked-parts/A.md)

Ministry press releases of 28 Aug 2026 and 10 Sep 2026; the 2026 Ministry "no transactions" notice with the Malaysia focal-point contacts; the 8 Apr 2026 joint statement; BOESL's 4 Sep 2025 circular; the HC KL Sabah fraud notice; the 2024 amendment to the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act and the 2025 code of conduct rules; BMET complaint statistics for 2024-26; any BMET Malaysia permission letter under the 2026 system (it would give the official maximum fee for private-agency jobs).

## Draft steps (for Step 1, "Before paying", and Scams)

1. **Don't pay anyone, hand over a passport or do a medical for Malaysia until the Ministry or BOESL publishes a notice** (official: W0020, W0021, W0027; practice: agencies were caught collecting passports in Sep 2026 [A0043]; the Ministry repeated the warning in 2026 [A0033, A0040], unofficial until the notice is found).
2. **Prefer BOESL's zero-cost circulars** (W0001-W0005). Unofficial, not verified: BOESL's past Malaysia programmes were small, and the stranded-worker scheme cost Tk 1,62,500 [A0059, A0056].
3. **Know what workers really paid** (unofficial, not verified): Tk 3-6 lakh in 2022-24 against Tk 78,990 [A0001, A0110, A0098], mostly through loans [A0064]. Agents have alleged 2026 costs of Tk 6-7 lakh [A0026]. **Need to know:** the official maximum for private-agency jobs under the 2026 system.
4. **Never sign a paper saying you paid only the official fee if you paid more**; keep proof of every payment. Workers were pressured to do this [A0061], and proof decides BMET compensation [A0077, A0079].
5. **If cheated:** BMET / Ministry complaint (official B0034-B0045). Unofficial, not verified: it often takes months and awards are usually under half the loss [A0073-A0076]; NGOs (WARBE, Ovibashi Odhikar Forum) can help [A0080]. In Malaysia, an NGO such as Tenaganita can help with labour office claims [A0086].

## Need to know (with what was searched)

- Official 2026 maximum fee for private-agency Malaysia jobs (searched BMET misc-infos, Ministry news/notices, news in English and Bangla).
- Official text of the 2026 Ministry warnings and complaint contacts (probashi.gov.bd news/notices pages show nothing after Dec 2025).
- Whether any Malaysia-corridor agency has outside evidence of low fees (searched ILO, IOM, Verité, Transparentem, BHRRC, news): none found.
- BMET complaint outcomes after 2023; refund completion for the 17,777 after Jan 2025.
- PKB loan use for Malaysia 2025-26.

## Conflicts

- A0020 (40 medical centres, news) vs W0009 (51, FWCMS): official is the rule.
- A0057 (7,869 stranded workers given to BOESL, news) vs W0018/W0020 (7,873): official is the rule.
- A0073 (complaints take 4 months to 1 year in practice) vs B0034/B0045 (30 working days): official is the rule; show both.


# Findings, part B: Bangladesh-side steps, money home, coming home, corridor numbers

Researched 6 Nov 2026. Ledger ids B0001-B0145 in `ledger-parts/B.csv`: 64 verified, 67 possibly-outdated, 11 conflict, 2 lead, 1 unofficial-practice.

All ten PDFs in `official-pdfs/` were read in full. Seven were copied to raw/ and logged under their official URL; the 74-page WEWB annual report, the 28-page ministry charter and the 10-page PKB charter were read page by page from images (reader subagents transcribed the quotes, which I checked against the claims). The Saudi-only PDFs (SVP FAQ, Takamol poster, Saudi skill-test notice, Saudi permission letter) hold nothing Malaysia-specific; the only general points are BMET's 104 TTCs and the standard permission-letter conditions (worker chosen from the BMET database under s.19(3) of the 2013 Act; signed contract with a Bangla translation; payment by cheque/draft/pay order). These were not ledgered as facts for Malaysia.

Newer versions found online: the BMET citizen charter (page updated 30 Sep 2026) and the PKB charter (published 29 Sep 2026, identical to the local copy). The ministry's welfare-fee circular of 20 Sep 2026 changes the welfare fee. The WEWB citizen-charter PDF in `official-pdfs/` was not re-read page by page: its services are covered by the Board's live service pages (most updated 7 Oct 2026), which supersede it.

## Notes by doc section

### Step: register on OEP and train (all work routes)
- OEP registration: employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp in Chrome; details exactly as on the NID (drop dots, correct spaces); OTP; full form; pay by bKash/Nagad; download Registration Card [B0139]. The fee amount is not stated [B0139].
- TTC course: log in, Training portal, Trade/Skills, division/district/centre, Search, Apply, photo must match your face, Process to Enroll, Confirm, My Course [B0140]. 55 trades; short-course fee set by BMET (money receipt); project-funded courses are free and pay a stipend [B0037].
- **Malaysia skill test:** BMET's leaflet tells Malaysia-bound workers to pass the CIDB skill test, run at Khulna TTC, to be added in Dhaka, Chattogram and Rajshahi [B0001]. The leaflet is undated (Part A should confirm current CIDB arrangements).
- OEP hotline 09614445271 (on OEP page; ledger row failed on quote length; it is in raw/oep-country-clearance.md "### 09614445271").

### Before paying (checks, payment, complaints)
- Pay the agency only through the bank into the agency's account; keep receipts [B0003]. Have visa and papers checked at BMET Dhaka or the district office [B0005].
- **Visa check at BMET:** free, 3 working days; bring the visa and the passport page with the visa stamp. Online checks cover only Saudi Arabia, Qatar, Bahrain, Singapore and UAE: **not Malaysia** [B0032, B0033].
- **Complaint to BMET:** self-explanatory application with proof to the DG, BMET (complaint cell); inquiry committee and hearing; free; 30 working days; BMET recommends and the ministry decides [B0034, B0035]. GRS ladder: Director (Employment) diremi@bmet.gov.bd 3 months, then Secretary secretary@probashi.gov.bd 1 month, then grs.gov.bd 3 months [B0041].
- **Complaint to the ministry (enforcement branch):** application + proof + your full address + agency RL number to sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd (02-41030255); hearing of both sides; starts in 3-5 working days, settled in 30 working days; free [B0044, B0045]. For agencies that sent workers without permission: application to the Secretary with smart card, ticket, contract, passport copy; 60 working days [B0046]. Mobile courts under the 2013 Act give immediate remedies, 7 working days [B0047]. Ministry GRS: adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd 30 working days, appeal adsorg@ 20, Cabinet Division 60 [B0053].
- If anyone offers to fix TTC admission/passing or clearance for money, tell the police [B0010].

### PKB migration loan
- Up to Tk 3 lakh, no collateral, 9% simple interest, up to 3 years including grace, monthly instalments, decided in 7 working days [B0017]. Eligibility: Bangladeshi, 18-60, applicant and guarantors permanent residents of the branch area, no loan default [B0013].
- Documents: free form, 3 photos, NID, passport, visa, BMET card (not needed for re-entry visa), contract (optional), 3 signed MICR cheques + statement, PKB savings account (min Tk 500) [B0014]. Re-entry visa: proof of legal remittance, proof of job abroad, cost statement [B0015]. Two guarantors (one solvent with cheques/statement/income proof; one family member), each with photo, NID, address proof [B0016].
- Fees: 0.5% processing + VAT, 0.5% documentation + VAT, Tk 900 per lakh risk cover; 16 Tk 10 revenue stamps; 3 Tk 100 adhesive stamps per guarantor [B0018]. Online application on mygov.bd (one application at a time; pay after decision) [B0019, B0020]. Helpline 16238, from abroad +880 9677-787878 [B0018]; loans dept migration@pkb.gov.bd, 01716-175560 [B0055]; PKB GRS gm2@pkb.gov.bd [B0056].
- Women migrant loan: same Tk 3 lakh / 9% / 3 years [B0025].
- The PKB loan page itself is undated (status possibly-outdated); the rehabilitation and women-loan pages are dated Sep and Aug 2026.

### Police clearance
- Online at pcc.police.gov.bd: passport valid 3+ months; present address = an address in the passport where you live; register, Apply, details, addresses, upload scans, submit [B0105]. Documents: attested scan of passport data page (gazetted officer, or embassy if abroad), NID/birth certificate if MRP has no address [B0106]. Status by SMS "PCC S <ref>" to 26969; helpdesk 01320001824/25 [B0107]. Someone else can collect with an attested authorisation letter [B0108].
- **Fee conflict:** portal says Tk 1,500 treasury challan (code 1-7301-0001-2681) or card [B0105]; police.gov.bd guidance says Tk 500 (code 1-2201-0001-2681) [B0109].

### Attestation/apostille
- Bangladesh joined the Apostille Convention (in force 30 Mar 2025); **Malaysia is not in the HCCH status table**, so an apostille alone is not the route for Malaysia [B0110]. Malaysia's own attestation requirements: Part A/C (BD HC KL "checklist for attestation" page exists on kualalumpur.mofa.gov.bd).

### Pre-departure orientation and BMET clearance
- Compulsory 3-day PDO at 70 institutes; fee set by the institute, paid against a money receipt [B0036, B0012].
- Agency-processed clearance: 3 working days; agency submits application, visa, attested contract, attested OEP registration card, Tk 300 stamp undertaking, original pay-order for welfare fee and smart card fee, original income-tax challan [B0026, B0027].
- **Own-visa clearance:** apply yourself at BMET emigration branch (Dhaka) or DEMO Chattogram: application, visa copy, contract if any, attested OEP card copy, Tk 300 non-judicial stamp declaration, pay-order for welfare fee + insurance; smart card fee Tk 250; 3 working days [B0029, B0030].
- **Amounts (current):** welfare fee **Tk 5,500 from 1 Oct 2026** (ministry circular 20 Sep 2026) [B0043], replacing Tk 3,500 still shown in the BMET charter [B0028]; insurance premium Tk 1,000 one-time [B0008, B0028]; emigration clearance fee Tk 250 per worker; source income tax Tk 400 (unattested visa) / Tk 250 (attested) for single visas, Tk 1,200 skilled / Tk 800 unskilled for group visas [B0028]. Expected total at clearance (my arithmetic, not stated anywhere): Tk 5,500 + 1,000 + 250 + tax.
- Airport desks check smart cards; 1,098 travellers with no/fake clearance were stopped in FY2023-24 [B0038, B0128].
- Before flying: copies of all papers (1 set at home, 3 with you), 2 bank accounts, labour wing contacts [B0002, B0004]; never carry others' packets [B0006].

### After arrival: joining the Welfare Board from Malaysia
- If you came without BMET clearance: apply at portal.wewb.gov.bd; processed by the High Commission labour wing; copy of online application, passport, work permit; fee **equivalent of Tk 6,500 in ringgit, including life insurance** (page 7 Oct 2026) [B0057, B0127]. Workers with BMET clearance don't need membership; check at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check [B0058].

### Sending money home
- Use legal channels, not hundi [B0007].
- Costs from Malaysia: Bangladesh Bank says average cost up to USD 200 was 3.19% in Apr-Jun 2026 (overall 1.12%) [B0095]. World Bank (Q3 2025, older): average 7.51% for MYR 610; MoneyGram 5.05%, Western Union 5.15%, NBL/Agrani 5.32%, Merchantrade 5.49%, CBL 5.50%; banks 8.9-15.8% [B0096, B0097].
- Check a remitter is licensed on BNM's MSB licensee list / approved agents list; report illegal remittance to BNM's contact centre (BNMTELELINK 1-300-88-5465) [B0098-B0100]. Agrani Remittance House is a licensed Class B remitter with branches in KL, Rawang, Klang, Bukit Mertajam, JB, Seremban [B0101].
- bKash partners in Malaysia: Ria, CBL, Mycash Online, Mobilemoney Ripple, Merchantrade, Al Jadeed, Wise, Global Money Exchange, NBL, Aman Exchange, WorldRemit, Placid Express, Touch 'n Go, Western Union, MoneyGram; give receiver's bKash number and full name; ATM cash-out Tk 7 per thousand [B0102, B0103].
- 2.5% government incentive: stated by bKash [B0103] and news (TBS, June 2026) [B0104 lead]; Bangladesh Bank circular blocked.

### If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job
- Body from Malaysia: company letter to the High Commission; employer appoints casket company; papers (company letters, insurance claim letter, compensation letter, hospital letter, death certificate, Malaysian police report, passport) to the HC; family's claim/consent letter naming the receiver; tell the airport desk [B0119]. Bodies normally come home at the employer's cost; the Board pays if no employer/employer refuses/community can't help (Tk 10 lakh spent for Malaysia in FY24) [B0125]. Family applies to the Board (call 16135) [B0074]; or to the ministry (aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd) with death certificate etc., 7 working days, incl. undocumented workers [B0051]. Burial in Malaysia: family's written view sent via the Board [B0075].
- Airport: Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial for every body; collect within 3 months; documents: warish certificate, nominee photo and NID/passport, deceased's passport, NOC, death certificate, airway bill [B0066, B0067, B0039, B0038].
- Tk 3 lakh grant: if BMET clearance or Board membership; only for death in the country of clearance; no application needed if the airport cheque was taken; if buried abroad apply within 1 year; heirs = spouse, children, parents (else full siblings); documents: family certificate countersigned by UNO, Tk 400 stamp indemnity, embassy NOC/death certificate, bank certificate with routing number, photos, passport copy; paid by BEFTN with SMS [B0060-B0065]. (Saudi doc's "60 working days" is not on the current page.)
- Insurance (Jiban Bima Corporation): Tk 10 lakh death, total disability Tk 10 lakh, partial 10-90%, Tk 50,000 if job lost and back within 6 months; 5 years from card [B0008, B0009, B0085]; Board helps claim with clearance/membership proof and passport copy [B0059].
- Malaysian compensation for deaths: Labour Department Form H sent to heirs, returned via the HC [B0120]; labour wings recover dues and the Board pays heirs; legal cell helps with power of attorney [B0136, B0076].
- Injured/sick: Board helps bring home and admit to hospital [B0073]; treatment aid up to Tk 2 lakh, apply within 1 year of return, WhatsApp video check [B0071, B0072] (conflict with Tk 1.5 lakh in 2024 report [B0129]); free airport ambulance [B0070] (2024 report says "low-cost" [B0130]).
- Help bringing a living worker home: relative applies to the ministry with workplace/residence address, agency details, employer's mobile; 7 working days [B0052]. Unpaid wages: ministry Welfare-1 branch, 7 working days [B0048]. Harassment of women: aswelfare@, 7 working days [B0049]. Compensation for deaths via ministry [B0050].
- No Welfare Board safe home in Malaysia [B0079, B0135].

### Coming home
- Customs (HSIA, SRO 261/2025): duty-free 100 g gold jewellery, 200 g silver, 200 cigarettes, 2 used + 1 new phone once a year; one gold bar up to 117 g (10 tola) a year at Tk 5,000 per tola, declared; no alcohol for Bangladeshi passport holders; FMJ form over USD 10,000; declaration form on the plane, or within 7 days at the custom house [B0111-B0115]. Customs emergency +88 01319-056591 [B0116].
- Airport desk: help with customs and lost luggage, complaints from cheated returnees, food for returnees from jail/outpass, cash in special cases (war, disaster, layoff), ambulance [B0068, B0069]. Women returning within 1 year after abuse/fraud get Tk 5,000 cash [B0038].
- Wage Earners' Centre, Borua (Lonjonipara, Ward 48, Khilkhet): book at wec.wewb.gov.bd or in person with clearance/membership proof, passport and ticket copies; Tk 100 admission + Tk 200/night; 01310 350555, 01754 715720 [B0080, B0081]. Probashi Lounge at HSIA: free wifi, food up to 30% off, free phone, print/photocopy; show clearance/membership proof [B0082].
- Family protection: apply to the Board directly or via DC office/DEMO; Board writes to local administration (DC/SP) [B0078, B0132].
- Reintegration: PKB rehabilitation loan up to Tk 50 lakh (Tk 3 lakh unsecured), 9%, 10 years, 15 working days, trade licence compulsory [B0021-B0024]. RAISE project (cash incentives, counselling, training) ran July 2021-June 2026 [B0084, B0133]; district welfare centres since July 2023 [B0133]. BRAC Migration Programme: airport help, counselling, livelihood support, legal help [B0122].
- Children: scholarships, 0.5% HSC seat quota, disability allowance [B0131].

### Malaysia as a destination (corridor numbers)
- BMET clearances to Malaysia: 2007 273,201; 2008 131,762; 2009 12,402; 2010 919; 2011 742; 2012 804; 2013 3,853; 2014 5,134; 2015 30,483; 2016 40,126; 2017 99,787; 2018 175,927; 2019 545; 2020 125; 2021 28; 2022 50,090; 2023 351,683; 2024 93,632; 2025 to May 2,486 [B0091]. OEP live: 2023 350,633; 2024 93,562; 2025 3,451; 2026 to 6 Nov 1,592 [B0086-B0089] (small conflicts with the BMET table). Malaysia = 9.30% of all departures 2004-May 2025; Saudi 34.49% [B0091, B0092]. The collapses 2009-2014 and 2019-2021 and the near-stop since mid-2024 are visible in the numbers; official reasons not yet sourced (blocked).
- Remittances from Malaysia: USD 1,021.85m (FY22), 1,125.86m (FY23) [B0094]; USD 666.73m in Jul-Sep 2026, 5th-largest source [B0093].
- Foreign workers in Malaysia by nationality: only news (803,332 active Bangladeshi PLKS holders at 30 Jun 2025, 37%); official reply blocked.
- New government route: BOESL zero-cost notice of 5 Nov 2026: 112 jobs at DHL Supply Chain (Malaysia), RM 1,700 basic, ages 22-35, apply on OEP only on 8 Nov 2026 10:00-16:00, lottery of 300 then employer test; no cost to the worker [B0141-B0143] (hand to Part A).
- Practical (leads): ministry-fixed cost Tk 78,990 (TBS 2022) [B0123 lead]; workers report paying up to Tk 6 lakh (Migrant Welfare Network, June 2026) [B0124 unofficial-practice].

## Draft steps

**Step 1 (Before paying), additions for Malaysia:**
1. Register on OEP: open employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp in Chrome, type your mobile number and the code sent to it, enter everything exactly as on your NID, pay by bKash or Nagad, and download the registration card [B0139]. Hotline 09614445271.
2. If you need a trade, enrol at a TTC through OEP: Training, Trade/Skills, choose division, district and centre, Search, Apply, Process to Enroll, Confirm [B0140, B0037]. BMET tells Malaysia-bound workers to take the CIDB skill test, now at Khulna TTC [B0001].
3. Pay the agency only into its bank account and keep receipts [B0003].
4. Check the visa at BMET (Kakrail) or your district manpower office: bring the visa and the passport page with the visa stamp; free, 3 working days. BMET's online check doesn't cover Malaysia [B0032, B0033, B0005].
5. Borrow from PKB if needed (terms above) [B0013-B0020].
6. Complain if cheated: BMET DG (30 working days) or ministry enforcement branch (3-5 days to start, 30 to settle); GRS ladder [B0034, B0044, B0041, B0053].

**Police clearance step:** register on pcc.police.gov.bd, Apply, fill details and the address where you live (it must be in your passport), upload the attested passport page, pay the fee shown (portal: Tk 1,500 challan or card; police.gov.bd guidance: Tk 500), track by SMS "PCC S <ref>" to 26969 [B0105-B0109]. **Need to know:** processing time.

**Orientation and clearance step:**
1. Do the 3-day PDO at a TTC; pay the centre's fee against a receipt [B0036].
2. BMET clearance: the agency applies; 3 working days [B0026]. Own visa: apply yourself at BMET Dhaka or DEMO Chattogram with the documents listed [B0029].
3. Fees: welfare fee Tk 5,500 (from 1 Oct 2026), insurance Tk 1,000, smart card/clearance fee Tk 250, income tax Tk 250-400 (single) or Tk 800-1,200 (group) [B0043, B0028, B0030].
4. At the airport the welfare desk checks your card; without one you are stopped [B0038, B0128].

**After arrival:** if you came without clearance, join the Welfare Board at portal.wewb.gov.bd through the labour wing; about Tk 6,500 in ringgit, with life insurance [B0057].

**Sending money home:** choose a BNM-licensed remitter (check BNM's list), compare costs (BB: 3.19% average for small sums; WB: MTOs ~5-5.5%, banks 9-16%), send to a bank or bKash account with the receiver's exact name; family gets the 2.5% incentive; report hundi operators to BNM [B0095-B0104].

**Death/injury/coming home:** as in the notes above.

## Need to know
- OEP registration fee amount (TTC guide and leaflet don't state it) [B0139]. Searched: OEP home, BMET charter, TTC guide.
- Police clearance processing time, and which fee is current (Tk 1,500 vs Tk 500) [B0105, B0109]. Searched pcc portal, police.gov.bd, DMP.
- Whether the Tk 1,000 insurance premium is unchanged after the 1 Oct 2026 welfare-fee rise; total paid at clearance in one pay-order (the circular only covers the welfare fee) [B0043].
- Official Bangladesh Bank circular on the 2.5% incentive [B0104] (blocked).
- Official Malaysian figures for foreign workers by nationality (Bangladesh vs Indonesia, Nepal, India) (blocked parliamentary replies, data.gov.my).
- Official statements of the bans/reopenings (2009, 2018 SPPA suspension, 2024 freeze, 2025-26 reopening) (MOHA pages 404).
- Which Malaysian remitters are cheapest today (World Bank data is Q3 2025) [B0096].
- BRAC Migration Programme phone/walk-in address.
- Whether RAISE services continue after June 2026 [B0084].
- Whether returnees with BMET card get an extra phone allowance (Saudi doc said two new phones with BMET card; HSIA page says 1 new + 2 used for all) [B0113].
- Whether Malaysia requires Bangladeshi documents to be attested by MOFA + Malaysian HC (Malaysia not an Apostille party) [B0110]: Part A/C.

## Conflicts
- Welfare fee: B0043 (Tk 5,500 from 1 Oct 2026, circular) vs B0028 (Tk 3,500, BMET charter page updated 30 Sep 2026). The circular is newer and specific.
- Insurance start date: B0085 (1 Jan 2023, WEWB) vs B0138 (12 Mar 2023, BMET leaflet).
- Treatment aid: B0071 (up to Tk 2 lakh, Oct 2026) vs B0129 (Tk 1.5 lakh, 2024 report).
- Ambulance: B0070 (free) vs B0130 (low-cost).

# Findings, part D: tests and documents (practical pass)

Researched 6 Nov 2026. Ledger ids are in `ledger-parts/D.csv` (D0001-D0066). 30 sources registered; 15 rows official, 51 news/guide. Every news row is practice context, "unofficial, not verified", and is never the basis of a fee, eligibility, document, processing-time or legal rule. Official rows: Bangladesh e-passport portal (DIP, D0049-D0058), CIDB (D0040-D0042), FWCMS (D0019), BMET (D0064).

Key terms (explain inline in the doc): **MiGRAMS** = Bestinet's online registration portal for the Malaysia pre-departure health screening; the "MiGRAMS slip" (written "Migram slip" or "medical slip" in news) is the registration slip an agency makes before the worker can be screened [D0002, D0024]. **FOMEMA** = the company that runs the post-arrival medical for Malaysia's Health Ministry. **CLAB** = Construction Labour Exchange Centre Bhd, CIDB's subsidiary that handles foreign construction workers [D0036, D0042]. **SKKP** = Sijil Kecekapan Kemahiran Pembinaan, CIDB's Construction Skills Competency Certificate [D0038].

## Step 2: Medical check in Bangladesh (Bio-Medical)

**Current round (Oct-Nov 2026), all from news reports of Ministry statements (no official text found online):**
- Medicals for Malaysia started 31 Oct 2026 at approved centres; any medical done before 31 Oct is not accepted and not processed for clearance [D0007, D0013].
- The ministry said its own list of approved centres would be on its website and could change with compliance and capacity [D0007]. **Not found on probashi.gov.bd** on 6 Nov 2026 (notice search for "মেডিকেল" returns only 2022-2023 notices). Jugantor (6 Nov) reports FWCMS now shows 51 accredited Bangladeshi centres with addresses [D0014], consistent with official W0009 (51 on fwcms.com.my/affiliates).
- The 15 Oct release (Jugantor): Malaysia's existing list was used after removing "controversial" centres; Bangladeshi authorities will regularly check the listed centres and close any with irregularities; new centres added in phases [D0020; same release as W0013].
- **Fee:** Tk 8,000 per worker, no more; no fee for the MiGRAMS slip; don't use brokers [D0001, D0002, D0006]. Jugantor says the notice was posted on the Ministry's Facebook page [D0004]. Doc must write this as "Need to know ... reported unofficially" (fee = exact fact; no official source).
- **Choice of centre and switching agency:** no centre works under a particular agency; a selected worker may test at any approved centre; if the worker moves to another agency, the agency that made the slip must send the report without delay, no excuse accepted [D0002, D0003, D0005].
- **Complaints:** email secretary@probashi.gov.bd with the date, place, names of people/organisations and evidence (overcharging, harassment, delay, unauthorised medicals) [D0003, D0008, D0012]. Penalties: centre closure, suspension of the agency's recruitment, cancellation of approval [D0011].
- **Order of steps (conflict):** the 28 Oct release says agencies applying for BMET clearance send the documents and the VDR papers to the approved centre for the test [D0009], which implies the medical comes after VDR approval. Official FWCMS pages put the Bio-Medical before the VDR (W0009, W0058). D0009 marked conflict. Need to know: the actual order in 2026.
- **Fake/unauthorised medicals:** brokers and agencies were collecting passports and money promising medicals before approval; workers told not to hand over passport or money without confirming official approval [D0008]. On 1 Oct 2026 a mobile court sealed Human Diagnostic and Medical Center (Box Culvert Road, Paltan), which had tested and issued reports to 14 Malaysia-bound people without government authorisation and used two signboards [D0017, D0018]. **The same centre is on the FWCMS accredited list** (102/1/A New Paltan) [D0019, D0015]: FWCMS listing alone did not mean Bangladesh authorisation. Doc implication: check the centre is on the Ministry's list (when published), not only FWCMS.
- **What happened last round (practice):** BAIRA says the fixed fee was Tk 6,500 but agencies took Tk 11,000; at least 5 times as many people did the medical as actually went [D0021, D0022] (TBS, Sep 2025). Gulf-centre practice of "unfit first, then bribe for fit" [D0023] (not Malaysia-specific; context only).
- **MiGRAMS (background, 2023):** the worker must be registered on MiGRAMS (RM100, paid by the agency via a top-up e-wallet) before the screening; the health report is needed before the calling visa; re-screening meant paying RM100 again [D0024, D0025]. Whether the RM100 still applies in 2026 is unknown (the 2026 notice only says the slip is free to the worker, D0002).
- **Agencies' pressure:** on 5 Nov 2026 agencies asked the minister to speed medical approvals and set a "reasonable" BOESL processing fee [D0016].

## Step 3: VDR / calling visa, visa sticker, and fake visas

- Real route is official (W0045, W0061-W0065). Practice:
- **Fake visas:** a Narsingdi man promised Italy (Tk 21.98 lakh) was sent in June 2025 with fake Malaysian and French visas pasted in his passport; Malaysian airport immigration detected them and sent him back within 4 days [D0048] (PBI case, Oct 2026). A Bangladeshi couple in KL sold forged European visas (up to RM20,000) to clients in Bangladesh, with fake immigration stamps and forged immigration slips [D0043, D0044].
- **How to check:** official checks already in W0065 (MyIMMs status), W0066 (ePass). No news source gives a worker-facing way to check a VDR sticker. Need to know stays.

## Step 4: Police clearance, passport, certificates

- **Police clearance:** no Malaysia-specific practice found (searched English and Bangla news, guides). Only generic visa-guide sites list a PCC (not used). Need to know remains: whether Malaysia requires a PCC for PLKS workers.
- **Passport (official, DIP, verified):** apply online; no attestation of papers or photo [D0053]. Fill it per NID or online BRC (English): under 18 BRC; 18-20 NID or BRC; over 20 NID compulsory (BRC only at a mission abroad) [D0054]. Apply at the divisional/regional passport office for your present address, or a mission abroad [D0055]; bring original NID/BRC and any technical certificates [D0056]. Fees inside Bangladesh, 48 pages incl. VAT: 5 years Tk 4,025 / 6,325 / 8,625 (regular / express / super express); 10 years Tk 5,750 / 8,050 / 10,350 [D0049]. Pay online (A-Challan, DGePay, ShurjoPay: cards, bKash, Nagad, Rocket) or at any bank via A-Challan [D0051]. Delivery 15 / 7 / 2 working days from biometric enrolment [D0050]. Lost passport: GD at the nearest police station, then re-apply with old passport photocopy and GD [D0057]. At missions, workers and students pay USD 30/45 (5 yrs) or USD 50/75 (10 yrs) for 48 pages [D0052]. Under-18s get only a 5-year, 48-page passport [D0058].

## Age (doctoring)

- IMI age 18-45 (W0073); job-order ranges 18-35, 22-35 etc. (W0029, W0003). The passport's birth date comes from the NID/BRC (D0054), so age is set by the NID/BRC.
- Practice: Perak Labour Department rescued four Bangladeshi boys aged 14-16 who had worked in a factory for over a year; employer detained under ATIPSOM Act s.14 for employing underage workers [D0065, D0066] (Malay Mail, 2024). No source found on how they entered or on age-doctoring for the Malaysia corridor.
- **Need to know:** any official Bangladeshi or Malaysian warning on falsified ages/NIDs for Malaysia (searched Bangla "বয়স জালিয়াতি", "জন্মনিবন্ধন জালিয়াতি", English "fake age passport Malaysia"; nothing Malaysia-specific).

## Step 6: BMET registration, clearance and smart card

- The Tk 250 smart card fee and Tk 250 modification fee were abolished (Ministry letter 1 Dec 2024, as reported); QR-code exit clearance is free and online [D0059, D0060]. Doc: "Need to know: official confirmation" or cite B-part official source if one exists.
- Fingerprints: BMET (Apr 2023, official) let Ami Probashi supply biometric fingerprint devices to all TTCs, IMTs and DEMOs for digital clearance [D0064]. A 2026 travel guide says fingerprinting is done in person there, not online; status/card download by passport number; no card = no exit at the airport [D0061, D0062, D0063] (consistent with official B0038).

## Step 7: After arrival: FOMEMA

- Official: within 30 days at a FOMEMA clinic; unfit = sent home (L0042, L0043, W0047). Renewal: fit result for 2nd and 3rd year (L0049).
- **Frequency conflict:** employer groups say since 16 Dec 2023 FOMEMA requires a yearly screening [D0026, conflict with L0049].
- **Fee (unofficial):** RM207 men / RM217 women since Dec 2023 (news 2024-2025) [D0026, D0030]; an employer guide gives RM191.40 / RM201.40 [D0032]. Refund request RM27 [D0031]. FOMEMA's site is blocked (other parts' blocked lists). Need to know: official fee.
- **Who pays:** employer groups describe the fee as an employer cost (about RM414 million a year) [D0029, D0030]; an employer guide says the employer must pay [D0032]. No source found on deductions from Bangladeshi workers' wages for FOMEMA.
- **Tests added Dec 2023:** filariasis, hepatitis C, methamphetamine on top of HIV, hepatitis B, syphilis, pregnancy, opiates, cannabis [D0030]. FOMEMA rejects workers with diabetes, hypertension or high cholesterol (employer group's complaint) [D0027].
- **Results:** FOMEMA gives no copies of reports to workers or employers [D0028, D0029]; employer sees status on the FOMEMA portal in 5-7 working days [D0032]. Worker brings original passport and the printed FOMEMA form [D0033]. Unfit = employer must repatriate [D0034].
- **Appeal:** no source found (searched "Fomema appeal", "rayuan Fomema", Bangla "ফোমেমা আনফিট"). Need to know.
- **Fraud tried by migrants:** a syndicate sold 200-300 altered Bangladeshi passports a month to Bangladeshis who had failed FOMEMA so they could re-sit it; 22 seized (Feb 2026) [D0035].

## CIDB skill test / construction

- Official BMET notice (B0001, possibly outdated): CIDB test at Khulna TTC, to expand. No 2026 Bangladesh-side CIDB test news found.
- Practice 2025-26 (CLAB, NST Feb 2026): of 3,000 left-behind construction workers interviewed, about 1,000 rejected (health, suitability); the 2,000 passed got 12 days' basic construction training, then a Bangladesh government certificate; after arrival CLAB trains them for CIDB's SKKP [D0036-D0039].
- Official CIDB: LKK skills training open to local and foreign construction workers aged 18-55 at Akademi Binaan Malaysia [D0040, D0041]; foreign construction worker applications go through CLAB [D0042].

## Scams with documents (both ways) and What can get you jailed

- Fake e-PLKS copies sold for RM150 and fake police reports for RM70 in KL (27 Bangladeshis held, Sep 2026) [D0045].
- "A-to-B" quota fraud: quota approved with fake company papers; workers brought on one employer's PLKS and supplied to other firms; 265 Bangladeshi passports seized (Oct 2026) [D0046, D0047]. Doc implication: a worker who ends up with a different employer is working illegally (link to L-part rules).
- Forged FOMEMA passports [D0035]; fake visas sold in Bangladesh [D0043, D0048].

## Draft steps (for the doc writer)

**Step 2 (medical in Bangladesh):**
1. Wait until your agency (or BOESL) has selected you and made your MiGRAMS ("medical") slip; the slip is free (unofficial, not verified) [D0002].
2. Go only to a centre on the Ministry's approved list (not yet found online: Need to know) and on fwcms.com.my/affiliates (W0009); being on FWCMS alone isn't enough: a listed centre was sealed for unauthorised Malaysia tests in Oct 2026 [D0017, D0019]. Medicals done before 31 Oct 2026 don't count [D0007].
3. Pay no more than Tk 8,000 (reported unofficially; Need to know the official fee) [D0001]; you may choose any approved centre and can move your report to another agency [D0002, D0003].
4. Don't hand your passport or money to anyone offering a medical without checking they are approved [D0008]. Report overcharging or unauthorised medicals to secretary@probashi.gov.bd with date, place, names and proof [D0003, D0012].
5. Need to know: how a worker sees their own result; whether the medical now comes before or after the VDR [D0009 vs W0009].

**Step 4 (passport):** apply online at epassport.gov.bd per your NID (over 20), pay Tk 4,025 (5 years) or Tk 5,750 (10 years) for 48 pages regular delivery online or at a bank via A-Challan, attend biometrics at the passport office for your present address with original NID, collect in 15 working days [D0049-D0056].

**Step 6:** BMET smart card is free (reported; need official confirmation) [D0059]; fingerprints at a TTC/IMT/DEMO [D0064, D0061].

**Step 7 (FOMEMA):** employer takes you to the clinic within 30 days (official L0042); bring original passport and printed FOMEMA form [D0033]; employer pays (unofficial) [D0032]; no copy of the report is given [D0028]; unfit = sent home [L0043, D0034].

## Need to know (part D)

- Official Tk 8,000 notice text and the Ministry's 2026 approved-centre list (searched probashi.gov.bd news and notices, Bangla and English; only news reports and a Facebook mention found).
- What the MiGRAMS slip is officially and whether the RM100 registration still applies (only 2023 news, D0024).
- Order of medical vs VDR in 2026 (D0009 vs W0009).
- How a worker checks their own Bio-Medical or FOMEMA result; FOMEMA appeal process (FOMEMA site blocked).
- Official FOMEMA fee and current renewal frequency (D0026 vs L0049).
- Whether Malaysia requires a PCC for PLKS workers.
- Any official warning on age falsification for Malaysia.
- 2026 CIDB/skill test arrangement in Bangladesh (only B0001, possibly outdated, and CLAB news D0036-D0039).

## Conflicts

- D0009 (news: VDR papers go to the medical centre) vs W0009/W0058 (official FWCMS: Bio-Medical before VDR).
- D0026 (news: yearly FOMEMA since Dec 2023) vs L0049 (official IMI: fit result for 2nd and 3rd year renewals only).
- Unofficial fee figures disagree among themselves: RM207/217 [D0026, D0030] vs RM191.40/201.40 [D0032].
- D0015/D0017 vs D0019: centre sealed for unauthorised Malaysia tests is on the official FWCMS accredited list (not a conflict of rules; a caution for the doc).
