# Findings, part E: professional and long-stay passes, family passes

Researched 6 Nov 2026. Ledger rows E0001-E0149 (ledger-parts/E.csv). Sources: esd.imi.gov.my (ESD/MYXpats), imi.gov.my, mm2h.gov.my and motac.gov.my (MOTAC), mdec.my (MDEC), talentcorp.com.my, the JTKSM ePPAx FAQ. Most IMI and ESD web pages carry no date, so their rows are `possibly-outdated`. The ESD FAQ pages print "Latest Update" with today's date, which the server generates, so I treated them as undated. Dated, current sources are the Revised Expatriate Salary Booklet (PDF dated 7 Aug 2026), its FAQ (12 Feb 2026), the PVP-Expert FAQ (Sept 2026), mm2h.gov.my ("Last Update 10/02/2026"), DE Rantau FAQ v11 (1 Nov 2026) and the Enhanced DE Rantau FAQ (Oct 2026).

Context for the doc: none of these passes is a route for an ordinary Bangladeshi worker. PLKS (factory, construction, plantation) workers **cannot bring family and cannot marry in Malaysia** [E0138, E0139, E0140]. The passes below are for graduates and professionals earning RM5,000+ a month, for wealthy retirees or investors, or for remote workers earning USD36,000+ a year from abroad. Bangladeshis are not excluded from any of them: MM2H is open to any country with diplomatic relations with Malaysia [E0100], and DE Rantau to every country except Israel [E0084]. A Bangladeshi needs a Visa with Reference (VDR), and the visa costs RM20 a year [E0045, E0046, E0047, E0058].

## 1. Employment Pass (EP)

**What:** a work pass for an "expatriate", the Malaysian term for a foreign professional or skilled worker, valid for one employer [E0004, E0005, E0006]. Issued for 1-60 months, multiple entry [E0015]. Valid only in Peninsular Malaysia; Sabah and Sarawak have their own immigration and ESD units [E0005; see IMI charter E0056 for Sabah/Sarawak units].

**Who decides:** the Expatriate Committee (EC), or a sector approving agency: MIDA (manufacturing), MDEC (ICT/MSC), Bank Negara (banking), Securities Commission, the Public Services Department, and the ECERDC and IRDA regions [E0004, E0007]. Paying the fee doesn't guarantee approval [E0049].

**Categories from 1 June 2026** (basic salary only, taxable in Malaysia; allowances, bonuses and pay outside Malaysia don't count) [E0008, E0013, E0017]:
- Cat I: RM20,000+ a month, up to 10 years, dependants allowed [E0009].
- Cat II: RM10,000-19,999, up to 10 years (succession plan required from 1 Jan 2027), dependants allowed [E0010].
- Cat III: RM5,000-9,999, up to 5 years with the same employer (succession plan from 1 Jan 2027), dependants now allowed [E0011]. In manufacturing and manufacturing-related services under MIDA the range is RM7,000-9,999 [E0018]. Before June 2026 Cat III was RM3,000-4,999, 12 months per application and no dependants [E0012]. Cat III holders issued before 1 June 2026 still can't bring dependants [E0019].
- Minimum qualification: degree + 3 years' relevant experience, or diploma + 5 years, or technical certificate + 7 years [E0022, E0023] (guidebook dated April 2025).
- The employer must be registered with ESD. Paid-up capital: RM250,000 if 100% local, RM350,000 for a joint venture, RM500,000 if 100% foreign, RM1m for foreign-owned trade companies [E0024].

**Who applies:** only the employer, never the worker [E0002, E0001]. The process from the booklet [E0016]: (1) company registers on ESD Online, about 5 working days; (2) JTKSM (Department of Labour) prior approval under Section 60K of the Employment Act, plus a MYFutureJobs (PERKESO) job advert acknowledgement, both on Xpats Gateway, about 10 working days [E0050, E0051, E0052, E0053]; (3) support letter from the approving agency if the sector is regulated; (4) EP application on ESD Online, 2 working days fast track or 5 normal [E0016, E0054, E0055]; (5) VDR for visa-required nationals [E0058]; (6) entry and ePASS endorsement, 1 working day, at the Expatriate Satellite Centre (ESC) at KLIA T1/T2 with no appointment, or at the MYXpats Centre [E0059]. An employer that hires without JTKSM approval faces a fine of up to RM100,000 and/or 5 years in jail [E0052].

**Documents:** colour scan of the full passport (more than 12 months' validity, 6 empty pages), photo, attested certificates, contract and job description; for renewals, the last 3 months' payslips, tax forms and receipts [E0025, E0026].

**Fees:** MYXpats application fee RM2,000 + 8% SST = RM2,160 [E0040]. Immigration endorsement: RM200 a year (RM300 for a MIDA key post) + RM125 processing + visa (Bangladesh RM20 a year) [E0042, E0045]. For a 12-month EP for a Bangladeshi that is RM200 + RM125 + RM20 = RM345 immigration fees plus the RM2,160 application fee (my arithmetic from E0040/E0042/E0045; the doc should show the parts, not a total). A rejected application gets 75% of the processing fee back unless the employer appeals [E0048].

**Appeal:** the employer appeals within 14 days in ESD Online; the EC-Appeal decides within up to 30 working days; after that the case can be referred to MOHA [E0063, E0064, E0021].

**Renewal:** can be filed 3 months before expiry, under the revised salary rules [E0020]. A new passport requires a Transfer of Endorsement, or the holder can be stopped from leaving Malaysia [E0065].

**Check it is real:** the ePASS has a QR code; anyone can check it at imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan, or email esdhelpdesk@imi.gov.my (1 working day) [E0060, E0061, E0062]. MYXpats helpdesk: +603-7839 7171, helpdesk@myxpats.com.my, Level 4 Surian Tower, Mutiara Damansara [E0066].

## 2. Dependant Pass and LTSVP for EP holders' families

- From 1 June 2026: the **Dependant Pass (DP)** covers a spouse and children under 18 (step and adopted included; disabled children of any age). The **LTSVP** (Long-Term Social Visit Pass) covers parents, parents-in-law, children aged 18-25 and a common-law spouse [E0014, E0027].
- The company applies through its ESD account, together with the EP or later [E0034, E0037]. Fees: RM540 application (RM500 + SST) [E0041], plus RM90 a year, RM50 processing and the visa fee [E0043]. The LTSVP needs a personal bond: RM750 for Bangladeshis [E0144]. Charter time 14 working days [E0029].
- Documents: photo, colour passport (12+ months), and a marriage or birth certificate translated into English and certified (CTC) by the Malaysian High Commission in Dhaka or the Bangladesh High Commission in KL, with a current-year CTC for new applications [E0030]. A common-law spouse needs an affidavit from the embassy; children 18-25 need a statutory declaration and embassy confirmation at every renewal [E0031].
- Validity follows the principal's pass, capped by the dependant's passport [E0034]. **Conflict** on the LTSVP maximum: 12 months [E0148] vs following the principal [E0147].
- **Can dependants work?** **Conflict:** the ESD guidebook and DP page say no, the dependant must get their own EP [E0028, E0035, E0145]; the older IMI FAQ (2021) says a DP spouse can work with an Immigration work endorsement [E0038]. LTSVP holders can't work or study (they need an EP or a Student Pass) [E0032].
- Domestic maid: EP holder earning RM5,000+ (1 maid), RM10,000+ (2), RM15,000+ (3) [E0033].

## 3. Can PLKS workers bring family?

No. "Pekerja Asing tidak dibenarkan membawa keluarga masuk dan tinggal di negara ini" (foreign workers are not allowed to bring their family to live in Malaysia) [E0138, E0140]. They also can't marry a local or another foreign worker while on PLKS [E0139]. A Bangladeshi worker's only official family route is a family member's own pass (a visit visa, which is another part's topic) or the worker moving to a professional pass after qualifying.

## 4. LTSVP for a foreign spouse of a Malaysian citizen

- Minimum 6 months, up to 5 years. The holder may work or run a business without an EP or PLKS once Immigration endorses permission in the passport [E0122, E0123, E0125]. The marriage must be registered under Malaysian law [E0129]. Immigration may visit the home or workplace to check (naziran) [E0124].
- New application: both spouses attend the state Immigration office with Imm.12, Imm.38 (2 copies), Imm.55 (2 copies), a sponsor statement, a security bond stamped RM10, a sworn declaration with photo, the spouse's IC and birth certificate, the marriage registration, a wedding photo, proof of income and address, and the spouse's Malaysian passport [E0126, E0127]. Charter: 25 working days [E0057].
- Work endorsement: at the Visa, Pass and Permit Division in the state of work, with the spouse present. Bring the spouse's MyKad copy, passport and pass, a sworn declaration, the marriage certificate, an offer letter, a contract stamped RM10 (stating position, period and salary), the information form and the SSM company printout [E0125].
- A false statement is an offence under s.56(1)(f): fine up to RM10,000 and/or 5 years in jail [E0128].
- After 3 years on a long-term pass, family of citizens can get the **Pas Residen** (Residence Pass): RM500 for 5 years; holders may work, study and run a business [E0131, E0132, E0133].

## 5. MM2H (Malaysia My Second Home), MOTAC

- Open to any country with diplomatic relations with Malaysia, which includes Bangladesh. Age 25+ (Silver/Gold/Platinum) or 21+ (SEZ/SFZ) [E0100].
- **Apply only through a MOTAC-licensed MM2H operator** [E0101]. Final approval and appeals rest with MOHA/Immigration [E0102]. The licensed list, with licence numbers (MM2Hxxx) and validity dates, is at mm2h.gov.my/agencies [E0116]. Since 1 Aug 2025 applications go through the system at mm2h.gov.my/portal (helpdesk 03-8891 7207), and from 1 Oct 2025 by online appointment [E0118].
- Tiers [E0106-E0109, E0114]:

| | Fixed deposit | Pass | Min. property | Participating fee | Renewal fee |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Platinum | USD 1,000,000 | 20 yrs | RM2,000,000 | RM200,000 | RM5,000 |
| Gold | USD 500,000 | 15 yrs | RM1,000,000 | RM3,000 | RM3,000 |
| Silver | USD 150,000 | 5 yrs | RM600,000 | RM1,000 | RM1,500 |
| SEZ/SFZ | USD 65,000 (21-49) / USD 32,000 (50+) | 10 yrs | Forest City, Johor only | RM1,000 | RM300 |

  Processing fee: RM5,000 per principal and RM2,500 per dependant [E0107]. Under 50s must spend 90 days a year in Malaysia [E0103]. The property must be bought within 1 year of endorsement and not sold for 10 years [E0119, E0103]. Up to 50% of the fixed deposit can be withdrawn for a house, education, medical or tourism costs [E0106]. Police may interview applicants at random [E0120].
- **Work:** Platinum only; Gold, Silver and SEZ can't work or run a business [E0112, E0113].
- Family: spouse, children under 21 (21-34 if single and unemployed), disabled children, parents and parents-in-law; maid for Platinum only [E0104]. **Conflict:** maximum child age 34 [E0110, E0146] vs 35 [E0111].
- Renewal: RM500 a year pass fee + RM0-50 visa [E0105].
- Sarawak runs its own S-MM2H. **Lead only:** from 2025, age 30+, RM500,000 fixed deposit in a Sarawak bank, 30 days a year in Sarawak [E0143]. The official MTCP pages were blocked.

## 6. DE Rantau Nomad Pass (MDEC)

- A type of Professional Visit Pass for remote workers and freelancers with foreign employers or clients. Open to all nationalities except Israel; age 18+ [E0084].
- Income: tech roles more than USD36,000 a year (raised from USD24,000 for applications from 1 Dec 2026), non-tech USD60,000 [E0085, E0098]. The contract must have started at least 3 months before applying; up to 3 contracts [E0085].
- Duration: 3-12 months, then up to three 12-month renewals, 48 months maximum. No route to PR [E0086, E0098].
- Apply at mdec.my/derantau. Processing 6-8 weeks; the e-Pass comes within 1 week of endorsement; check status at malaysiadigital.mdec.my [E0089, E0090]. Bangladeshis then get a VDR or eVISA and enter for endorsement [E0089].
- Fees: RM1,080 for the main applicant and RM540 per dependant (non-refundable), plus the RM360 a year pass fee and visa. Refundable bond for Bangladeshis: RM750 [E0091, E0092].
- Family: spouse or common-law partner, children under 18, special-needs children, parents [E0087]. **The spouse can't work** [E0088].
- No appeal [E0093]. Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan only; a tourist pass can't be converted [E0095]. Passport needs 14+ months' validity [E0096]. Documents include a letter of good conduct from the home country [E0097].
- **New applications are closed 1-30 Nov 2026 and reopen on 1 Dec 2026** [E0099].
- "Agents" can't guarantee approval [E0094].

## 7. Other passes

- **Professional Visit Pass (PVP-Expert):** short assignments of up to 12 months for an overseas company; no Dependant Pass [E0067]. Not for general workers or operators; the applicant waits abroad; false documents lead to blacklisting [E0068, E0070]. A Social Visit Pass does not allow work [E0069].
- **PLS@Xpats:** permission to work on a Social Visit Pass, for emergency or critical professional work, applied for by the company at mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats [E0071, E0072]; charter 3 working days [E0056]. **Need to know:** the duration (news says 30 days), sectors and fees; the FAQ page is empty (see blocked).
- **Investor Pass:** 6 months + 6, for foreign investors working through MIDA and other investment agencies, applied from abroad on Xpats Gateway with a business email. No dependants. RM1,296 + RM90 + RM500 + visa [E0075, E0076, E0077, E0073].
- **Residence Pass-Talent (RP-T, TalentCorp):** 10 years. Requires 3 consecutive years in Malaysia on an EP, RM15,000 basic salary, 2 years of tax paid, a qualification and 5 years' experience. The holder can change employer; the spouse can work [E0078, E0079, E0080].
- **MTEP (MDEC):** for tech founders. 1-year PVP (fee from RM2,700) or 5-year Resident Pass (from RM5,400, dependants allowed). Needs RM50,000 in bank statements, a good-conduct certificate and a sponsor [E0081, E0082, E0083].
- **PVIP (Premium Visa Programme):** listed on the IMI home page [E0074]. **Need to know:** official terms. No public official page was found; news reports a RM200,000 fee, RM40,000 a month offshore income, 20 years and appointed agents only.

## 8. Permanent residence

The Entry Permit (Permit Masuk) gives indefinite residence. Its categories are the wife, husband or child under 18 of a citizen, and "Warga Asing Sepenuhnya" (fully foreign nationals) [E0134]. It can be revoked [E0135]. Expatriates approved for PR get an LTSVP while waiting [E0137]. Status checks and a points calculator are at imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices and app.imi.gov.my/epc [E0136]. DE Rantau gives no PR route [E0098]. PLKS rules (no family, no marriage, 5 years maximum) mean an ordinary worker can't build up the qualifying stay [E0138, E0139]. **Need to know:** the official criteria for "fully foreign" PR (the e-book is on Google Drive; see blocked).

## 9. Scams and checks

- EP/DP: the worker never applies; any "EP for sale" offer to an individual is outside the official process, because only a registered employer can apply [E0002]. Verify any ePASS by its QR code or at imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan [E0060]. PVP-Expert misuse and false information lead to blacklisting [E0070].
- MM2H: only MOTAC-licensed operators may submit; check the name and licence number at mm2h.gov.my/agencies [E0101, E0116]. Lead: MOTAC re-vetted all agents in 2024 after complaints [E0142].
- DE Rantau: no agent can guarantee approval [E0094].
- Practical lead: 3,604 Bangladeshis held active MM2H passes on 31 Jan 2024, 5th-largest nationality [E0141]. This needs the parlimen.gov.my answer (11 March 2024).

---

## Draft steps

### Employment Pass (for a Bangladeshi graduate or skilled professional)
1. **Check you qualify:** a degree + 3 years' experience, diploma + 5 years or technical certificate + 7 years, and a job offer of at least RM5,000 basic salary a month (RM7,000 in manufacturing) [E0022, E0011, E0018].
2. **Get a written offer from a registered Malaysian company.** It, not you, applies [E0002]. It must first be registered on ESD (paid-up capital RM250,000-1m) [E0024] and get JTKSM Section 60K approval and a MYFutureJobs advert acknowledgement on Xpats Gateway (about 10 working days) [E0050, E0051, E0016].
3. **Send the company your documents:** a colour scan of every passport page (12+ months valid), a photo, attested certificates and the signed contract [E0025].
4. **The company applies on ESD Online and pays RM2,160.** Decision in 2-5 working days; the approval letter is printed from the system [E0040, E0016, E0054].
5. **Get your Visa with Reference (VDR).** The company applies with the EP and collects it at MYXpats [E0058].
6. **Fly in and get the ePASS endorsed** at the ESC at KLIA T1/T2 (no appointment) or MYXpats. The company pays the immigration fees: RM200 a year + RM125 + RM20 visa [E0059, E0042, E0045].
7. **Check the ePASS** by its QR code or at imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan [E0060].
- If refused: only the company can appeal, within 14 days (decision in up to 30 working days) [E0063].
- Changing employer means a new application [E0006].
- Bringing family: see below.

### Bringing your family (EP holder)
1. Get your marriage and children's birth certificates translated into English and certified by the Malaysian High Commission in Dhaka (or the Bangladesh High Commission in KL), dated the current year [E0030].
2. Your company applies on ESD Online for a DP (spouse, children under 18) or an LTSVP (parents, in-laws, children 18-25, common-law spouse) and pays RM540 per person [E0014, E0041]. For an LTSVP, you sign a personal bond (RM750 for Bangladeshis) [E0144, E0031].
3. Decision in about 14 working days; then endorsement at RM90 a year + RM50 + visa [E0029, E0043].
4. Your family can't work on a DP or LTSVP unless they get their own EP (ESD); IMI's older FAQ mentions a work endorsement for a DP spouse (conflict) [E0028, E0038].

### PLKS workers
- You can't bring your family or marry in Malaysia while on PLKS [E0138, E0139].

### Married to a Malaysian
1. Register the marriage under Malaysian law [E0129].
2. Both go to the state Immigration office with the forms and documents in E0126; LTSVP of 6 months to 5 years; 25 working days [E0122, E0123, E0057].
3. To work, apply for the work endorsement with the offer letter and RM10-stamped contract [E0125].
4. After 3 years, you can apply for a Pas Residen (RM500 for 5 years) [E0131, E0133].

### MM2H
1. Pick a licensed operator from mm2h.gov.my/agencies and check its licence number and validity [E0116, E0101].
2. Have the fixed deposit (Silver USD150,000 and up) and money for the property (Silver RM600,000 and up) [E0106, E0108].
3. Pay a RM5,000 processing fee plus the participating fee; police may interview you; medical check at a MOTAC panel clinic [E0107, E0109, E0120].
4. Buy the home within 1 year; stay 90 days a year if under 50; only Platinum may work [E0119, E0103, E0112].

### DE Rantau
1. Check you earn USD36,000+ (tech) or USD60,000+ (non-tech) a year from foreign employers or clients, with contracts that started 3+ months ago [E0085].
2. Apply at mdec.my/derantau (closed 1-30 Nov 2026, reopens 1 Dec 2026); pay RM1,080 + RM540 per dependant [E0099, E0091].
3. Wait 6-8 weeks and check malaysiadigital.mdec.my [E0089, E0090].
4. Get a VDR or eVISA, enter, and endorse (RM360 a year pass fee + visa + RM750 refundable bond) [E0089, E0091, E0092].

## Need to know
- MYFutureJobs advert: how long it must run and which positions are exempt (PERKESO page blocked) [E0051].
- Whether a DP spouse can now work with an endorsement (conflict E0038 vs E0145).
- Whether the LTSVP for EP family is capped at 12 months (conflict E0147/E0148).
- PVIP: official fees and eligibility. Only IMI's portal link was found [E0074]. Searched imi.gov.my and the web; news only.
- PLS@Xpats: duration (news: 30 days), sectors and fees. The IMI FAQ page is empty.
- MM2H: whether the RM500 "system processing fee" (Aug 2025) [E0118] is separate from the RM5,000 processing fee on the website [E0107].
- Sarawak and Sabah MM2H: official requirements. The Sarawak MTCP site is blocked; for Sabah, no official page was searched beyond the news.
- PR for "fully foreign" nationals: criteria and points (the IMI e-book is on Google Drive; REP PDF is image-only).
- How many Bangladeshis hold EPs (no official figure found).
- Status check for EP applications: the ESD "Check Application Status" page is a JavaScript form for companies; how an individual checks before the ePASS exists isn't stated.

## Conflicts
- E0038 (IMI FAQ 2021: DP spouse may work with endorsement) vs E0145 (also E0028, E0035) (ESD: no, must convert to EP).
- E0146 (and E0110) (mm2h.gov.my: child up to 34) vs E0111 (MOTAC PDF Jan 2025: 35).
- E0147 (ESD LTSVP page: duration follows principal) vs E0148 (payment table: LTSVP max 12 months).
- E0149 (ESD EP page: parents under Dependant Pass) vs E0014 (2026 booklet: parents get LTSVP).

## Blocked
See blocked-parts/E.md: the PERKESO MYFutureJobs page, the Sarawak MTCP S-MM2H PDF and news, mm2h.sarawak.gov.my, the IMI Permit Masuk e-book (Google Drive), the REP guideline and MOTAC MM2H guide (both image-only), the IMI PLS@Xpats FAQ (empty), the PVIP guidelines (login portal) and the Borneo Post PLS@Xpats news (403).


# Part F: practical pass, life and problems in Malaysia (6 Nov 2026)

Scope: what actually happens to Bangladeshi workers in Malaysia (wages, deductions, unpaid pay, passports, housing, overtime, raids and depots, jobless arrivals, changing employer, absconding, complaints to JTK / WFW / Labour Court / the High Commission, bank accounts, SIM cards, health care, deaths and compensation). News and NGO rows are practice context only ("unofficial, not verified") and never the basis of a fee, eligibility, document, time or legal rule. Official sources found for three gaps: MCMC SIM rules (verified), Maybank account documents (bank's own page, possibly outdated), JTKSM complaint and Labour Court leaflet (official, 2017, possibly outdated).

Ledger: 104 rows F0001-F0104 (official 27: 11 verified, 16 possibly-outdated; news/NGO 77: 73 unofficial, 4 conflict). 13 facts rows.

New raw files: mcmc-prepaid-faq-2026-en, maybank-savings-account-i (Firecrawl), news-jagonews-bdhc-dues-recovery and news-jagonews-85-workers-bdhc-probe (Firecrawl), 30 news/NGO pages, plus a flow-text copy `raw/jtksm-masalah-di-tempat-kerja.md` of the already-saved JTKSM PDF (the -layout .txt interleaves columns, so quotes would not match).

## Work visa (PLKS), Step 7: After arrival

**SIM card (official, verified, MCMC FAQ on Determination No. 1 of 2026, PDF dated 26 Feb 2026).** Every prepaid SIM user must register, any nationality [F0001]. A foreign worker brings the original passport plus the work permit identification document; photocopies, photos or scans are refused [F0002, F0003]. Registration is done in person at a telco service centre or authorised dealer, on a passport reader with facial recognition [F0013]; nobody else may register for you [F0004]. Limit: 2 prepaid SIMs per telco for non-Malaysians [F0005]. Do not use a SIM that is already active before you register [F0006]. Activation status comes within 12 hours [F0007]. Never give your passport photo, ID or OTP to anyone offering to "help" register; each telco has a free check of all SIMs registered in your name [F0009, F0010]. Tourists' SIMs end after 3 months [F0008]. This closes the L part's SIM "Need to know" (its news lead on 2 SIMs and the biometric passport reader is now confirmed officially). Need to know: whether the "work permit identification document" means the i-Kad or the PLKS sticker in the passport (the FAQ doesn't say).

**Bank account (Maybank's own product page, undated, rates effective 1 June 2026, so possibly outdated).** Foreigners open a savings account at a Maybank branch with a passport; a valid work permit, valid visa and an employer confirmation letter may be required [F0011]; you can also open one at any Maybank branch [F0012]. Minimum initial deposit RM250 [F0014]. Online opening in the MAE app needs a MyKad scan, so it is not open to foreign workers [F0015]. Official rule already in doc: wages must go into an account in the worker's name and the High Commission expects the employer to open it [L0132, L0104, L0034]. Need to know: Bank Negara Malaysia's rule on which ID banks must accept from PLKS holders and any basic-account product for foreign workers (searched bnm.gov.my via WebSearch; no page found; BSN, CIMB and RHB pages not found). The employer letter in [F0011] means the worker in practice depends on the employer to open the account.

**Health care.** Official: renewal needs the SPIKPA slip [L0046]. Practice (news): public hospitals take a deposit from foreigners before treatment [F0063]; a worker covered by the government foreign workers' health insurance scheme may be exempt from the deposit [F0066]; the emergency "Lampiran A" form only delays payment, it does not waive it [F0067]; employers must pay SPIKPA in the PLKS application and can have bank accounts cut off if they leave workers' hospital bills unpaid [F0065, F0064]. Need to know: an official MOH page on foreigner deposits and SPIKPA cover (not searched on moh.gov.my in this pass; the L part lists SPIKPA as a gap).

**Passport.** Official rule: the employer may not keep it [L0149]. Practice: passport retention "remains widespread" and only 15 people were convicted for it in the US TIP 2026 period [F0084]; workers at Mentakab (Aug 2026) and Kawaguchi say their passports were kept [F0032, F0045]; Aerofoam workers got passports back after complaining to the authorities [F0042]. UN experts (Nov 2025) list confiscated passports among common abuses [F0086].

## Your rights at work (what happens in practice)

**Real pay, hours and deductions.** Mentakab workers (Aug 2026) say they work up to 12 hours a day for RM65 a day against 8-hour contracts, with no regular weekly day off [F0031]; they say they spent RM16,000-18,000 each to get there [F0035]; the company is said to demand a RM2,500 deposit before annual leave [F0033]. Aerofoam workers report flight-ticket deposits, hostel deductions and no proper payslips [F0039]. The US TIP 2026 says some employers deduct the levy from wages, often without telling the worker [F0082] (official rule: the employer pays the levy [L0068]; deductions capped at 50% only to recover advances [L0137]). These are workers' claims, not rulings.

**Housing.** Official standard: Act 446 (text in raw/lom-act446, other part). Practice: many employers still use non-compliant "hostels" six years after the amendments [F0060]; a PPR flat meant for 5 can hold 8-10 workers [F0061]; some employers rent empty units and leave workers to buy beds and kitchen items [F0062]; about 35 Aerofoam workers lived in shipping containers [F0040].

**Fees and debt.** US TIP 2026: some Bangladeshis paid up to about USD 7,200 before arrival [F0085]; UN experts: some were pressured to sign false declarations that they paid only official fees, some were asked for extra payments or moved to other jobs without consent [F0087, F0088].

## If you aren't paid (complaint routes in practice)

**Official JTK route (JTKSM leaflet "Masalah di tempat kerja", printed Oct 2017, possibly outdated).** Free advice: 03-8000 8000, jtksm@mohr.gov.my, or the nearest labour office [F0093]. A complaint is made in writing to the labour office with your name and address, the employer's name and address, and the issues and documents [F0094]. JTK investigates; if valid it issues a correction notice to the employer, then prosecution or a compound if ignored; you are told the result [F0095]. It covers no contract, late/unpaid wages, below-minimum pay, no payslip, unpaid overtime, illegal deductions, dismissal, excess hours, no rest, no leave [F0096]. A wage claim (Labour Court, s.69) is made in person with a sworn statement before an officer [F0097, F0103]; bring passport, employer's name and address (from a Companies Commission search), payslips, appointment letter and termination letter if any [F0104, F0098]. Steps: statement, mention date, notice to you and summons to the employer, hearing, order [F0100]. The Labour Court is free [F0099]. Appeal to the High Court within 14 days of the decision [F0101]. If the employer ignores the order, JTK can use a judgment debtor summons, garnishment, prosecution and a writ of seizure and sale [F0102]. Note: the leaflet's RM2,000-5,000 wage bands predate the 2022 amendment; check against the Act text (raw/jtksm-employment-act-1955-act265-2023, other part).

**What happens in practice.**
- Esstar Vision, Johor (2026): 85 workers emailed the High Commission on 16 Jan [F0018]; they filed with JTK and a High Commission officer attended the hearing on 28 Jan [F0019]; the High Commission met the company, which promised instalments, and visited the site [F0020]; in mid-March, after media reports, JTK found wages delayed Sep 2025-Feb 2026 and opened 14 case files worth RM636,414.65 [F0016, F0017]; workers asked for a release letter to find other work [F0021].
- Aerofoam (Jul 2026): 109 workers filed JTK claims with legal help; passports returned after complaints; but permits expired while the claims ran, leaving them open to arrest [F0038, F0043, F0042, F0041].
- Mentakab (Aug 2026): workers told the High Commission and JTK; no official had visited [F0034]; a complaint to the Expatriates' Welfare Ministry said some workers with valid permits were sent home against their will and food was stopped [F0036, F0037].
- Kawaguchi (2025): a labour tribunal ordered over RM3 million, but workers had got RM251,000 [F0044]; wages withheld up to 8 months before closure [F0046].
- Mediceram (Nov 2025): about 180 workers who protested visa non-renewal were sacked; 10 were deported after company executives took them to the airport; 152 hired lawyers and asked to be transferred [F0047, F0048, F0049].
- High Commission figures: about 450 complaints since 2022, over 330 resolved by mediation [F0022, F0023]; RM11.42 million recovered on 2,857 claims in 2025 and RM5.89 million on 1,381 claims Jan-Jun 2026, mostly work-injury (PERKESO-type) benefits [F0025, F0026, F0027, F0028]. Its labour minister says many workers do not know their compensation rights [F0029]; community leaders cite language, paperwork and middlemen [F0030]; a worker says many don't know where to go [F0024].
- US TIP 2026: labour courts resolved 28,992 disputes and ordered RM70 million in back wages in the period [F0083].
- Existing rows: agents warn workers not to complain [L0192]; in 2023 the mission took a case to the HR ministry, which summoned the employer [L0193].

Pattern for the doc (stated as reports, not advice): complaints that reached JTK with the High Commission involved led to hearings and case files; payment came slowly or partly; permits can expire during the case; protest can lead to dismissal, deportation or arrest [F0048, F0058].

## If you want to change employer

**Conflict.** Official IMI pages (undated) say PLKS holders may not change employer or sector [L0055, W0052], or only with Home Ministry permission [L0067]. News (May 2025) reports the Home and HR ministries decided to allow cross-sector transfers [F0077]; before that, transfers were allowed within the same sector on restructuring, closure, expansion or a JTK committee's approval [F0078]; the HR minister said guidelines would cover a minimum period, worker consent and five formal sectors [F0080]; the US TIP 2026 says the policy exists but written consent of all parties limits it [F0081]. Status `conflict` on F0077, F0078, F0080, F0081 with notes naming L0055/W0052/L0067. Official process document already in raw: `raw/jtksm-proses-kerja-ptm-pdg` (JTKSM "Pertukaran Majikan" flow: both employers register on ePPAx, the original employer applies and picks the new employer, the new employer uploads documents within 7 days, the branch labour office investigates, JTKSM decides, the new employer finishes at the One Stop Centre). Not logged by me (other part). Need to know: the official 2025 cross-sector transfer guideline (searched news only; jtksm.mohr.gov.my not reached in this pass).

In practice, workers stuck with non-paying employers ask for a "release letter" [F0021] or a transfer [F0049].

## If your employer reports you absent (lari)

Practice: workers who entered legally, were cheated and left the employer have been detained as illegal migrants (171 in Johor, Dec 2023) [F0057 (2023)]. Need to know: an official way to check or challenge a false absconding report (searched English and Bangla news; none found; the Indian High Commission's page shows a mission-side practice of putting a worker's emergency-certificate on hold when an employer reports absconding, saved as raw/hcikl-absconding-workers but not logged, as it concerns Indian workers).

## What can get you jailed: raids and depots

Practice: night raids on worker hostels check papers and detain those without valid documents or permits [F0050] and send them to depots such as Belantik (Kedah) [F0051]; people smuggled in via a third country and Kelantan paid up to RM15,000 and were held at the Putrajaya depot under the anti-smuggling law [F0052, F0053]. Home Minister: 465 depot deaths in 2021-2025 out of 349,856 admissions, Bangladeshis among the smaller groups [F0054]; detainees may contact family by phone, letter or visit with the depot commander's approval [F0055]. Fortify Rights: depots over capacity (22,045 against 21,530), 140 deaths since 2024 [F0056]; a Bangladeshi former detainee says people are afraid to report sick [F0092]. HRW: about 18,000 held in immigration detention [F0089]. Existing official rows on depots: L0180, L0181; release by Travel Permit through the camp: L0017, L0018.

## Jobless arrivals

Existing rows L0190, L0191. New: workers in 2026 cases ask for release letters to find other jobs [F0021]; Bangladesh asked Malaysia (Aug 2026) to register unregistered Bangladeshis; about 800,000 are registered [F0090]. Need to know: an official Malaysian registration window for undocumented Bangladeshis in 2026 (news only; none found).

## Leaving at the end of the job

News (May 2025): employers must be present at exit points to manage foreign workers' departure under the Check-Out Memo [F0079] (a Malay Mail page on this is already in raw as malaymail-employers-escort-workers-out-2025-05, other part).

## If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

Practice: Malaysia had the second-highest number of Bangladeshi worker deaths in 2026 after Saudi Arabia [F0072]. Bodies of documented workers come home in reasonable time; undocumented workers' can take long [F0073]. Families can apply to the Welfare Board even without clearance or membership [F0074] (official: B0051, B0052). Families report repeated visits to the Board [F0075]; causes of death are usually recorded as natural and not verified [F0076]. A Kuala Lumpur case (Aug 2026): a body stayed 15 days in a morgue at RM75 a day because the passport's emergency contact held only "0088" and no family could be traced; if none is found he may be buried in Malaysia as unclaimed; the labour wing searched his past workplaces [F0068, F0069, F0070, F0071]. High Commission recoveries include dependants' benefit, funeral and repatriation costs [F0028]. This supports telling workers to keep a working family phone number in the passport and with the High Commission (the doc should phrase this as what the case shows, not our advice).

## Malaysia as a destination

Jun 2026: the two prime ministers agreed to clean up recruitment and cut middlemen; hiring of Bangladeshis restricted since 2024 [F0091]. Aug 2026: a state minister said Malaysia would take about 200,000 workers over six months, 10,000 at no cost (in raw/news-bss-419282; only F0090 logged).

## Draft steps (for the doc writer)

Step 7 additions:
1. **Get a SIM in your own name.** Go yourself to a telco service centre or authorised dealer with your original passport and work permit ID; the dealer scans your passport and face; no copies; up to 2 prepaid SIMs per telco; you hear within 12 hours; don't accept a SIM that is already active; never hand your passport photo or OTP to a "helper"; check free with your telco which numbers are in your name [F0001-F0010, F0013].
2. **Open your own bank account.** At a bank branch with your passport; Maybank may ask for your work permit, visa and an employer confirmation letter; RM250 opening deposit at Maybank [F0011, F0012, F0014, F0015]; wages must go into your account [L0132, L0104].
3. **Hospital.** Public hospitals take a deposit from foreigners; insured foreign workers may be exempt (unofficial, not verified) [F0063, F0066].

If you aren't paid:
1. Write down hours, keep payslips, contract and appointment/termination letters [F0104].
2. Complain on WFW [L0163] or go to the nearest labour office / call 03-8000 8000; give a sworn statement for a Labour Court claim; it is free; appeal within 14 days; JTK can enforce an ignored order [F0093-F0102].
3. Tell the labour wing at the High Commission (contacts L0021, B0117); in 2026 cases its officers attended JTK hearings and called employers in [F0019, F0020]; it reports about 330 of 450 complaints resolved [F0023].
4. Practice warnings, labelled unofficial: cases take months and payment may be partial [F0016, F0044]; permits can expire meanwhile, so ask who is renewing yours [F0041]; protests have led to dismissal, deportation and arrest [F0047, F0048, F0058].

- Changing employer: the official 2025 cross-sector transfer guideline (conflict with IMI pages).
- Absconding: how to check or challenge a false lari report.
- Undocumented Bangladeshis: any official 2026 registration window.
- Labour Court: time limit for filing and current wage thresholds after the 2022 amendment (leaflet is 2017).

# Findings, part G: intro, visas not open, PLKS intro, Step 1 find a real job

Researched 7 Nov 2026 for the rewrite of malaysia.src.md lines 1-57 (draft: drafts/MY1.md). New ledger rows G0001-G0040; carried-over rows re-checked against their ledger quotes and, where possible, the saved files.

## What changed from the old draft and why

- Intro: dropped "Researched 6 Nov 2026", the reference-list "possibly outdated" explanation and the "unofficial, not verified" label rule (CLAUDE.md: no labels, no research log in the doc). Added one line telling readers (and a later AI agent) that each procedure starts with "You'll need:" (mentor: "AI agents").
- Visas-not-open table: removed "all checked on 6 Nov 2026", the eVOA closure history (C0009), the eNTRI 2017-18 history, and the Border Pass "Need to know" (who can use it doesn't matter to Bangladeshis; IMI's page has no text, C0026). MEV row rewritten: MyVISA FAQ (V0009) says MEV from 1 Sep 2026 is limited to "31 eligible nationalities" for fly & cruise, business, medical and wedding tourism; IMI's image notice says 30 (V0010, conflict); the 31-nationality list on the same FAQ page is the list of nationalities that MUST get an eVISA, which includes Bangladesh. The Daily Observer (V0108, lead) reads it as: Bangladeshi tourists generally get single-entry visas; MEV only for those four purposes. The doc states that reading. Conflict 30 vs 31 left out of the doc (doesn't change what a Bangladeshi does).
- PLKS intro: removed the MoU date conflict (BMET order 19 Dec 2021, W0027/R0010, vs Ministry 2026 releases 18 Dec 2022, Z0152/Z0156; Amar Desh says 18 Dec 2021; Daily Star Bangla says Dec 2021) because it changes nothing a worker does. Removed agency-list counts beyond one line, the 2025 plantation-only/7,873 left-behind history (W0020, W0018, W0024), and the April/June 2026 joint-statement news (A0097, A0104, A0022, A0105). Added "How a job reaches you" from Z0145-Z0149, W0041/W0043, W0045, W0009, A0035.
- Step 1: every imperative now has a numbered SOP with "You'll need:". Old fee line "In June 2022 BMET charged Tk 200" replaced by the current official fee (G0001, G0002).

## OEP registration (Step 1.1)

- Fee Tk 200, official: Sylhet DEMO notice (posted 17 Aug 2025, page updated 15 Dec 2025) "সরকারি ফি- ২০০/-" and BKTTC Dhaka admission notice (19 May 2026) "২০০ টাকা প্রদান করে BMET রেজিস্ট্রেশন" [G0001, G0002]. Third-party guides give Tk 200, Tk 300 or Tk 1,000 (web search 7 Nov); the two official notices agree on Tk 200, so the old "Need to know: current OEP registration fee" is closed.
- Form fields seen live by Firecrawl on 7 Nov 2026 (employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration): English name, mobile, NID number, date of birth, division, district (all "as on NID"), captcha, terms, "যাচাই করুন" [G0035]. Joypurhat TTC flowchart (Jan 2026, read page by page) gives the 14 steps through payment and card download [B0139]; TTC enrolment 13 steps [B0140].
- After registration, bring 2 copies of the slip to the DEMO for fingerprints [G0001]. Employee Card re-download page on oep.gov.bd (BMET number + captcha) [G0036 official page exists; G0038 guide for "no login, BMET number"].
- Profile asks passport, education, skills (guide only) [G0037]. Not seen officially because the profile needs a login.
- Wego guide (Aug 2026) says clearance moved to oc.bmet.gov.bd; BDGovtGuide (1 Nov 2026) says oc.bmet.gov.bd returned 404 on 1 Nov. Not used in my chunk (clearance belongs to Step 6).
- OEP hotline 09614445271 confirmed on the live oep.gov.bd page [G0036].
- Scams at this step: no specific evidence of OEP-registration scams found; BMET's own line "don't use middlemen, pay against receipt" [B0042] used instead.

## TTC courses (Step 1.2; mentor: "process of getting into TTC courses", "course, curriculum")

- BKTTC Dhaka notice (19 May 2026, scanned PDF read in full): 10 courses, 3 or 6 months, BNQF levels 1-3, minimum JSC or SSC, 12-24 seats; register on OEP (Tk 200), then Training portal > Training (Trade/Skill) > Division > District > Institution > Select Course > Apply > Confirm; admission test; results on notice board, website and by SMS; fee Tk 1,484 (6 months) / Tk 748 (3 months); allowance Tk 150/day with 80% attendance, subject to allocation; NSDA certificate [G0002-G0006].
- Web search (Bangla) found Oct-Dec 2026 session notices at Tangail, Lakshmipur, Lalmonirhat and Kushtia TTCs (25-26 Aug 2026), including 3-month English, Arabic, Japanese, German and Korean language courses. Not fetched; one full official notice was enough to write the SOP.
- BMET's "TTC/IMT subjects" page is from 2017 (bmet-ttc-subjects-pdf saved, not cited).
- Curriculum: NOT found. TTC notices give only trade, length, BNQF level and seats. Next search: NSDA competency standards (nsda.gov.bd) per trade. Kept as "Need to know".
- CIDB: CIDB's own page (18 Oct 2024) says from Jan 2025 foreign construction workers need SKKP certification in the source country plus online eSICW [G0007]. BMET leaflet: CIDB test at Khulna TTC, to expand to Dhaka, Chattogram, Rajshahi [B0001]. NST (Feb 2026, raw news-nst-800-bd-workers-clab-2026-02) reports CLAB vetting and training of ~800 Bangladeshi workers after arrival. Still open: whether the CIDB test currently runs in Bangladesh, booking, fee.

## BOESL zero-cost jobs (Step 1.3; mentor: "reality check?")

- Circular image (5 Nov 2026) re-read from the PDF image on 7 Nov: window 06 Nov 10:00 to 08 Nov 16:00 (matches W0002 as corrected); apply with passport number, education, experience [G0031]; applying/interview/selection don't guarantee a job [G0032]; driving licence and experience count [G0033]; email info@boesl.gov.bd [G0034]. The window closes 8 Nov 2026, so the doc presents it as "the example".
- Reality check:
  - Amar Desh 28 Oct 2026: state minister Nurul Haque said going through BOESL may cost about Tk 1.55-1.60 lakh (airfare, medical, service charge) [G0010]. This conflicts with the zero-cost DHL circular and the "10,000 at zero cost" announcement; likely refers to ordinary (non-zero-cost) BOESL jobs, but not stated. Doc tells people to check the circular says no cost.
  - Daily Star Bangla 16 Oct 2026: eligibility and selection for the 10,000 not specified [G0009]; Asif Munir (migration expert) warns of false hopes.
  - Oversubscription: 2013 G2G, 1.4 million applied for ~10,000 [G0029, The Star 2013]; earlier reports (search only, not fetched) say 36,000 picked by lottery and only 4,000-8,000 actually sent. BOESL sent 1,381 Oct 2022-May 2024 [A0059].
  - No 2026 news found on lottery fairness or BOESL bribery (Bangla and English searches, 7 Nov).
- Fraud: BOESL warning (Aug 2025) of a ring using BOESL's name; BOESL has no agents [W0068].

## Private agencies (Step 1.4; mentor: "need insider info (KII) and news")

- News since the Oct 2026 reopening:
  - Daily Star Bangla 16 Oct: an FWCMS-registered agency owner says workers must pay at least Tk 5 lakh; government might set ~Tk 86,000 [G0008]. Ex-BAIRA joint secretary Fakhrul Islam: 312 agencies depend on the 25 processing agencies, effectively sub-agencies; only change is clearance under own licence. ~350 Malaysian employers applied, >6,000 quota places allotted; High Commission attested NSK Trade City demand letter for 28 workers on 29 Sep.
  - Amar Desh 29 Oct: agencies and middlemen collecting passports, asking Tk 7-7.5 lakh [G0011]; an agency owner had collected over 1,000 passports; demand letters now RM8,000-10,000 (RM10,000-12,000 by some) vs RM2,000-3,000 before [G0013]; minister: old cost unrealistic, "nobody followed it"; officials: new cost being worked out [G0012]. Comparison quoted: Nepal Tk 2-2.5 lakh, Indonesia Tk 1-1.5 lakh, Philippines Tk 50,000-80,000 (not used; corridor section).
  - 2022-24 costs (A0001, A0002, W0070, W0072, A0064) retained only as one sentence.
- KIIs: not possible from desk research. Insider accounts above are anonymous agency owners quoted in news. A proper KII with a BAIRA member, a BOESL official and returnees is still needed (mentor's ask).
- Agency lists: FWCMS list [W0008]; OEP list status values [X0001, X0002, Z0035]. No agency named: no outside evidence for any Malaysia agency (ILO statement May 2026 names no pilot; A0092).

## Permission letter (Step 1.5; mentor: "verification process")

- BMET misc-infos page re-checked 7 Nov 2026: still 19 Malaysia entries, newest DN25-225 RL1274 of 27 Feb 2025; nothing from the 2026 reopening [G0030].
- Verification route used: compare letter vs agency's claims; BMET/DEMO checks visas and demand letters free within 3 working days (Malaysia not in BMET's online check list) [B0005, B0032, B0033].
- Dropped from the doc: the KSM/KDN/BHC-LW reference-number discussion and the 2023 one-line notice (W0039, Z0004, Z0005, W0032): research detail that doesn't tell a worker what to do.

## Before paying and loans

- PKB loan rows B0013-B0019 are from the undated PKB loan page (pkb.gov.bd blocks bots); the PKB citizen charter of 28 Sep 2026 (pages 1-3 read) covers policy services, not loan terms. Prothom Alo (June 2023) confirms Tk 3 lakh, 9% simple, no collateral, decision in 7 days, and adds: valid visa needed first, bank won't arrange it, approval by SMS, guarantors hard to find [G0039, G0040]; also says grace period 2 months and 2-year term for re-entry visas (not used). 2026 news (Sep 2026) about a separate PKB language-learning loan up to Tk 10 lakh (9% or 11%) not used.
- Cap: R0008 (Tk 78,990, Jul 2022) is the last official Malaysia cap; the Ministry says it will set a new one [G0012].

## Complaints (mentor: "where and how do I complain?", "address, letter draft, reality check", "should I write to ILO/IOM?")

- Official: BMET charter (Sep 2026) complaint to DG BMET, complaint cell, 30 working days, Ministry decides [B0034, B0035]; GRS ladder [B0041]; Ministry enforcement branch [B0044, B0045]; mobile courts [B0047]; 16135 call centre [B0083].
- Addresses: BMET 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000, 02-49357972, dg@bmet.gov.bd (contact page last updated 31 Jan 2021) [G0026]; Ministry: Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000 (page updated 1 May 2025) [G0025]. Note BOESL's circular gives "71-72 Eskaton Garden, Ramna" for the same building.
- Forms: BMET paper "Complaint Form (Against Recruiting Agent)" posted by DEMO Tangail (page 18 Feb 2024) [G0020-G0022]; BMET online form ovijog.bmet.gov.bd (Wayback copy 23 Jan 2025) with evidence uploads up to 10 MB and a status search [G0023, G0024]. Live ovijog.bmet.gov.bd: curl failed (code 000) and Firecrawl failed on 7 Nov 2026; the older ovijogbmet.org returns HTTP 410. BDGovtGuide (1 Nov 2026) claims online complaints are now on employee.oep.gov.bd; not verified (needs login).
- Letter draft in the doc built from the form fields (G0020) and the charter's "self-explanatory application with proof" (B0034).
- Reality (RMMRU in-depth grievance study, uploaded Mar 2026, fieldwork to Jan 2025): documents BMET wants [G0014]; hearings usually in Dhaka [G0015]; deduction from the agency's security deposit [G0016]; by law (OEMA 2013 s.41) investigation within 30 days and settlement within 3 months of that (not used in the doc: the charter's 30 working days is the official service standard and the practice figure is 4 months-1 year, A0073); court cases possible in parallel but workers can't sustain them [G0017]; village courts can award up to Tk 3 lakh on contract/receipt claims [G0018]; WARBE doesn't arbitrate sub-agent fraud [G0019]; RMMRU says 51% of migrants face fraud by agents or sub-agents (not used).
- ILO: representation procedure is for industrial associations of employers or workers against member States [G0027]; UN DESA compendium also says ILO procedures are usually not open to individuals (saved: un-compendium-ilo-complaints, text copy made from the HTML; not cited). IOM: its Prottasha hotline (run with BRAC) was handed to the Welfare Board and merged into 16135 in 2022 [G0028]. No IOM individual-case route in Bangladesh found.
- BRAC: not recommended in the doc (project rule: don't recommend BRAC Probashbandhu; no evidence found that BRAC handles agency-fraud cases for aspirant migrants).
