# Findings, part H: leaving Malaysia and coming home (practical pass)

Researched 6 Nov 2026. Ledger rows H0001-H0046 (ledger-parts/H.csv). 23 sources registered (22 news/NGO, `--official no`; 1 BRAC's own contact page, `--official yes`, as for the existing `brac-migration` source). Almost everything here is practice context: news rows are `unofficial` (what happens) or `lead` (a rule reported only by news, used only where an official search failed). None of them may set a fee, eligibility, document, processing time or legal rule in the doc; where they touch one, the doc needs a "Need to know" with the report labelled "unofficial, not verified".

Searched: English, Malay and Bangla (WebSearch), imi.gov.my / moha.gov.my / mofa.gov.bd (site-restricted), bb.org.bd circular list (Firecrawl single scrape: it loads, but no 2026 incentive circular is in the latest entries), brac.net.

## Leaving at the end of the job (doc: Work visa, "Leaving at the end of the job")

- **Employer must now be at the exit point for the Check-Out Memo (COM).** The Home Minister said in May 2025 that employers must be present at international exit points to manage the exit registration of foreign workers, as on entry; employers who don't comply may face limits on new quota applications [H0029, H0030]. Lead only: no KDN/IMI text found. Adds to the official COM steps L0071-L0074.
- **What goes wrong in practice:** Bangladeshi workers at a Malaysian factory went 3 months unpaid, with passports held by the company and about 35 left without a valid PLKS because the company didn't renew; they complained to the Labour Department (JTK, Jabatan Tenaga Kerja) with the Migrant Welfare Network platform [H0032]. Without a valid pass such a worker can't leave through the normal COM route; see PRM below.

## Going home on leave (doc: "Going home on leave", currently a Need to know)

- **Multiple-entry visa for Bangladeshi PLKS workers:** reported from 8 Aug 2025 (Home Ministry statement via FMT): workers already holding a PLKS and a single-entry visa don't apply separately; the multiple-entry visa (MEV) is given when the PLKS is renewed for the next year [H0027, H0028]. Lead only: the KDN/IMI circular (reported dated 10 Jul 2025) was not found on imi.gov.my or moha.gov.my.
- **Need to know (keep):** the official PLKS re-entry rule. Also whether IMI's MEV restriction from 1 Sept 2026 (V0009, written for visitors) touches workers' MEVs. The official rule that not returning on the agreed date counts as running away (L0058) stays the core line.

## If you want to change employer (cross-reference for part L/W)

- **Conflict:** The Star (May 2025) reports the same KDN-KSM meeting allowed employers to apply to move foreign workers **across sectors** (previously only within a sector, on closure, restructuring or expansion, or with a Labour Department committee's approval) [H0031], against the official but undated IMI pages L0055/W0052 (no change of employer or sector) and L0082 (outsourcing: same sector only). Status `conflict` on H0031. The doc must show the official line as the rule and add a Need to know: the current official process for a cross-sector transfer.

## Going home without valid papers (doc: same section)

**Travel Permit (TP) in practice**
- The High Commission issued about 83,000 travel permits to Bangladeshis May-Dec 2025 and about 100 a day in April 2026 [H0005]; 6,853 travel passes Jan-Jun 2026, 1,319 of them to people in detention camps [H0022]. (Two different counts from two outlets; the doc should not merge them.)
- High Commission staff visit 8 prisons and 13 detention camps regularly [H0023]; about 400 people got consular or legal help Jan-Jun 2026 [H0026].

**PRM 2.0 (Program Repatriasi Migran 2.0) in practice**
- The High Commission's own guidance (First Secretary Press, video on IMI's Facebook page, Sept 2026, reported by bdnews24 and Jugantor): valid passport or TP [H0001]; confirmed ticket dated **at least 5 days after the Immigration appointment and within 14 days** [H0002] (the official IMI poster L0116 gives only "within 14 days": the 5-day minimum is unofficial, not verified); appear in person, no representative; bring the original passport or TP, a copy of the biodata page, a copy of the relevant immigration pass, old and new passports if any, and the ticket [H0003]. "There is no need to pay any broker" [H0004].
- **Who can't use PRM 2.0:** people who registered under an earlier repatriation programme but didn't leave, people blacklisted by Immigration, people wanted by the authorities [H0008] (lead: an exact eligibility rule; not on the IMI poster, so Need to know with this report labelled unofficial).
- **Numbers:** Bangladeshis had the highest participation in Feb 2026 [H0007]; by mid-April 2026 Indonesians were first and Bangladeshis second, 204,523 gone out of 228,961 registered [H0006]. (Corridor section only.)
- **Crowds:** before Eid 2026 Klang Valley workers went to the Seremban office, which serves 150 a day; queues from 6am for an 8am opening; staff working to 10pm [H0009, H0010]; a Bangladeshi construction worker with work-pass problems used it to go home for Eid [H0011]. Practical point for the doc: go early, expect a cap per day, don't wait for the last weeks (the High Commission also says "do not wait until the last moment" [H0004 source]).

**Deportation and depots**
- On entering a depot, biometrics are taken and the person is blacklisted as a prohibited immigrant [H0015 (2022)].
- Deportees in 2026 (Kedah, Belantik depot; KLIA) were blacklisted in the Immigration (JIM) system to stop re-entry [H0018, H0019, H0021]; the air ticket is paid by the detainee, the family or the embassy [H0020]; in 2024, 39 Bangladeshis from the Pekan Nenas depot went home at their own cost after serving sentences [H0016, H0017 (2024)]. Official complement: L0017/L0018 (camp arranges the flight once there is a passport and a ticket; TP free in camps).

**How long the ban lasts (doc Need to know, step 5)**
- No official source found (searched imi.gov.my, Malay and English news, Akta 155 already in raw/). Only news: the Immigration DG said in 2017 that deported undocumented migrants can be blacklisted **1 to 5 years** by category of offence, and for **life** in serious cases, with a fingerprint blacklist (BioSL) [H0012, H0013, H0014 (2017)]; Daily Star Bangla (2024) reported **5 years or for ever** for 39 deported Bangladeshis [H0016 (2024)]; 2026 reports say "for a set period" without the length [H0019]. Official hook: prohibited immigrants include people removed from any country whom the DG deems undesirable (L0091).
- Whether **PRM 2.0 participants** are blacklisted: not found in any source (official or news). Keep as Need to know.

## If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job (doc: same section)

- Jan-Jun 2026: 13 bodies sent home at Bangladesh government cost; the High Commission helped with 584 more paid by employers, companies or relatives [H0024]; it recovered RM492,667 in unpaid wages and "condolence money" compensation [H0025].
- Cost in practice: about RM7,000 to send a body from Johor; one body lay 18 days in a hospital morgue because no relative was found, and a Bangladeshi community association paid [H0033 (2024)].
- At the airport the Welfare Board pays Tk35,000 per body, in practice as well (Apr 2026, incl. 2 bodies from Malaysia) [H0034], matching official B0066.
- Context: 4,813 bodies came home in 2024; Malaysia is among the main origins [H0036]; migrants say causes are often written as stroke or heart attack to avoid accident compensation [H0037]. (Context only; the doc rule is to ask for the police report and post-mortem papers already listed in L0023-L0025.)
- PERKESO: official PDFs already in raw/ (perkeso-foreign-worker-*), registered by another part. Not repeated here.

## Coming home (doc: same section)

- **Airport:** a BRAC Migration Programme official is stationed at Shahjalal airport [H0035]; Welfare Board Tk35,000 at the desk [H0034]. The Wage Earners' Centre at Khilkhet/Borua was reported in 2024 as temporarily disrupted because of a bad access road [H0046 (2024)]; official B0080 describes it as open, so the doc keeps B0080 and may add "check before going".
- **BRAC contact (doc Need to know):** brac.net gives only general contacts: BRAC Centre, 75 Mohakhali, Dhaka-1212; tel 88 02 2222 81265; info@brac.net [H0041, H0042]. No Migration Programme number on brac.net. A 2019 BRAC/IOM "Prottasha" returnee hotline (08000102030 toll free, 09610102030) [H0043 (2019)] was later reported merged into the government's Probashbondhu call centre (16135); current status unverified, so the doc should give the BRAC Centre contact and keep a narrower Need to know (Migration Programme's own line).
- **Reintegration:** RAISE (official B0084: July 2021-June 2026) paid direct cash to 29,518 returnees through district welfare centres ("Obhibashi Sheba Kendra") in RMMRU's districts alone [H0038]; many returnees stayed on the waiting list and RMMRU calls for a follow-up project [H0039]; returnees with trade skills were referred for Recognition of Prior Learning (RPL) at TTCs [H0040]. For the doc: RAISE has ended; Need to know whether any successor scheme takes registrations now.

## Sending money home (doc: same section)

- **Risk of hundi to the family:** in 2022 Bangladesh Bank and BFIU (Bangladesh Financial Intelligence Unit) suspended about 5,000 agentships including 480 bKash/Nagad agent accounts over hundi, so remittances in those accounts couldn't be cashed [H0044 (2022)]; Bangladesh Bank's notice called sending money outside banking channels a punishable offence [H0045 (2022)] (lead: legal rule, needs the official notice).
- **2.5% incentive circular:** not found. bb.org.bd's circular list loads via Firecrawl (raw/bb-mediaroom-circular-list-fc.md) (the 10 newest entries, 26 Oct-5 Nov 2026, have no incentive circular); FEPD-2 Circular 09 of 29 Oct 2026 (consolidated FX rules, raw/bb-fepd2-circular-09-2026-10) does not mention the incentive. Existing lead B0104 (budget 11 Jun 2026) stands.

## Draft steps (for the doc writer)

**Leaving at the end of the job** (add after L0071-L0074 steps):
- Your employer must come to the airport with you and register your exit; this was announced in May 2025 (unofficial, not verified) [H0029]. If the company has stopped paying you, holds your passport or hasn't renewed your PLKS, complain to the Labour Department (JTK) and contact the High Commission's labour wing (B0117) [H0032; L-part steps].

**Going home on leave:**
- Need to know: the PLKS re-entry rule. Reported (unofficial, not verified): since 8 Aug 2025 Bangladeshi workers get a multiple-entry visa with their PLKS renewal, without a separate application [H0027, H0028]. Not returning on the agreed date counts as running away [L0058].

**Going home without valid papers:**
1. No passport: get a Travel Permit at the High Commission (official steps L0001-L0016). In practice it issued about 100 a day in April 2026 [H0005].
2. PRM 2.0 (official steps L0113-L0124). In practice: buy a ticket dated at least 5 days after your Immigration appointment and within 14 days (unofficial, not verified) [H0002]; go yourself, no agent; bring original passport or TP, a copy of the biodata page, a copy of your last pass, old and new passports, the ticket [H0003]; don't pay brokers [H0004]; expect daily caps and early queues [H0009, H0010]. Need to know: who is excluded (reported: earlier no-shows, blacklisted, wanted people [H0008], unofficial).
3. If detained: the depot takes fingerprints and blacklists you [H0015 (2022)]; you, your family or the embassy pay the ticket [H0020]; the High Commission visits camps and issues TPs there [H0023, H0022; official L0017-L0018].
4. Need to know: how long the ban lasts. Reported (unofficial, not verified): 1-5 years by offence, life for serious cases [H0012, H0013 (2017)]; "5 years or for ever" [H0016 (2024)]. Whether PRM participants are blacklisted: not found.

**If a worker dies:** add after step 2: in practice, sending a body from Malaysia has cost about RM7,000; where no employer pays, families and community groups have raised it, and the High Commission sent 13 bodies at government cost in Jan-Jun 2026 (unofficial, not verified) [H0033 (2024), H0024].

**Coming home:** BRAC: BRAC Centre, 75 Mohakhali, Dhaka-1212, tel 88 02 2222 81265, info@brac.net [H0041, H0042]; BRAC staff are at Shahjalal airport [H0035]. RAISE cash support has ended [B0084; H0039].

## Need to know (open, with what was searched)

- Ban length after deportation or PRM (searched imi.gov.my in Malay/English, "senarai hitam" + "tempoh", news 2017-2026; only news, H0012-H0019).
- Whether PRM 2.0 participants are blacklisted (no source either way).
- PRM 2.0 exclusions (H0008 news only) and the 5-day minimum before the flight (H0002, High Commission via news only; IMI poster silent).
- PLKS re-entry / MEV for workers: official KDN/IMI circular of 10 Jul 2025 (not found on imi.gov.my, moha.gov.my).
- Employer at exit point for COM (H0029): official KDN statement of 8 May 2025 not found.
- Cross-sector change of employer (H0031): official KDN/JTK process.
- BRAC Migration Programme's own phone line (only BRAC HQ general line found).
- Bangladesh Bank FEPD circular on the 2.5% incentive for FY2026-27 (circular list scraped; not among latest entries; needs a search of older pages of the list, which is a JavaScript table).
- Any successor to RAISE taking returnee registrations now.

## Conflicts

- H0031 (news, cross-sector transfer allowed since May 2025) vs L0055 / W0052 / L0082 (official IMI, undated): status conflict on H0031.
- H0006 vs H0007 (news vs news): which nationality led PRM 2.0 participation (changed between Feb and Apr 2026); corridor detail only.
- H0046 (2024 news: Wage Earners' Centre disrupted) vs B0080 (official, centre open): not marked conflict (temporary, old); doc keeps B0080.

## Blocked

- None needing manual download. Not fetchable by curl (403), not essential, not retried with Firecrawl: jagonews24.com articles (MEV for Bangladeshi workers, https://www.jagonews24.com/en/national/news/84080; "140 deported, lifetime ban", https://jagonews24.com/probash/news/1156224); dailyinqilab customs harassment (https://dailyinqilab.com/national/news/931136).


# Findings, part I: PLKS steps 2-7 (medical, VDR, passport/police clearance, check visa, orientation/clearance/flight, after arrival incl MDAC and FOMEMA)

Draft: drafts/MY2.md (replaces malaysia.src.md lines 58-101). New ledger rows I0001-I0026 (26 rows); 13 new sources. Researched 7 Nov 2026.

## Changes from the old draft and why

- **EPF (retirement fund):** the old draft said EPF for foreign workers was only "proposed (Feb 2025), no start date" [M0163, M0164]. EPF's own page says mandatory from October 2025 wages, 2% employer + 2% worker, for Visitor's Pass (Foreign Workers) holders except domestic helpers; automatic registration (notices sent from 20 Sep 2025); worker must visit an EPF office for thumbprint; full "Leaving the Country Withdrawal" exists; Akaun Fleksibel withdrawals from RM50 and age 50/55/60 withdrawals online from 31 Oct 2026 [I0014-I0018]. The "Your rights at work > Pension" bullet and "Need to know: whether EPF has started" elsewhere in the doc are now outdated; whoever owns those chunks should update them. The KWSP news page (kwsp.gov.my/en/w/news/epf-begins-mandatory-...) returned 403 to curl; the FAQ page was scraped by Firecrawl instead.
- **Passport police verification:** Home Ministry circular of 18 Feb 2025 (scanned PDF, transcribed) ends police verification for new passports based on the online-verified NID [I0009]. DW Feb 2025 confirms and quotes TIB: three-quarters of applicants paid bribes at police verification; money demanded "at every step" [I0010, I0011]. TIB 2017 (jagonews, scraped but not saved/cited): Tk 797 average bribe for verification, Tk 2,221 at the office; too old to cite.
- **Passport complaints:** helpline 16445 / 09666716445 (Natore RPO notice, Apr 2023) [I0024]; each passport office site lists a grievance redress officer and links to GRS [I0025, I0026].
- **FOMEMA result for workers:** IMI MyIMMs has a public "FOMEMA Application Status" by passport number + citizenship [I0001]. This resolves the old "Need to know: how a worker sees their own result" partly (status, not the report). D0028 (FMT 2025: no one sees the report) kept as practice.
- **SPIKPA:** MOH page: cashless at MOH hospitals, show valid passport, worker pays above limit, unpaid bills block renewal, employer needs JTK permission to deduct premium [I0019-I0021]. CONFLICT on the cover limit: MOH page (undated, describes 2011 scheme) says RM10,000/yr and RM120 premium; insurers (Tokio Marine, Chubb, eInsurans) say RM20,000/yr for RM120. Not saved; doc omits the amount. Need an official current figure.
- **PDO:** BMET's PDO page lists the 10 course topics, a pre-departure checklist, contract checks (get contract 2 days before) and luggage rules [I0003-I0006]. Official portal training.oep.gov.bd has enrollment-card and certificate downloads by passport/NID [I0007, I0008]. Fee: BMET charter says institute-set, paid against money receipt [B0036]. probashiapp.net (Oct 2026 SEO blog, scraped not saved) claims Tk 100 course + Tk 100 service fee via the Ami Probashi app, 2-year certificate validity, fake-certificate rackets charging Tk 2,000-10,000; not cited because the site is an unofficial app blog with unverifiable and partly generic content.
- **PNR / fake tickets:** TBS May 2024: agencies gave fake tickets, workers turned out of the airport, last-minute demands of Tk 50,000 more [I0012, I0013]. How to check: Biman FAQ Manage Booking with six-character PNR + last name [I0022] (biman-airlines.com 403 to curl, Firecrawl direct-quote extraction saved). Other carriers (US-Bangla, Malaysia Airlines, AirAsia) not checked.
- **Medical (Bio-Medical):** Ministry Facebook notices 28 and 31 Oct 2026 (Z0159-Z0168) remain the core. Notable: "the agency that made the medical slip" = the agency makes the MiGRAMS slip. Ministry says the approved list "will soon be published on the website"; probashi.gov.bd notices search shows only 2022 and 2023 lists (probashi-notice-medical-centre-list 17 Aug 2022, -list-published 25 Jul 2022, -enlistment 4 Apr 2023); not used. FWCMS list of 51 (Jugantor 6 Nov 2026, D0014) – all Dhaka/Gazipur addresses per Jugantor. Order conflict: FWCMS says Bio-Medical before eVDR [W0009]; Ministry 28 Oct says agencies must submit VDR papers to the centre [Z0162]. Doc states both rules where they apply without commenting; worth asking BMET/Ministry.
- **Dropped from the doc (kept here):** security bond RM500 vs RM750 conflict (Z0087, Z0088, Z0169) and levy table (M0226, M0227, Z0006) – employer costs, not worker actions; doc says the employer pays all of them [W0054]. i-Kad delivery conflict (vendor vs employer collects) and colour conflict (Z0092, Z0093): doc follows IMI's Feb 2026 poster [L0126, L0127]. Apostille (B0110): PLKS document list (W0055) asks for no attested certificates, so dropped from the PLKS steps; relevant to Employment Pass. FOMEMA "within 3 days" IMI briefing (W0069). One Stop Centre ministry move (R0001). PCC Tk 500 (B0109, older police guidance) vs Tk 1,500 on the portal (B0105): doc uses the portal fee. Smart card fee: Dec 2024 news says abolished (D0059) vs BMET charter Sep 2026 lists Tk 250 clearance fee (B0028) and Tk 250 smart certificate fee for self-obtained visas (B0030): doc uses the charter's Tk 250 clearance fee only. Human Diagnostic centre being on the FWCMS list while sealed (D0019) – cut as detail.
- **Clearance fees:** welfare fee Tk 5,500 from 1 Oct 2026 (B0043) supersedes the charter's Tk 3,500 (B0028); insurance Tk 1,000 (B0008); source tax on group visas Tk 800 unskilled / Tk 1,200 skilled (B0028).

## Reality checks (what happens in practice)

- Medical: previous round, agencies took Tk 11,000 vs fixed Tk 6,500; five times as many did medicals as went (TBS Sep 2025, D0021, D0022); unfit-then-bribe at Gulf centres (D0023); unauthorised testing centre sealed 1 Oct 2026 (D0017). No reports yet (search 7 Nov 2026, past month) of overcharging since 31 Oct; minister repeated the Tk 8,000 cap in early Nov.
- VDR: levy deducted from wages (F0082, US TIP 2026).
- Arrival: workers arrived with valid permits and no jobs (ILO May 2026, A0095; Dhaka Tribune Jun 2026, A0014). Reuters May 2023 and Andy Hall May 2024 (KLIA overrun) found but not saved.
- FOMEMA: no report access (D0028); forged passports sold to FOMEMA-unfit workers (D0035).
- Passport retention: widespread (F0084, TIP 2026); a June 2026 case of workers complaining to JTK with Migrant Welfare Network (H0032). OHCHR Nov 2025 press release on systematic exploitation of Bangladeshi workers: 403, not saved.
- ILO/IOM: ILO representation procedure is for workers'/employers' organisations, not individuals (G0027); IOM hotline merged into 16135 (G0028).

## Need to know still open (after search)

1. Where the Ministry posts its own approved medical-centre list (only FWCMS list found).
2. How a worker sees their own Bio-Medical result (FWCMS public pages have no worker check; searched English and Bangla).
3. Whether/how an employer can appeal a refused VDR, and what happens to money paid if refused.
4. Names/addresses of the High Commission's authorised visa agencies for worker visas, who pays their charges, processing time (HC agencies page lists only Tourism Malaysia; Facebook travel agencies claim to be "authorised" – unverifiable).
5. Whether employers or agencies still ask Malaysia-bound workers for a police clearance (only YouTube/Facebook claims found; neither IMI's VDR list nor BMET's clearance list includes it).
6. How long a police clearance takes.
7. Where a worker finds their ePass number (IMI ESD FAQ says the ePass check is for companies, embassies, banks).
8. The PDO fee (charter: set by institute).
9. PNR check for airlines other than Biman.
10. Whether the SIM "work permit document" is the i-Kad.
11. (For other chunks) current SPIKPA cover limit (RM10,000 on MOH page vs RM20,000 from insurers).

## Source dates

- Ministry FB notices: 28 and 31 Oct 2026. DIP circular: 18 Feb 2025. DW: 21 Feb 2025. TBS tickets: 31 May 2024. Natore passport helpline: 1 Apr 2023. KWSP, MOH SPIKPA, BMET PDO, OEP training portal, FOMEMA guidelines, IMI FOMEMA status, Biman FAQ: undated (live pages, accessed 7 Nov 2026).

## Fix pass after the independent check (7 Nov 2026)

New ledger rows I0027-I0046 (20 rows); 9 new sources registered (Feni TTC PDO fee news, Malaysia Airlines/US-Bangla/AirAsia booking pages, IMI enforcement page, e-Passport onboarding and enrolment-documents notice, Bangladesh Police online GD, SPIKPA MOH FAQ).

- **Medical:** intro now states the rule (Z0159 says "immigration clearance", not BMET clearance). Order of medical vs VDR is now an explicit Need to know (W0009 vs Z0162). MiGRAMS explained from D0024 (Observer 2023: Bestinet's online registration portal for health screening). "Tk 8,000 for general workers" (Z0164). B0042 receipt line replaced by "keep the receipt as evidence" (Z0163). W0058 (Jan 2022 COVID-era SOP) dropped; "if you pass, the employer can apply" now rests on W0009. Statistics (D0021 Tk 11,000 vs 6,500), Gulf unfit-bribe line (D0023) and the 1 Oct mobile-court anecdote (D0017) cut. Complaint route now: Ministry secretary -> enforcement branch (B0044/B0045, 3-5 days start, 30 working days) -> BMET DG complaint cell (B0034, G0026) or DEMO (G0047) -> police online GD for a broker holding a passport (I0045) -> 16135. Mobile court kept as a remedy (B0047) with a Need to know on how to request one: the charter gives no application route; searches found only Ministry/BMET-initiated drives (A0043, D0017).
- **Ministry medical-centre list:** probashi.gov.bd notice 6940313035ce18e1c054e919 ("medical centre list published") is dated 25 Jul 2022 (old round). News of 6 Nov 2026 (Jugantor, Dhaka Tribune, Daily Star Bangla) all point to the FWCMS list of 51; Instagram/TV posts quote the Ministry saying its list "will soon" be on its website. Need to know kept, "The Ministry says" dropped.
- **VDR:** levy-deduction route added (WFW/JTK -> labour wing -> 16135). W0065 IC option added; "agency gives the number" removed (now a Need to know). Refused VDR: W0036 (no new permissions until the agency repays workers) + BMET/Ministry complaint; Need to know narrowed to employer appeal. High Commission fees: visa fee paid by the employer (W0054); RM105 processing fee with "about BDT 2,500" is on the High Commission's own visa-requirement page (I0043), so the conversion stays, cited. Who pays the processing and agency fees: Need to know. High Commission address/contacts from B0121.
- **Passport:** validity line rewritten as a rule (W0003, L0047; e-passports are 5 or 10 years, D0049). Enrolment: portal names the RPO by district/police station (I0032); documents to bring incl. printed application summary with appointment (I0033, notice dated 7 May 2025). "Speed money" inference removed. TIB line now quotes the director's "every step" sentence (I0046). Complaint route ordered: 16445 -> office grievance officer (I0025 example) -> GRS; "each" dropped. Online GD how-to (I0034, I0039): NID number, mobile with OTP, live photo; digital GD copy. GD fee: no source found; Need to know.
- **Police clearance:** B0105/B0109 conflict resolved in favour of the live portal (Tk 1,500, code 1-7301-0001-2681, Bangladesh Bank/Sonali Bank or card with service charge) as I0040; B0109's Tk 500 (code 1-2201-...) is the older police.gov.bd text, and Bangla news/videos report the rise from Tk 500 to Tk 1,500. B0105's status in part B was left unchanged (part I only); the merge owner may want to mark B0109 superseded. Collection with passport/application copy (I0042) and PCC helpdesk (I0041) added. PCC processing time: only a commercial site (rates.bd, "7-10 days") found; Need to know kept. Clearance-list sentence now cites W0055, W0030 and B0029 without narration.
- **Check visa:** BMET address/phone (G0026), DEMO list link (G0047), ePass page "meant for authorities and agencies" (W0066), and an ordered fake-document route (BMET complaint with G0014 documents, Ministry enforcement, High Commission of Malaysia dhaka@imi.gov.my, police online GD, 16135).
- **PDO:** TTC list link (G0048, G0049). Fee: Feni TTC news of 3 Feb 2026 says Tk 200 and "pay no more" (I0027); Munshiganj TTC page not fetched. Topic list trimmed. Contract: route added; W0030 now framed as a condition in BMET's Malaysia permission letters, with a Need to know on whether every letter has it.
- **Tickets:** Manage Booking pages for Malaysia Airlines (I0028), US-Bangla (I0029, booking reference or ticket number) and AirAsia (I0030, check-in search with booking number + name). I0013 wording matched ("Tk 50,000 more"). Route added.
- **MDAC:** moved to Step 6 (before departure); exemptions listed in full (V0060); free (N0057, V0065); PIN. Fake-site refund route: no source found; Need to know.
- **FOMEMA:** 30-day route added; "full result" -> "can check the result" (I0002). Unfit: only employer/agency appeals, appeal fee paid to FOMEMA via portal (I0044, amount not published); fare home on employer (W0036). Forged-passport anecdote (D0035) cut to the jail rule (L0089).
- **Passports Act penalty:** IMI's current enforcement page confirms s.12(1) = fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years or both (I0031). The AGC reprint PDF (lom.agc.gov.my/ilims/upload/portal/akta/LOM/EN/Act%20150%20Reprint%202006.pdf) returned HTTP 500; mylegal2u says the AGC text is the 2006 reprint.
- **Passport retention route:** H0032 anecdote cut; ILO/IOM narration (G0027, G0028) cut. No official source found telling a worker to make a police report or IMI complaint to recover a passport (IMI enforcement page covers only lost passports; lawyers' and Bar Council pages discuss the offence). Need to know added.
- **SPIKPA:** MOH SPIKPA portal FAQ: compulsory, RM120 a year, cover starts on registration and payment, employer may pay first and deduct from wages (I0035, I0036). MOH Selangor page and moh.gov.my snippet still say RM10,000 cover; insurers (Tokio Marine, Chubb, Zurich, eInsurans) say RM20,000. Cover limit left as Need to know. moh.gov.my SPIKPA page: curl 403. PLKS renewal block from unpaid bills added (I0020).
- **EPF:** Leaving Country Withdrawal procedure from J0017-J0027 and EPF FAQ 42 for PLKS holders (I0037: passport copy, Check-out memo, bank document). Domestic helpers excluded (I0016). Payment time not published; Need to know.
- **i-Kad:** route added (WFW/JTK, labour wing).

Blocked in this pass: lom.agc.gov.my Act 150 Reprint 2006 PDF (HTTP 500); moh.gov.my/en/private/skim-perlindungan-insurans-kesihatan-pekerja-asing (403); epassport.gov.bd/onboarding (403 to curl, Firecrawl worked).


# Findings, part J: PLKS rights, stay, jail, unpaid, change employer, lari, leaving, leave, family, scams; domestic worker

Rewrite of malaysia.src.md lines 102-176 into drafts/MY3.md (7 Nov 2026). New ledger rows J0001-J0086 (ledger-parts/J.csv). Everything carried over from the old draft was re-checked against its ledger row (claim and quote); the Employment Act sick-leave figures were re-read in raw/jtksm-employment-act-1955-act265-2023.txt.

## What changed from the old draft

- Every complaint route is now an ordered procedure with "You'll need", where, what to send, sample text, what you get back, time and a reality check: WFW app, High Commission labour wing, Labour Court, NGOs/legal aid, Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare.
- New, from official sources: EPF (retirement fund) is now compulsory for foreign workers (2% + 2% from Oct 2025 wages) and can be withdrawn in full on leaving Malaysia, with the documents and the 1 Nov 2026 bank-document rule [J0011-J0029]. This replaces the old "Need to know: any pension".
- New: Bangladesh's Probash pension scheme (National Pension Authority) for migrants [J0071-J0081, J0085, J0086].
- New: termination-benefit rates (10/15/20 days' wages per year) [J0053, J0054] (OECD note quoting the 1980 Regulations; the Regulations themselves were not saved).
- New: drug-trafficking penalty text after Act 846: death or life imprisonment, at least 12 strokes if not sentenced to death [J0052 with official K0041].
- New: government policy that foreign workers can't get permanent residence [J0055, J0056] (Immigration DG, Mar 2021, via news; IMI's own Facebook post blocked).
- New: Special Pass, the only way to stay legally while a labour case runs after the pass ends [J0031-J0034].
- New: IMI's online blacklist check is for Malaysian citizens only (12-digit IC) [J0001, J0002], so a worker can't check a lari report online.
- New: only the employer or its appointed company representative may deal with Immigration; using an agent is an offence [J0061, J0062]; changing sector needs KDN permission first [J0063].
- Rules stated once: sick leave 14/18/22 days (Employment Act s.60F, M0023) instead of showing MyGOV's 16; FOMEMA medical yearly (FOMEMA, Aug 2026, Z0130) instead of IMI's "2nd and 3rd year only"; PLKS total 10 years (L0050, M0229, M0157) instead of IMI's PLKS page "5 years" (L0066); overstay fine "not less than RM10,000" (Act text K0015 and IMI English page R0004) with the compound of up to RM3,000 (L0086).
- Dropped as not actionable: the High Commission's phone/fax confusion (+603 2604 0934), the BMET/Bangladesh MyGOV source comparison lines, the 2023 complaint statistics in the rights section, the Budget 2027 RM2,500 semi-skilled proposal (only an unofficial blog), the employers-at-exit-point lead (H0029, still unofficial and changes nothing the worker does), the Sep 2026 arrest count (kept only the RM150/RM70 prices).

## Conflicts (kept out of the doc)

- Sick leave 2-5 years: 18 days (Employment Act as amended, M0023) vs 16 days (MyGOV page, L0110). Statute used.
- PLKS maximum: 10 years (L0050, M0229, M0157, R0007) vs 5 years (IMI PLKS page, L0066). 10 used: three official pages, and the MOHR policy.
- FOMEMA at renewal: every year since Dec 2023 (FOMEMA, Z0130, dated Aug 2026) vs only 2nd and 3rd year (IMI, L0049, M0221, undated). FOMEMA's dated page used.
- Overstay penalty: fine "not less than RM10,000" (Act 155 2006 text K0015; IMI English R0004) vs "not more than RM10,000" (IMI Malay page L0086). The doc says "at least RM10,000"; if the Act was amended later this could be wrong. Re-check against a current Act 155 reprint when one is available (the saved AGC copy is the 1975 revision, K0038).
- Changing sector: IMI says a PLKS holder may not change sector (L0055); IMI FAQ 2021 says the employer needs KDN permission first (J0063); May 2025 news says the government now lets employers apply across sectors (H0031, F0077). The doc gives the KDN-permission rule and the 2025 statement; the 2026 MOHR form that would settle it is blocked.
- Maximum housing rent: leads give RM100 (negaraku.md, factoryhub, Scribd copy of a JTK guide, "Peraturan kadar sewa atau caj maksimum, kuat kuasa 1 September 2020") and RM150 (a KESUMA Facebook post seen only as a search snippet). Not saved; "Need to know" kept.

## Reality checks (detail behind the doc's lines)

- **Labour Court in practice** (Bar Council guide, 2021) [J0036-J0046]: complaints free at any state Labour Office, by letter, email, in person or phone (Telekerja); no lawyers; documents demanded (passport, contract, payslip, arrival card, termination letter) though the Act only needs a statement; worker must attend every date; employers delay until the pass expires; MOHR figures: ~50% settled at mention, ~20% go to hearing, ~85% of those won by the worker, 38% withdrawn or dismissed for non-attendance; average 34 days (negotiated) and 84 days (hearing). Complaints page and hotline not in Bengali. NGOs help gather documents.
- **SUHAKAM** (Malaysia's human rights commission, submission to UNGA 80th session, undated, uses Sep 2024 data) [J0065-J0070]: false absconding reports near contract end and after complaints; 40% of WFW grievances about unpaid wages/illegal deductions; workers lack payslips/contracts; passport confiscation routine; contract substitution on arrival.
- **Fortify Rights / Migrant Welfare Network, 30 Sep 2026** [J0057-J0060]: visa cancelled after a wage protest; permit not renewed then employer denied employing the worker, 46 days in Muar depot; RM100/month housing deduction and RM500 for uniforms; agency videos falsely recording the official fee (Tk 79,000) when workers paid ~Tk 5.5-5.7 lakh.
- **High Commission** (Jagonews, Mar 2026; Jugantor, Sep 2026; Jagonews Aug 2026) [F0018-F0025]: Esstar Vision case: complaint by email 16 Jan 2026, first Labour Department hearing 28 Jan with a High Commission officer present, company meeting 11 Feb, visit 8-9 Mar, still unpaid mid-March. HC says 330+ of ~450 complaints since 2022 resolved; RM11.42 million recovered on 2,857 claims in 2025. NSU policy brief: no dedicated helpline, HC slow (A0111).
- **WFW**: launched May 2021; minister said complaints handled in 3-7 days; 17 complaint categories; registration with passport number (AJobThing, May 2021) [J0003-J0006]. No independent data on WFW resolution rates for migrants found; SUHAKAM figure above is the only one.
- **Tenaganita**: handled ~125 unpaid-wage/forced-labour cases in 2025 (Instagram snippet only, not saved); helped 74 Bangladeshi workers win RM1.54 million at the KL labour office (A0086).
- **Bar Council Legal Aid Centres** [J0045-J0051]: free advice to non-citizens, settlement help, rarely court representation; all state centres in raw/malaysianbar-legal-aid-pamphlet-2026.txt (Johor +607 223 5698, Selangor +603 5510 7007, Penang, etc.).
- **ILO/IOM**: searched ILO site for individual complaints; ILO procedures are for states and organisations, not individual workers (no quotable page saved). No IOM Malaysia individual-case channel found. Kept as "Need to know" in the doc. Bar guide lists the people who actually advise migrants: NGO staff, legal-aid volunteers, embassy labour officers, unions [J0083].

# Part K: Malaysian law texts (citizenship by birth, immigration offences, passports, death penalty)

Sources read (all in raw/, registered in sources.csv; not edited):

- `federal-constitution-en` (.txt): Federal Constitution, **reprint as at 30 November 2025** (latest amendment in force: Act A1663, 5 Oct 2022; Act A1752 of 2025 noted as "not yet in force"). Status verified. (sources.csv lists page_date "undated"; the cover shows 30 Nov 2025.)
- `immigration-act-155-en` (.pdf, image-only, 50 pages, CamScanner scan): this is **not a current reprint**. It is the Immigration Act 1959/63 **Revised 1975** ("Revised up to 31st December, 1974"; in force 1 May 1975; gazetted 10 April 1975), with handwritten amendment marks (A719, A985, A1154/02). Its arrangement of sections (pp.4-5) has no ss.55A-55E. Read pp.1-2, 4-5, 11-12 to establish this; not used for any current penalty [K0038].
- `imi-immigration-act-155-pdf` (.txt, text layer): Immigration Act 1959/63 reprint **incorporating all amendments up to 1 January 2006** (Commissioner of Law Revision, 2006), hosted by the Immigration Department (imi.gov.my). All Immigration Act rows below come from this text; status possibly-outdated. It is the newest consolidated text available in raw/.
- `lom-act846` (.txt): Abolition of Mandatory Death Penalty Act 2023 (Act 846), **Royal Assent 9 June 2023, gazetted 16 June 2023**, as enacted; commencement "on a date to be appointed" (date not in the file). Status possibly-outdated.
- `lom-passports-act-1966` (.md/.txt): Passports Act 1966 (Act 150) on UNHCR Refworld; Refworld date **1 April 1966**; "unofficial consolidation" including amendments up to **Act A948 (in force 1 January 1997)**. Logged with page date 1997-01-01, possibly-outdated.

## Citizenship of a child born in Malaysia (doc: "having a child"; family sections per visa)

- Born on/after Malaysia Day = citizen by operation of law only if a qualification in Second Schedule Part II applies [K0001].
- Rule: citizen if **at least one parent is, at the time of birth, a Malaysian citizen or permanent resident** (Sch.2 Pt II s.1(a)) [K0002]. A child of two Bangladeshi parents who are neither citizens nor PRs does not qualify under this rule.
- Only other route: child "not born a citizen of any country" (s.1(e)) [K0003]; any citizenship acquired within one year after birth counts as citizenship at birth for this rule (s.2(3)) [K0004].
- Pending change: Act A1752 (2025, not yet in force at the reprint) will make s.1(a) "a citizen" only, i.e. a PR parent will no longer be enough [K0005].
- Citizenship decisions of the Federal Government cannot be appealed or reviewed in court [K0006].

## What can get you jailed: entry, overstay, removal, bans (doc: "what can get you jailed"; "going home without valid papers")

- Entering without a valid Entry Permit or Pass (or s.55 exemption) [K0007]: fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years or both, plus up to 6 strokes of whipping (s.6(3)) [K0008].
- Prohibited immigrants: no means of support or no definite job awaiting [K0009]; unlawful entrants, people without valid or with forged travel documents, family of prohibited immigrants [K0010]. Appeal to the Minister (final) for a permit holder refused entry on arrival [K0011]. The Act sets no money amount for "means of support".
- Overstay: may not remain after the pass expires or is cancelled [K0016]; without reasonable cause: fine **not less than** RM10,000 or up to 5 years or both (s.15(4)) [K0015].
- Removal (deportation): after conviction under s.5/6/8/9 [K0017]; overstayers removed whether or not prosecuted [K0018], with no appeal against removal where the pass expired or was cancelled [K0019]; any non-citizen unlawfully entering or remaining is liable to removal [K0037]. Detention until removal can be arranged [K0020]; arrest without warrant and up to 30 days' detention while removal is decided [K0021]. Failing to produce a pass on demand = presumed unlawful [K0023].
- Bans: the Director General can bar a person from entry for a stated period or permanently [K0012]; when a pass is cancelled the holder is removed and "thereafter" prohibited from entering [K0013]; appeal to the Minister within 7 days by written petition [K0014]. Coming back after removal: fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years or both, up to 6 strokes, and removal again (s.36) [K0022].
- Working in breach of a pass: the pass "shall cease to be a valid Pass" when any of its conditions is broken (s.55B(2)) [K0025]. Employers of workers without a valid pass: RM10,000-50,000 or up to 12 months or both, per worker [K0024]; more than five at once: 6 months-5 years and whipping [K0026]. The worker-side offence for breaching pass conditions is regulation 39 of the Immigration Regulations 1963 (referred to in s.55E(7)) [K0029]; that regulation is not in raw/.
- Landlords/occupiers who let an illegal immigrant (including an overstayer) stay: RM5,000-30,000 or up to 12 months per person [K0029]. Harbouring: RM10,000-50,000 per person [K0033]. Smuggling people in: RM10,000-50,000 and 2-5 years and whipping [K0027].
- Forged papers: making, forging or altering a visa, permit or pass: RM30,000-100,000 **and** 5-10 years **and** up to 6 strokes (s.55D) [K0028]. False statements, using someone else's pass, getting a pass by false statement, using or holding a forged pass: fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years (s.56(1)) [K0030, K0031, K0032]. Attempts and abetment punished like the offence [K0034]. General penalty: up to RM10,000 or 5 years or both [K0035].

## Refugees and asylum (doc: "asylum", written to discourage)

- The Immigration Act (2006 reprint) contains no refugee or asylum provision: 0 matches for "refugee"/"asylum"; every non-citizen needs a valid permit, pass or exemption to enter [K0036]. The Federal Constitution reprint also has 0 matches (noted in K0036).

## Passports (doc: "rights at work" passport withholding; "scams"; "what can get you jailed")

- Holding another person's passport without lawful authority is an offence for any person (s.12(1)(f)): fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years or both [K0045, K0046]. The Act does not mention employers or consent; "lawful authority" is not defined.
- A holder who lets someone else possess their passport intending or knowing it may be used by another person also offends (s.12(1)(e)) [K0047].
- Forged or altered passport, or using/holding one: s.12(1)(a) [K0048]; false statement for one's own passport: s.12(1)(c) [K0049]; both up to RM10,000 or 5 years [K0046]. Getting a passport/visa for someone else by false statement: RM10,000-50,000 and 1-5 years (s.12A) [K0050]. Making a false passport: RM15,000-100,000 and 2-10 years and up to 6 strokes (s.12B) [K0051].
- Text is an unofficial consolidation to 1997 [K0052].

## Drugs and the death penalty (doc: "what can get you jailed")

- Act 846 abolishes the **mandatory** death penalty across seven laws, including the Dangerous Drugs Act 1952 [K0039]; commencement by gazette notification, date not in the file [K0040].
- Drug trafficking (DDA s.39B): Act 846 only lowers the minimum whipping (for those not sentenced to death) from 15 to 12 strokes and deletes s.39B(2A)-(2B); it does not remove death [K0041]. Applies to anyone convicted after commencement even for earlier offences [K0042].
- Murder (Penal Code s.302): death or 30-40 years, plus at least 12 strokes if not sentenced to death [K0043]. "Imprisonment for natural life" replaced by 30-40 years in several laws [K0044].

## Draft steps (for the doc writer)

**Having a child in Malaysia.** 1. Know that a baby born in Malaysia to two Bangladeshi parents who are not Malaysian citizens or PRs is not a Malaysian citizen by birth [K0002]; the stateless-child route does not apply if the child gets another citizenship within a year [K0003, K0004]. 2. Need to know: how to register the birth in Malaysia (JPN) and with the Bangladesh High Commission for Bangladeshi citizenship by descent, and the child's immigration status.

**Staying legal.** 1. Leave before the pass expires: overstaying is a minimum RM10,000 fine or up to 5 years [K0015], and removal with no appeal [K0018, K0019]. 2. Work only within the pass conditions: a broken condition voids the pass [K0025]. 3. Carry the pass or a copy accepted by Immigration: failing to show it means you are presumed illegal [K0023]. 4. Never use, buy or carry forged passes or passports [K0028, K0032, K0048, K0051].

**If an employer keeps your passport.** Keeping another person's passport without lawful authority is an offence under Passports Act s.12(1)(f), up to RM10,000 or 5 years [K0045]. Need to know: where a worker reports this (Immigration, police, Labour Department) and what happens in practice.

**After deportation.** A cancelled pass means a ban "thereafter" [K0013]; the DG may also ban for a stated period or permanently [K0012]; appeal within 7 days to the Minister [K0014]; returning unlawfully is a further offence [K0022].

## Need to know

- Commencement date of Constitution (Amendment) Act 2025 (Act A1752); a newer Constitution reprint or AGC notification [K0005].
- A current (post-2006) consolidated reprint of the Immigration Act 1959/63: the AGC file in raw/ is the 1975 revision, and the IMI copy stops at 1 Jan 2006. Need to check whether any penalty changed after 2006 (e.g. amendments in later years).
- Text of Immigration Regulations 1963 reg. 39 (conditions of passes) and its penalty for the worker; and the length of entry bans in practice (blacklist period after removal or compound), which the Act leaves to the Director General [K0012].
- Commencement date of Act 846 (P.U.(B) notification) [K0040], and the current wording of Dangerous Drugs Act s.39B(2) (the alternative prison term to death) from a current DDA reprint [K0041].
- Whether the Passports Act was amended after 1997; whether "lawful authority" covers a worker's written consent to an employer keeping the passport [K0045, K0052].
- Bangladesh Citizenship Act rules for a child born abroad (registration with the High Commission), which decide whether s.1(e) could ever apply.

# Findings, part L: life and problems in Malaysia on PLKS, and getting home

Researcher L, 6 November 2026. Scope: Malaysian side after arrival, plus the Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur (BDHC KL). Ledger: `ledger-parts/L.csv` (L0001-L0193). Blocked: `blocked-parts/L.md`.

**Access problem that shapes this part.** The Labour Department (JTK, jtksm.mohr.gov.my), Ministry of Human Resources (mohr.gov.my, ssp.mohr.gov.my, minimumwages.mohr.gov.my), PERKESO (perkeso.gov.my), FOMEMA, the Home Ministry (moha.gov.my) and KWSP all time out from here, and Firecrawl failed on every one (tunnel error or timeout). So JTK complaint procedure, the Labour Court, change of employer, PERKESO benefits, FOMEMA fees, SPIKPA, the minimum wage order and the housing act (Act 446) have **no official source saved yet**. They are in `blocked-parts/L.md` for manual download. The Laws of Malaysia site (lom.agc.gov.my) works, but its links are token-protected. I got the Employment (Amendment) Act 2022 (A1651) PDF directly. The consolidated Act 265, Act 446, Act 846 and P.U.(A) 376/2024 still need a browser download.

**Most useful official sources found:**
- IMI foreign worker page (`imi-foreign-worker`, undated): PLKS 12 months, renewal, 10-year cap, i-Kad, runaway reporting, Check-Out Memo.
- IMI PLKS page (`imi-plks`, undated, older): Check-Out Memo documents, runaway cancellation, domestic helper rules. Says 5 years, which conflicts with the 10 years on the foreign worker page.
- IMI posters (Sept 2026): PRM 2.0 runs 1 May 2026 to 31 May 2027, with compounds of RM500/RM500/RM300 plus a RM20 special pass.
- IMI i-Kad poster (Feb 2026).
- The BD-Malaysia MoU 2021 Contract of Employment, hosted by the BDHC KL labour wing. It covers pay by the 7th, the bank account, the 50% deduction cap, housing, no passport retention, who pays the ticket home, and 2 months' notice.
- BDHC KL pages: Travel Permit (fee conflict RM38 vs RM44), NOC for remains, labour wing FAQ (Jan 2025), officers list (Oct 2026).
- Employment (Amendment) Act 2022: 45 hours, 98 days' maternity, paternity leave, s.60K/60KA foreign employees, s.90B forced labour.
- MyGOV pages: leave entitlements and the WFW complaint app.
- CAAM AIP GEN 1.3 (May 2026): drugs and the death penalty, prohibited imports.
- Sarawak JTK licence conditions (May 2025).
- IMI FAQs (2021): Bangladeshi PLKS renewals only at Putrajaya, depots.

---

## 1. After arrival

- **Collection and medical.** The employer must collect the worker within 6 hours of arrival [L0041]. The employer then has 30 days to get a FOMEMA medical at a FOMEMA-registered clinic [L0042]. The PLKS is issued only if FOMEMA certifies the worker fit; an unfit worker is sent home with a Check-Out Memo [L0043]. The older IMI page says all arrival steps, including the PLKS sticker, must be done within one month of entry [L0080].
- **Who pays.** The employer pays the deposit, visa, pass and levy [L0068]. For a Bangladeshi worker the visa is RM 20 and the security bond RM 500 [L0052]. Payments to Immigration are made only after approval, against an official receipt [L0081].
- **FOMEMA itself.** Need to know: the fee, who pays it, and how a worker checks the result. The FOMEMA site was unreachable and its domain is misconfigured. News leads give RM207 for men and RM217 for women after Dec 2023, but these are unverified and conflicting, so they are not in the ledger. A MOHR policy PDF reportedly says the employer pays (blocked).
- **i-Kad.** Every PLKS holder gets an i-Kad (the foreign worker ID card), valid for as long as the PLKS. It is free and grey, and the worker does not apply for it [L0062, L0063, L0126]. The employer collects it at the Immigration office where the PLKS was applied for, 7 days after the notification email [L0127]. The worker must carry it at all times and show it to the authorities on request [L0128]. The High Commission's process also says the employer must ensure an employment visa and i-Kad for every worker [L0035].
- **Checking your pass.** IMI's MyIMMs ePass check (imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/checkEpassValid?semakan) asks for the ePass number, document number and nationality. It shows the name, nationality, pass type and expiry date. The page says it is "for authorities or agencies" [L0129]. Need to know: whether workers can use it and where the ePass number is printed.
- **Passport.** Under the MoU contract the employer may not keep the worker's passport [L0149]. The worker hands it over only for the medical, the PLKS, the i-Kad and renewal, and gets it back afterwards [L0151]. The employer pays if it is lost while in its custody [L0154]. The worker carries the passport, a copy of the PLKS or an ID document [L0150]. IMI says foreigners should carry their passport at all times [L0085]. Workers report employers not returning passports [L0190, unofficial]. Need to know: the Malaysian legal offence for withholding a passport (Passports Act 1966 s.12(1)(f); ATIPSOM). The LOM text is blocked.
- **Bank account.** The MoU contract says wages go into a bank account in the worker's name [L0132]. The High Commission's process says the employer opens the account [L0034]. The Employment Act as amended requires wages to be paid into an account at a financial institution; cash needs the worker's written request plus Director General approval [L0104, L0105]. Need to know: which ID banks accept (passport plus i-Kad?), from BNM or the banks. BNM's basic savings account was for citizens and permanent residents in 2005; that is a lead only, not logged.
- **SIM card.** Need to know. News leads say MCMC Determination No. 1 of 2026 requires non-Malaysians to register with the original passport on a biometric passport reader, with a limit of 2 prepaid SIMs per telco. No official MCMC page was retrieved.
- **Health insurance and SOCSO.** Renewal needs the SPIKPA slip (except plantation) and the SPPA (foreign workers compensation scheme) slip [L0046, L0048]. The contract insures the worker under the PERKESO Employment Injury Scheme and, where applicable, SPIKPA [L0136]. Need to know: SPIKPA cover and premium, and PERKESO registration, contribution and benefits (blocked). The leads say the employer pays 1.25%, and that from 2026 there is an invalidity scheme with 0.5% employer and 0.5% worker. EPF (KWSP) 2% + 2% for foreign workers from October 2025 wages is also a lead; the MOHR PDF is blocked.

## 2. Rights at work

- **Hours.** At most 45 normal hours a week in Peninsular Malaysia [L0108, L0092]. The contract says 8 hours a day [L0133]. Overtime only if the worker agrees, paid under labour law [L0134]. Need to know: the overtime rate (1.5x on normal days, 2x on rest days, 3x on public holidays under s.60/60A/60D) from the Act text (blocked).
- **Rest day.** 1 a week [L0135].
- **Annual leave.** 8, 12 or 16 days by length of service [L0109].
- **Sick leave.** 14, 16 or 22 days by length of service, plus 60 days' hospitalisation leave [L0110].
- **Maternity.** 98 days [L0112, L0093]. A pregnant worker cannot be dismissed except for misconduct, wilful breach or closure [L0094].
- **Paternity.** 7 days [L0095].
- **Public holidays.** Need to know: the number of paid public holidays (the contract only says "per labour law").
- **Minimum wage.** Need to know (official). The contract says the basic wage must meet the Malaysian minimum wage [L0130]. Sarawak requires the minimum wage and the same rate as local workers [L0169]. Leads: RM1,700 under P.U.(A) 376/2024 from 1 Feb 2025 (1 Aug 2025 for employers with fewer than 5 workers), and a rise to RM2,000 from June 2027 announced in Budget 2027 on 9 Oct 2026 [L0188, lead].
- **Pay.** Monthly, by the 7th of the next month [L0131]. Deductions are capped at 50% a month, to recover advances [L0137]. Sarawak bans deductions for recruitment or repatriation costs [L0170]. Need to know: whether levy may be deducted in Peninsular Malaysia. The IMI page makes the employer responsible for the levy [L0068]; the Act's s.24 text is blocked.
- **Housing.** The employer provides reasonable accommodation with basic amenities [L0138]. Need to know: the Act 446 minimum standards (blocked). The employer commitment letter to BDHC KL also promises "good accommodation" (seen in `bdhckl-employer-notice-2023-pdf`, not logged by me).
- **Forced labour.** Under s.90B, an employer who threatens, deceives or forces a worker and stops them leaving the workplace faces a fine of up to RM100,000 or 2 years' jail [L0100, L0101]. The Director General of Labour can decide discrimination disputes [L0102]. A court can order a convicted employer to pay what it owes, by seizing its property [L0103].
- **Contract.** The MoU contract is in Malay, Bangla and English, and English prevails [L0153]. Notice is 2 months on either side [L0144, L0145]. The worker may not change employment or run another business [L0146]. Marriage in Malaysia can lead to the PLKS being revoked [L0148, L0056].

## 3. How long you can stay

- **Per issue.** The PLKS lasts 12 months and is renewed from 3 months before expiry [L0044]. The employer renews it and pays any penalty if it is late [L0139, L0140]. Renewals are filed by the employer only [L0079]. Bangladeshi renewals are done only at Immigration HQ in Putrajaya (2021 FAQ) [L0174]. A renewal filed after expiry is treated as an overstay case [L0045]. Renewal needs a passport valid more than 12 months [L0047], and a FOMEMA medical for the 2nd and 3rd year renewals [L0049].
- **Total.** Up to 10 years in total, or 3 years for workers registered under 6P [L0050]. This **conflicts** with "not more than 5 years" on the older PLKS page [L0066].
- **Age and family.** Age 18-45 at application [L0064]. No family can come [L0053].
- **Permanent residence.** Need to know: no official statement that PLKS never leads to permanent residence was found.

## 4. What can get you jailed

- **Illegal entry or no valid document (s.6(3)).** Fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years' jail or both, and up to 6 strokes of the cane; or a compound of up to RM3,000 [L0083, L0084].
- **Overstay (s.15(4)).** Fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years or both; or a compound of up to RM3,000 [L0086, L0087].
- **Forged visa or pass (s.55D).** At least RM30,000, 5-10 years and up to 6 strokes [L0088].
- **False passport (Passports Act s.12B).** RM15,000-100,000, 2-10 years and up to 6 strokes [L0089].
- **Drugs.** Banned from import and export; "the punishment for drug smuggling is the death penalty" (CAAM, May 2026) [L0155]. Need to know: the current s.39B text after the Abolition of Mandatory Death Penalty Act 2023 (lead: death is now discretionary, with life imprisonment and caning as the alternative).
- **Customs.** Declare all dutiable or prohibited goods [L0156]; false declarations carry stiff penalties [L0157]. Prohibited imports include obscene material on any media, daggers and flick knives, and cloth printed with Quran verses [L0158-L0160]. Need to know: an official warning about carrying parcels for others (none found on a Malaysian site).
- **Using an agent for Immigration matters** is an offence (employer side) [L0175].

## 5. If you aren't paid, or a dispute

- **Official route found.** The Working for Workers (WFW) app from the Ministry of Human Resources. It is for all workers, local or foreign, and takes complaints about unpaid wages, unfair conditions or any Employment Act breach, 24/7, without visiting an office [L0163-L0165]. It keeps the complaint confidential and lets the worker track its status [L0166]. It is on Android and iOS; the portal is workforworkers.com.my [L0167].
- **Labour Court.** Need to know: the Labour Court claim under s.69 (where to file, documents, statutory declaration, fee, time limit, how long it takes), the forced labour complaint line, and Industrial Relations Department unfair-dismissal representations. All are on JTK or MOHR pages that are blocked. The 2022 amendment raised the fine for not obeying a Labour Court order to RM50,000 [L0106].
- **BD High Commission KL labour wing.** For any labour welfare problem, email ss.lab.kl@gmail.com, fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com or counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd [L0021]. The labour officers listed in Oct 2026 are:
  - Minister (Labour) Md. Siddiqur Rahman, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd, +603 2604 0934 [L0029].
  - Minister (Labour) Syed Shariful Islam, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, +601 2290 3252 [L0028].
  - Counsellor (Labour), +603 2604 0929 [L0030].

  The mission is at No 8 Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 KL, PABX 603-2604-0946/48/49 [L0032, L0033]. For injury compensation, a working worker files a case at the labour office with payslips, the contract and attendance records, and gives the High Commission a copy [L0037].
- **What happens in practice.** In 2023 the mission took a case to the Human Resources Ministry, which summoned the employer [L0193, unofficial]. Workers report employers' agents warning them not to complain [L0192, unofficial]. Activists estimate 100,000-200,000 Bangladeshis in Malaysia are jobless or unpaid [L0191, unofficial].
- **Sabah and Sarawak.** Their worker applications fall under state jurisdiction [L0065]. Sarawak uses its own Labour Ordinance (Cap. 76) licence for non-resident workers [L0168]. That licence requires the minimum wage and pay equal to locals, bans recruitment or repatriation deductions, requires PERKESO, makes the licence holder responsible for repatriation, and requires a police report if a worker absconds [L0169-L0173]. Need to know: Sabah (the JTK Sabah site is unreachable).

## 6. Changing employer

- **Status.** PLKS holders may not change employer or sector [L0055]. The older IMI page says it is possible with Home Ministry (KDN) permission [L0067, L0176]. Outsourcing companies may move workers only within the same sector [L0082]. The contract also bars changing jobs [L0146]. For domestic helpers, Immigration can move an abused helper to a new employer without the first employer's consent [L0078].
- **Need to know.** The JTK/BPPA "Pertukaran Majikan" (PTM) process. A lead from a search snippet of the BPPA form dated 7 Jun 2024 says it is allowed when the employer is bankrupt or has closed; not for workers in their 10th year or later; the PLKS must have at least 3 months left; a JTK support letter comes first; and the original employer files it. The form is blocked; no ledger row (no saved raw).

## 7. Reported absent (lari) or pass cancelled

- **What counts.** Leaving the workplace without telling the employer, or not returning on the agreed date after going home, counts as "lari" [L0057, L0058].
- **What the employer does.** It reports at Immigration with its ID, a copy of the passport, the runaway form and a police report [L0059]. To cancel the PLKS it brings the worker's original passport and a certified police report [L0075]. It must also tell the Director General of Labour within 14 days (s.60KA) [L0099]. In Sarawak it must report to the police [L0173].
- **What happens to the worker.** Workers reported as lari are blacklisted [L0060, L0178]. The employer's bond is forfeited [L0061]. Prohibited immigrants include people removed from any country whom the Director General deems undesirable [L0091].
- **Need to know.** How a worker checks whether he has been reported (the ePass check [L0129] may show the pass status), and the official remedy for a false report. The labour route is WFW [L0163]; Immigration appeal procedure not found.

## 8. Leaving at the end of the job

- **Check-Out Memo.** The employer applies for the Check-Out Memo (Memo Periksa Keluar) with the form, the worker's original passport and a ticket [L0071, L0072]. The worker leaves on the date and through the exit point in the memo [L0073]. Without a memo the employer stays responsible for the worker [L0074]. Workers must be sent home when the job ends, after dismissal, when the pass expires or is cancelled, or when they are found unfit [L0069]. The bond is refunded only after a proper exit with the memo [L0051]. The employer tells JTK within 30 days of termination (s.60KA) [L0098].
- **Ticket home.** The employer pays the fare home on completion of the contract [L0141]. It also pays when it ends the contract (except for the worker's breach), when the worker quits because the employer broke the contract, and after serious work injury or illness [L0142]. Otherwise the worker pays [L0143].
- **Wages owed.** On early repatriation the employer pays all outstanding wages [L0152].
- **Leaving the country.** You need a passport valid at least 6 months and a valid pass [L0186, L0187].
- **Lost passport.** Report to Immigration Enforcement with the police report, the new passport and a ticket within 7 days; the Special Pass costs RM100 [L0182-L0185, L0177].

## 9. Going home on leave

- Not returning on the agreed date counts as running away [L0058].
- **Need to know.** Whether the PLKS sticker allows re-entry, and whether any permit or employer letter is needed. No official IMI text on PLKS re-entry was found. The leave entitlement is annual leave [L0109].

## 10. Undocumented workers going home

- **Programme Repatriasi Migran 2.0 (PRM 2.0).** Runs 1 May 2026 to 31 May 2027. Walk-in at one of 14 Immigration Enforcement offices in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan; hotline 03-8880 1269 [L0113-L0118]. Bring a valid passport or travel document and a ticket home for a date within 14 days [L0116, L0117]. Compounds are RM500 for no valid pass, RM500 for overstay and RM300 for breaking pass conditions, plus RM20 for each special pass [L0120-L0123]. Pay by debit or credit card or Touch 'n Go eWallet only [L0119]. Children under 18 pay no compound but need the RM20 pass [L0124]. The earlier phase ended 30 April 2026 [L0125]. News adds that participants are exempt from prosecution subject to conditions [L0189, lead]. Need to know: whether Sabah and Sarawak are covered (the poster says Peninsula and Labuan) and the exclusions (news: PRM 1.0 no-shows, blacklisted people, people with warrants).
- **Order of steps per the High Commission.** First pay Immigration's fine and get a Special Pass, then buy a ticket and leave on time. Without a valid passport, get a Travel Permit from the High Commission first [L0013].
- **Travel Permit (out pass).**
  - **Who can apply:** Bangladeshis whose passport has expired, been lost or damaged [L0001].
  - **What to bring:** apply in person, with a copy of your past passport and a copy of your NID or online birth registration certificate [L0003-L0005]. The older pages add the form, 3 photos and an interview. Instead of a passport copy, a letter from the UNO (or from the DC for Cox's Bazar residents), emailed to the labour wing, is accepted [L0009, L0010, L0014, L0015].
  - **Validity:** 3 months [L0002].
  - **Fee:** **conflict**. RM 38 (bdhckl.gov.bd, Aug 2026; and the Jan 2026 fee chart) [L0006] against RM 44 at a Maybank counter (2023 page and form) [L0008].
  - **Processing time:** **conflict**. 1-2 working days [L0012] against same day after 4 pm [L0016].
  - **Payment:** by card at the counter, or a Maybank deposit to account 564427102268 [L0007].
- **In detention.** Detainees with a passport only need a ticket, and the depot handles the rest. For detainees without a passport, the camp sends the TP application and the High Commission issues the TP to the camp, with no government fee [L0017, L0018]. There are 13 depots, 11 in the Peninsula and 2 in Sarawak [L0181]. To visit a detainee you need an Immigration permission letter; the detainee's body number or police report number helps find them [L0179, L0180].
- **Bans.** Need to know: the official ban length after PRM or deportation. "Prohibited immigrant" status applies to people removed and deemed undesirable [L0091].
- **Program Rekalibrasi (RTK 2.0).** The IMI FAQ page (2023) is empty. Need to know: its end date. It is believed closed; no official status found.

## 11. If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

- **NOC to send a body home (High Commission).** For a documented worker:
  - the passport;
  - the receiver's details in Bangladesh;
  - a police report and NOC for abnormal deaths;
  - the original death certificate;
  - the funeral company's letter;
  - the employer's letter;
  - a copy of the employer's PERKESO accident/illness notice for foreign workers (form PA-F101R1) [L0023-L0025].

  For an undocumented worker: the passport or a police report that it was lost, the receiver's details, a police report and NOC, the death certificate, the funeral company's letter, and the employer's name and contact [L0026, L0027].
- **Sending the body home (labour wing page, 2024).** The company writes to the High Commission and appoints a casket company. Papers go to the High Commission: the company letter, insurance claim, compensation letter, hospital letter, death certificate, police report and passport. The family sends a claim/consent letter naming the receiver. Inform the Probashi Kallyan desk at the airport [L0038, L0039]. The Welfare Board pays for the body to be sent home if no employer is found [L0040]. Under the contract the employer pays repatriation after serious work injury or illness [L0142]. Need to know: who pays to send a body home under Malaysian rules (the PERKESO funeral benefit reportedly includes repatriation; blocked).
- **Compensation.** The employer notifies the insurer, and the insurer and Labour Department investigate. For a death, Labour Department Form H is sent to the heirs in Bangladesh, completed and returned through the High Commission [L0036]. Need to know: PERKESO Employment Injury Scheme benefits, dependants' benefit, how to claim and time limits (blocked). The Welfare Board benefits listed by the High Commission: Tk 1 lakh for treatment, Tk 35,000 at the airport, a Tk 3 lakh grant, and recovery of compensation and unpaid wages [L0020]. Joining the Welfare Board from Malaysia costs RM 155 [L0019]. This overlaps with the Bangladesh-side part.

## 12. Domestic workers

Bangladesh is **not** an approved source country for foreign domestic helpers (PRA). The approved countries are Indonesia, Thailand, Cambodia, the Philippines, Sri Lanka, India, Vietnam and Laos on the PLKS page; the PRA page also lists Nepal and still not Bangladesh [L0076, L0194]. Helpers must be women aged 21-45 [L0077].

## 13. Practical pass (leads only)

- **Daily Star, 3 Apr 2024.** Johor company workers got no job, no permit and no passport back, and received RM200 for food over seven months after Tk 5 lakh in loans [L0190]. Activists estimate 100k-200k jobless or unpaid [L0191]. Agents warn workers not to complain [L0192]. (The raw file name says 2026, but the article is dated 2024.)
- **TBS, 2023.** The High Commission escalated a case to the Human Resources Ministry, which summoned the employer [L0193].
- **Search leads not yet fetched.** Observer BD (Jul-Aug 2026: Aerofoam, about 109 workers with 3 months unpaid, complained to JTK and the High Commission); Arab News/Dhaka Tribune (Nov 2025: Kawaguchi/Mediceram protests); Malaysiakini (UN experts on debt bondage); CNA (May 2025: Malaysia to take 7,964 of over 17,000 stranded workers).

---

## Draft steps

### After arrival
1. **At KLIA, wait for your employer.** It must collect you within 6 hours [L0041]. The High Commission expects the employer to take you to the hostel [L0034].
2. **Do the FOMEMA medical within 30 days** at a clinic the employer takes you to. The employer pays the Immigration fees, levy and bond (Bangladesh: visa RM20, bond RM500) [L0042, L0068, L0052]. If you fail, you are sent home with a Check-Out Memo [L0043]. Need to know: the FOMEMA fee, who pays it, and how to see your result.
3. **Get your PLKS sticker and i-Kad.** The employer collects the i-Kad free, 7 days after Immigration's email. Carry it always [L0126-L0128].
4. **Get your passport back.** The employer may hold it only for the medical, the PLKS, the i-Kad and renewal [L0149, L0151]. If it is kept, complain on WFW [L0163]. Need to know: the Malaysian offence for withholding a passport.
5. **Make sure wages go into a bank account in your name, by the 7th of each month** [L0131, L0132, L0104]. Need to know: the ID banks accept, and how to get a SIM.
6. **Check you are covered** by PERKESO's Employment Injury Scheme and SPIKPA [L0136, L0046]. Need to know: the benefits and how to check.

### If you aren't paid
1. **Complain on the WFW app** (Android or iOS, or workforworkers.com.my): it takes unpaid-wage complaints from foreign workers, is confidential, and lets you track the case [L0163-L0167].
2. **Email the High Commission labour wing** (counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, ss.lab.kl@gmail.com, fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com) or call +601 2290 3252 / +603 2604 0934 [L0021, L0028, L0029].
3. **Keep payslips, the contract and attendance records** [L0037].
4. Need to know: the Labour Court (s.69) filing steps, time limit and cost.

### End of job or going home
1. **The employer applies for the Check-Out Memo** with your passport and ticket. Leave on the date and through the exit point on the memo [L0071-L0073].
2. **The employer pays your ticket** at the end of the contract, if it ends the contract, if you leave because it broke the contract, or after serious work injury [L0141, L0142]. If you resign, give 2 months' notice and pay your own ticket [L0145].
3. **Collect all unpaid wages before you leave** [L0152].

### Undocumented
1. **If you have no valid passport, get a Travel Permit** at the High Commission:
   - Go in person with a copy of your old passport and your NID or online birth certificate. Without these, a UNO letter emailed to the labour wing.
   - Bring photos.
   - Pay RM38 (or RM44, see conflict) by card or by Maybank deposit to account 564427102268.
   - It is valid 3 months [L0001-L0007, L0014, L0015].
2. **Walk in to an Immigration Enforcement office** (14 in the Peninsula and Labuan) under PRM 2.0 before 31 May 2027:
   - Bring your passport or TP and a ticket for a date within 14 days.
   - Pay the RM500 or RM300 compound plus RM20 by card or Touch 'n Go.
   - Hotline 03-8880 1269 [L0113-L0124].
3. **If detained,** the depot arranges the flight. The High Commission issues a free TP to the camp [L0017, L0018].

### Death
1. **The employer writes to the High Commission and reports the death to PERKESO (form PA-F101R1)** [L0023, L0024, L0038].
2. **The family sends a consent letter** naming who will receive the body in Bangladesh [L0039].
3. **The High Commission issues the NOC** once it has the passport, death certificate, police report and NOC (for abnormal deaths), funeral company letter and employer letter [L0023-L0027].
4. **For compensation, the heirs fill Form H** and return it through the High Commission [L0036].

## Need to know (open gaps)
- **FOMEMA.** Fee, who pays, how a worker checks the result, appeal (FOMEMA site unreachable; news only).
- **SPIKPA.** Cover and premium.
- **PERKESO.** Contribution, Employment Injury benefits, dependants' benefit, funeral and repatriation benefit, how to claim, time limits (perkeso.gov.my blocked).
- **EPF (KWSP).** 2% + 2% from Oct 2025 (lead; MOHR PDF blocked).
- **Minimum wage.** RM1,700 under P.U.(A) 376/2024 (lead), whether it covers foreign workers, and the June 2027 RM2,000 rise (lead [L0188]).
- **Employment Act details.** Overtime rates, number of public holidays, deductions rule (s.24), pay deadline in the Act (s.19), termination benefits (Act 265 consolidated text blocked).
- **Housing.** Act 446 standards (blocked).
- **Passport withholding.** The offence (Passports Act s.12; ATIPSOM) (LOM blocked).
- **Bank account and SIM.** The ID needed (BNM and MCMC official pages not found).
- **Labour Court.** s.69 procedure, cost, time limit; JTK forced labour line; IRD unfair dismissal (60-day limit?); JTK Sabah.
- **Change of employer.** PTM conditions and process (BPPA form and JTK process blocked).
- **Reported absent.** How a worker checks a lari report or blacklist, and how to challenge a false one.
- **Leave.** PLKS re-entry rules for going home on leave.
- **PRM 2.0.** Exclusions, Sabah and Sarawak coverage, prosecution exemption; ban lengths after PRM or deportation.
- **RTK 2.0.** End date and status.
- **No permanent residence.** No official statement found.
- **Drugs.** s.39B after Act 846. Also: an official warning on carrying others' parcels.
- **Currency.** The AIP's RM1,000 ringgit limit [L0161] may be stale; check against BNM's foreign exchange policy.
- **High Commission address.** Older letterhead PDFs (Jan 2025 FAQ, June 2023 notice) show No. 5B & 5C Jalan Sultan Yahya Petra, 54100. The current website (Apr 2025) and contact page show No 8 Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450. Use No 8; confirm.

## Conflicts
- **Travel Permit fee.** L0006 (RM 38, 2026) vs L0008 (RM 44, 2023 page and form).
- **Travel Permit processing.** L0012 (1-2 working days, 2023) vs L0016 (same day, Jan 2025 FAQ).
- **PLKS maximum total.** L0050 (10 years; 6P 3 years) vs L0066 (5 years).

## Blocked
See `blocked-parts/L.md` (JTK, MOHR/BPPA, the minimum wage order, LOM acts, PERKESO, FOMEMA, SPIKPA).


# Part M: rights at work, wages, claims, change of employer, housing, social security (PLKS)

Researcher M, 6 Nov 2026. Sources: 13 official files already in raw/ (all read in full). 243 ledger rows (M0001-M0243): 21 verified, 222 possibly-outdated. 53 facts rows (visa PLKS).
