Note: the JTK FAQ PDF copy came from Studocu (a third-party document-sharing site), not from jtksm.mohr.gov.my; its content matches the official HTML FAQ, plus worked examples. It says it is "for public use, not a legal authority" [M0243].

## Answers to the open "Need to know" items

**Overtime rates and public holidays (doc step "Hours").** Overtime beyond normal daily hours: at least 1.5x the hourly rate [M0001, M0002]. Rest day: daily-rated get 1 day's wages (up to half normal hours) or 2 days' (more) [M0011]; monthly-rated get half a day's or 1 day's pay [M0012]; beyond normal hours on a rest day at least 2x hourly [M0013]; no one can be forced to work a rest day except shift work or emergencies [M0010]. Public holiday worked: 2 days' wages on top of holiday pay [M0019]; overtime on a public holiday at least 3x hourly [M0020]. Paid public holidays: 11 gazetted a year, 5 fixed (National Day, King's Birthday, state Ruler's Birthday or FT Day, Workers' Day, Malaysia Day) [M0014, M0015]; substitute day if it falls on a rest day [M0016]; employer posts the other six [M0017]; absence the day before/after loses holiday pay without a reasonable excuse [M0018]. Hourly rate = (monthly wage / 26) / normal daily hours [M0007, M0008, M0234, M0235]. Limits: 8 h/day, 45 h/week, 30-min break after 5 h [M0003]; never more than 12 h a day [M0004]; 45 h excludes free breaks [M0237]; wages can't be cut for the 48-to-45 change [M0061]. Workers earning over RM4,000 (except manual workers) are excluded from OT/rest-day/holiday pay and termination benefits [M0058, M0109]. Still not answered: the monthly overtime cap (set by regulations, not in the Act) [M0005].

**Leave.** Annual 8/12/16 days [M0021, M0022]; sick 14/18/22 + 60 hospitalisation (74-82 total) [M0023, M0024, M0025, M0239]; maternity 98 days [M0026]; paternity 7 days [M0027]; pregnant worker protection [M0240].

**How and when wages are paid (s.19, s.25).** Wage period max 1 month [M0028]; pay by the 7th day after it ends [M0029]; OT, rest-day and holiday pay by the last day of the next wage period [M0030]; into a bank account in the worker's own name [M0034, M0035]; cash/cheque only on the worker's written request plus JTKSM approval [M0036, M0037]; on termination with notice, pay by the last day [M0032]; if the worker quits without notice (s.13 or s.14(3)), within 3 days [M0033]; employer can't dictate how wages are spent [M0038]; late pay is an offence [M0040] and a breach of contract by the employer [M0041]; partial-month formula [M0043].

**Deductions (s.24); levy and recruitment costs.** Only deductions the Act allows [M0044]: overpayment, notice indemnity, interest-free advances, deductions authorised by other written law [M0045, M0046]; third-party payments, insurance and accommodation/food need the worker's written request plus the Director General's prior written permission [M0108, M0047, M0048], and accommodation/food only if for the worker's benefit [M0049]; max 50% of monthly wages [M0050, M0051]; unauthorised deduction is an offence [M0040]. Levy/recruitment costs: none of these files names them; the Act's closed list does not include them. **Still not answered:** an explicit official statement that the levy or recruitment fees may not be deducted (look in JTKSM/KESUMA levy circulars, the "Employer Mandatory Commitment" for levy, or the Minimum Standards of Housing regulations 2020 for rent caps).

**Termination and lay-off benefits.** Notice 4/6/8 weeks or as in the contract [M0052]; pay in lieu [M0053]; misconduct dismissal only after due inquiry [M0054]; worker can leave without notice if in danger [M0055]; foreign workers retrenched before locals in a similar job [M0057]; locals can't be replaced by foreigners [M0069]; untaken annual leave paid out [M0022]; return ticket: employer pays at contract end or when it terminates, worker pays if dismissed for misconduct or resigns [M0149, M0150]. **Still not answered:** the amounts of termination/lay-off benefits: s.60J leaves them to the Employment (Termination and Lay-Off Benefits) Regulations 1980, not in these files [M0056].

**Labour Court claim.** Where: JTKSM (Labour Department) office; the Director General of Labour hears wage/payment disputes without upper limit [M0081, M0082]. Free advice: 03-8000 8000, jtksm@mohr.gov.my, nearest Labour Office [M0118]. How: go in person and give a sworn statement [M0087, M0088, M0112]; bring passport, employer name/address, payslips, appointment letter/contract, termination letter [M0113]. Fee: free [M0232]; no fee for summonses [M0093]; free copy of decision [M0095]; the Act allows filing fees by regulation (none named) [M0094]. Time limits: 60 days to challenge a misconduct dismissal [M0083, M0117]; 90 days for a claim against a principal contractor [M0100]; general wage-claim limit not in these files. Steps: sworn statement, mention date, summons, hearing, order [M0114]; employer summoned, worker brings witnesses [M0089]; evidence on oath, decision [M0090]; decided in the employer's absence if he doesn't come [M0091]; joint claims allowed [M0092]; employer may not stop the worker appearing [M0099]. Order: 8% interest from day 31 [M0085]; non-compliance an offence [M0086]; enforced through the Sessions/Magistrate's Court [M0096]; seizure and sale, garnishment, prosecution, judgment-debtor summons [M0116]; third-party payment order [M0097]; appeal to the High Court [M0098] within 14 days [M0115]; employer about to flee: complain to a Magistrate [M0110]; DG can represent the worker in civil court [M0101]; civil suit as an alternative [M0102]. Separate route: written complaint (aduan) to the Labour Office, leading to inspection, rectification notice, then prosecution/compound [M0119, M0080, M0079]. **Still not answered:** how long a case takes; the general time limit for wage claims; the current filing fee regulations (brochure is 2017).

**Forced labour.** Offence, fine up to RM100,000 and/or 2 years [M0073]; definition and three elements [M0074, M0075]; employers convicted can't get approval to hire foreign workers [M0063]; complain to JTKSM (complaint powers s.79) [M0080]. Discrimination complaints [M0076, M0077, M0236]; sexual harassment [M0078]. **Still not answered:** a specific forced-labour hotline or form (none in these files).

**Unfair dismissal (Industrial Relations).** These files only show that the Labour Court can't hear a claim pending under the Industrial Relations Act 1967 [M0103] and the s.69(3) misconduct route (60 days; remedy limited to pay in lieu, not reinstatement) [M0083, M0084]. **Still not answered:** the IRA s.20 representation to the Industrial Relations Department (JPPM): time limit, form, and whether PLKS workers use it. The IRA is not in these files.

**Change of employer (PLKS).** Allowed only if the employer is bankrupt, closed, hit by calamity, restructured, renamed, or died [M0123, M0124]; same sector, not frozen [M0126]; not for 6P/PKPP/Rehiring/RTK, domestic helpers, non-PLKS, or PLKS 10th year+ [M0127]; PLKS valid 3+ months [M0130, M0131]. Who applies: the employers, not the worker [M0132, M0125]: JTKSM step on ePPAx (both employers register, old employer applies, new employer uploads in 7 days, JTK branch investigates, JTKSM decides) [M0121, M0122], then JTKSM support letter [M0128, M0140], then BPPA KESUMA counter in Putrajaya, Mon-Thu 8.30-4.30, queue 8.30-12 [M0134, M0135], interview with originals, 2 copies [M0136]. Documents: worker passport front page + active PLKS sticker, workers' list [M0139]; EPF/PERKESO statements [M0137]; statutory declarations [M0138]; stamped release agreement [M0125, M0141]. Approval depends on compliance and quota [M0129]. Fee: form is marked free; no fee stated [M0142]. **Still not answered:** processing time; any fee at Immigration to transfer the pass; any route for a worker whose employer is abusive (not a listed ground).

**Absconding.** Definition [M0213, M0214]; employer reports to Immigration with police report and form [M0215]; worker blacklisted and bond confiscated [M0216]; employer must also tell JTKSM within 14 days [M0066, M0067, M0241]. **Still not answered:** how a worker challenges a false absconding report or gets off the blacklist (nothing in these files).

**Passport retention.** MOHR policy: employers must not hold or keep the worker's passport, per the Passports Act 1966 [M0143].

**Housing (Act 446).** Applies in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan [M0191]; Part IIIA for non-estate workers [M0192]; Certificate for Accommodation required [M0193]; employer notifies JTKSM within 30 days [M0194]; minimum standards apply [M0195] but are set in regulations [M0196]; unfit buildings banned [M0199]; decent amenities [M0200]; separate men/women, fire and wiring safety, medical help [M0201, M0202]; person in charge inspects twice a month, takes sick workers to clinic [M0203, M0204]; stay up to 4 weeks after short-notice termination [M0205]; no obligation for dependants [M0206]; estate-type housing (Part II): free water and electricity, no rent [M0207, M0208, M0209]. Who pays: employer provides housing [M0146]; rent may be deducted under Ministerial conditions [M0197, M0198] and, under the Employment Act, only with the worker's written request and JTKSM permission [M0048, M0049]. Complaints: JTKSM (DG of Labour) enforces and can inspect without notice [M0212, M0210, M0079]; penalties [M0211]. **Still not answered:** space per worker, beds, toilets, water per person, maximum rent: in the Employees' Minimum Standards of Housing, Accommodations and Amenities (Accommodation and Centralized Accommodation) Regulations 2020, not in these files.

**EPF (KWSP).** Proposed 2% worker + 2% employer, MOHR statement 3 Feb 2025 [M0163]; a bill to amend the EPF Act was expected [M0164]; rate held until review [M0165]. **Still not answered:** whether it is now law and the start date (search KWSP site, EPF (Amendment) Act 2025, Gazette).

**PERKESO / SOCSO.** EI Scheme since 1 Jan 2019 and Invalidity Scheme since 1 Jul 2024, covering foreign workers incl. domestic workers [M0166, M0167, M0168, M0169, M0176, M0190]. Contributions: employer 1.25% EI + 0.5% invalidity = 1.75%; worker 0.5% [M0170, M0171]; older EI-only rate 1.25% employer-only [M0180]; due by the 15th [M0181]; wages counted [M0185]. Eligibility and registration: valid passport and work pass; ASSIST portal or PERKESO office [M0173, M0182, M0183]; 12-digit FWSS number [M0184]; employer offence up to RM10,000/2 years [M0174, M0175]. Benefits: [M0172, M0186]; funeral RM6,500 if repatriated or RM2,000 if buried in Malaysia (2019) [M0187]; non-work death repatriation reimbursed up to RM4,500 (2022) [M0177, M0178]. Claims: Foreign Worker Claim Notification Form (Appendix C, Circular 3/2018) [M0188]; contacts 1-300-22-8000, perkeso@perkeso.gov.my [M0179, M0189]. Employer also pays funeral and repatriation of a deceased worker under MOHR policy [M0148]. **Still not answered:** benefit rates for temporary/permanent disablement, dependants' and invalidity pension amounts; claim time limits; who files the claim if the employer refuses; whether the RM6,500/RM2,000/RM4,500 amounts are current.

**FOMEMA, SPIKPA, insurance.** FOMEMA within 30 days / 1 month, PLKS only after fit, COM if unfit [M0223, M0144]; employer pays FOMEMA [M0144]; FOMEMA again for 2nd and 3rd-year renewals [M0221]; SPIKPA hospital insurance needed for renewal except plantation [M0220]; employer provides insurance under Act 4 [M0145]; biomedical screening before entry [M0160]; sick or infectious workers sent home [M0162]. **Still not answered:** SPIKPA premium and cover limits.

**Minimum wage.** Only that employers must follow minimum wage rules under Act 732 [M0153], basic wage equal to locals in the same job [M0152], and that the JTKSM brochure handles minimum-wage complaints [M0120]. **Still not answered:** the amount (not in these files).

**PLKS re-entry when going home on leave.** Only that not returning on the agreed date after a trip home counts as absconding [M0214]. **Still not answered:** re-entry permit/procedure.

## IMI English page (browser copy) vs raw/imi-foreign-worker.md (Malay)

New or different in the English browser copy: levy table by sector (manufacturing/construction/services RM1,850; plantation RM640/RM590; agriculture RM640/RM410; services Sabah/Sarawak RM1,490; VP(TE) RM60; process RM125) [M0226, M0227] (the Malay copy shows only "[TABLE]"); Bangladesh visa RM20 and bond RM500 [M0228]; Phase 1 text is different (English says the worker enters with VDR and clearance within 6 hours, Phase 2 says 24 hours; the Malay page says 6 hours) [M0224, M0225]; late extension goes to the Enforcement Division (Malay: overstay consideration) [M0219]; extension needs a "Slip of FWCS" (outdated; PERKESO replaced FWCS from 2019 [M0176]); i-Kad sent to the employer by an authorised vendor (Malay: collected at the JIM branch; grey for all sectors; English: 6 colours by sector) [M0230]; "Allowed to work in all sectors" (Malay adds "except frozen sectors") [M0231].

## Draft steps (PLKS, rights at work and problems)

1. **Know your pay and hours.** Max 8 h/day, 45 h/week, 12 h with overtime [M0003, M0004]. Overtime 1.5x, rest day 2x beyond normal hours, public holiday 2 days' pay + 3x overtime [M0001, M0013, M0019, M0020]. 11 paid public holidays [M0014]. Leave [M0021, M0023, M0239].
2. **Check your wage.** Paid by the 7th into your own bank account [M0029, M0034]; overtime by the end of the next month [M0030]; deductions only as allowed, max half [M0044, M0050]; rent only with your written request and JTKSM approval [M0048].
3. **Keep your passport.** The employer must not hold it [M0143].
4. **Housing.** Must have a Certificate for Accommodation; men and women separate; sick workers taken to clinic [M0193, M0201, M0204]; complain to JTKSM [M0212].
5. **If not paid:** call 03-8000 8000 or go to the nearest Labour Office with your passport, payslips, contract and any termination letter; give a sworn statement; attend the mention and hearing with witnesses; the order carries interest and can be enforced by court; appeal within 14 days [M0118, M0113, M0112, M0114, M0089, M0085, M0096, M0115]. It's free [M0232].
6. **If dismissed for misconduct:** complain to JTKSM within 60 days [M0083].
7. **If forced to work or locked in:** it's a crime under s.90B [M0073, M0074].
8. **Changing employer:** only in the listed cases, applied for by the employers through JTKSM ePPAx and BPPA KESUMA [M0123, M0121, M0128, M0134].
9. **If reported as absconded:** see consequences [M0216]; route to fix: Need to know.
10. **Injury, illness, death:** PERKESO covers you; employer registers you and you get an FWSS number; claim with the Foreign Worker Claim Notification Form [M0166, M0184, M0188]; employer pays funeral and repatriation [M0148].

## Need to know (with what was searched)

All searched only within the 13 assigned files (task scope); no web search done.
- Overtime monthly cap (Employment (Limitation of Overtime Work) Regulations) [M0005].
- Termination/lay-off benefit amounts (Termination and Lay-Off Benefits Regulations 1980) [M0056].
- Labour Court: general time limit, current filing fee rules, duration of a case.
- Industrial Relations Act s.20 unfair-dismissal route (JPPM).
- Whether levy or recruitment costs may be deducted (no explicit rule in these files).
- Change of employer: processing time; any Immigration fee; route for abused workers.
- False absconding report: how to clear it / get off the blacklist.
- Act 446 detailed standards (2020 Regulations): space, beds, toilets, water, maximum rent.
- EPF for foreign workers: current law and start date.
- PERKESO benefit amounts, claim time limits, current funeral amounts.
- SPIKPA premium/cover; minimum wage amount; PLKS re-entry procedure.

## Conflicts

- Clearance after arrival: 6 hours [M0225] vs 24 hours [M0224] on the same IMI English page (the Malay page says 6 hours).
- PERKESO domestic workers: 2019 FAQ excluded them [M0190]; Feb 2026 flyer includes them [M0166] (newer one is the rule).
- PERKESO contribution: 1.25% employer-only (2019 FAQ, 2022 brochure) [M0180, M0176] vs employer 1.75% + worker 0.5% after the Invalidity Scheme (Feb 2026 flyer) [M0170, M0171] (newer one is the rule).
- IMI renewal checklist still asks for the FWCS slip [M0221], though FWCS moved to PERKESO from 2019 [M0176].
- JTKSM brochure (2017) cites s.69B (RM2,000-5,000 wage band), deleted by Act A1651 from 1 Jan 2023; not ledgered for that reason.

## Blocked

None. jtksm-masalah-di-tempat-kerja images are decorative shapes (checked by extracting them); all text is in the text layer, but its columns are interleaved, so 10 brochure rows (M0112-M0120, M0232) use --no-quote-check with the quote rebuilt from the column lines (page noted). Same for 1 PTM flowchart row and 1 PERKESO flyer row.


# Part N: non-work visas, practical pass (news, NGO, agency guides)

Accessed 6 Nov 2026. 58 ledger rows (N0001-N0058): 47 unofficial, 1 conflict (news vs official), 7 verified, 3 possibly-outdated. 10 rows are official (MHTC, UNHCR, the Malaysian High Commission in New Delhi). 39 new sources registered. Every news row is practice context, labelled "unofficial, not verified". None of them is the basis of a fee, eligibility rule, document, processing time or legal rule.

## Tourist visa (social visit): Step 2, Apply on MyVISA

**MyVISA outages.** From 10 Feb 2026 the High Commission of Malaysia in Dhaka said MyVISA would be unavailable "until further notice". All new applications and approvals stopped, with no end date given (unofficial: N0001, N0003, N0004; same event as V0106, V0107). Applicants were told to email "the service provider" for urgent help, but the address is hidden on the saved page (N0002). This suggests HC Dhaka still uses an outsourced provider.

The Malaysian mission in New Delhi posted official notices of a further global MyVISA outage and of its restoration on 23 Jul 2026 (verified: N0005, N0006; notice bodies are images, transcribed in the notes). The doc can say that MyVISA sometimes goes offline for maintenance and that restorations are announced on Malaysian mission pages [N0005, N0006].

**Need to know:** the date MyVISA reopened for Bangladeshi applicants after the 10 Feb 2026 pause. I searched English and Bangla news, kln.gov.my/web/bgd_dhaka news and posts, and IMI announcements. Nothing found; the HC Dhaka Facebook page is blocked. MyVISA was clearly working again by Sep 2026 (the MEV change, V0108).

**Apply yourself.** In Dec 2025 the Malaysian High Commissioner was asked about Bangladeshis turned back at the airport. He urged people to apply directly on the official eVISA portal and said its link is posted on the HC's Facebook page (unofficial: N0054). This supports the official V0005-V0007.

**Agencies in Dhaka and their fees.** History only (pre-MyVISA):
- 2015: HC Dhaka used a private processor, Visa Luar Negara (VLN), through 24 nominated travel agents. Applicants paid a Tk 3,100 bank draft against a Tk 600 sticker fee (N0021, N0022 (2015)).
- 2016: applicants paid Tk 5,400 with no receipt for Tk 4,800 of it (N0023 (2016)).
- 2023: MACC (the Malaysian Anti-Corruption Commission) arrested an HC Dhaka enforcement officer for approving visas through the VLN system for US$300-500 bribes each since 2021. Tourist visas were reportedly sold to people actually going to work (N0024, N0025 (2023)).
- Current: a May 2026 travel blog puts agents' charges for an eVISA at Tk 5,000-8,000, against the official RM20 visa fee + RM105 processing charge (V0016, V0017). Low reliability: the same blog lists eNTRI, which is India-only (N0056).

**Need to know:** the current official list of authorised agencies or service providers in Dhaka and their service fee (as in V's Need to know; HC Dhaka "Agencies" page lists only Tourism Malaysia).

**Refusals.** In 2025 news, Malaysia was among the countries refusing Bangladeshi applicants most often. Travel operators blame fake documents, overstaying and people using tourist visas to work (N0026, N0027). There is no official refusal rate.

## Tourist visa: Step 4, MDAC

- IMI told travellers, on Facebook, to use only imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main (N0030; consistent with official V0065).
- Feb 2026: sponsored Google ads led to fake sites such as mcadfast.com. They take personal details and then charge (AUD52/RM145). Press reports say MDAC is free and there is no paid "faster approval" (N0057, N0031 (2024)).
- Press lists the official channels as IMI's website, the MyNIISE app and QR codes in the airport arrival hall (N0058).

**Need to know (still open):** an official statement that MDAC is free. IMI's MDAC announcement page and its 2023 infographic (raw/imi-mdac-announcement, raw/imi-mdac-infographic-bm-2023) give no fee either way. In the doc: "reported free (unofficial, not verified)".

## Tourist visa: Step 5, At the border (denied entry / "Not to Land")

**What AKPS checks.** AKPS is the Malaysian Border Control and Protection Agency. In 2025 it ran early-morning screening of Dhaka flights at KLIA Terminal 1. It turned back Bangladeshis holding valid visas:
- 51 in Mar 2025 (N0007, N0008)
- 96 on 11 Jul (V0111)
- 123 on 23 Jul (N0013)
- 80 on 25 Jul (N0011)
- 40 on 12 Aug (N0009)
- 98 on 15 Aug (V0109)

Reasons given: fake hotel bookings, no return ticket, too little money or foreign currency, falsified documents, a vague purpose, and failing to give "satisfactory justification". Some people admitted they came for jobs (N0007, N0008, N0011, N0013). AKPS says entrants must show a valid passport and visa, enough money and credible accommodation (N0012). More than 200 Bangladeshis were deported after refusal in 2025 (N0014). In 2023, most NTL notices went to Bangladeshi, Pakistani and Indian travellers (N0016 (2023)).

**What happens after an NTL.** Refused travellers are deported within 24 hours (N0010). They are held in a designated airport area until the airline flies them back (N0015 (2023), N0020 (2022)). Since a July 2023 Cabinet decision, airlines and not third parties handle the return (N0015). A traveller who doesn't understand the questions can ask for a translator (Indian High Commission advisory, by analogy: N0019 (2022)). An NTL means you cannot enter until you meet the entry conditions, and blacklisting is one listed reason for an NTL (N0017, N0018 (2023, NST explainer)).

**Need to know (still open):** whether an NTL is recorded against future visa applications or entries, and whether the traveller is ever charged for the return flight. No official IMI or AKPS page found.

## Tourist visa: Don't work / Scams (tourist visas sold as jobs)

- Jan 2025: the BD High Commission in KL warned that fraudsters bring Bangladeshi women on tourist or other visas with false job promises. There is no agreement for recruiting female workers, and women were told not to travel for general work (news report: N0028).
- Aug 2025: the BD High Commission in KL warned that a ring collects passports and cash for non-existent Sabah jobs. There is no MoU covering Sabah (news report: N0029; the official advisory page is blocked).
- 2023: corruption at HC Dhaka included tourist visas issued to people actually going to work (N0024 (2023)).

**Need to know:** the official texts of both BD High Commission warnings (see blocked-parts/N.md).

## Student visa: practical notes

- **Working.** A mainstream Bangla guide (Prothom Alo, Jun 2025) tells students they can work up to 20 hours "combining semester breaks or weekends". The official rule allows work only in semester breaks or holidays, with Immigration approval. N0053 is status **conflict** with official S0013, S0039 and S0041; the official rule wins. The lead should add a note on S0013.
- **Overstaying.** Community members say many Bangladeshis who came on student visas stayed after their studies (N0036).
- **Forgery.** In Feb 2026 a Bangladeshi on a valid student pass was arrested for running a passport-forgery ring that helped Bangladeshis pass FOMEMA (foreign-worker medical) checks. He was held under s.56(1)(i) of the Immigration Act (N0035).
- **Graduate Pass.** In Aug 2025 a Bangladeshi adviser said Malaysia had agreed "in principle" to Graduate Pass visas for Bangladeshi students (N0032, N0033). Malaysia's Higher Education Minister then called this "false and baseless" (N0034). The official list still excludes Bangladesh (S0044). The doc can say that claims of a Graduate Pass for Bangladeshis were denied by the minister [S0044, N0034].
- **Existing student rows still stand:** S0134-S0140 (agents' fees Tk 6-8 lakh, students promised work, 2017 fake colleges).

**Need to know (still open):** EMGS (Education Malaysia Global Services) real processing times for Bangladeshi applicants. Only an undated forum and SEO pages claim "1-3 months"; nothing usable was found. Also open: any 2025-2026 official list of colleges whose permission to take international students was withdrawn (searched in Malay and English; none found).

## Medical visit

**Official MHTC figures and events.** MHTC is the Malaysia Healthcare Travel Council. Close to 70,000 Bangladeshi healthcare travellers visited Malaysia in 2025 (verified: N0039). Malaysia Healthcare Week ran in Dhaka on 5-12 Apr 2026, and in Dhaka and Chattogram on 16-21 Sep 2026. Hospitals taking part: Alpha IVF, IHH Healthcare, KL Fertility and Sunway Medical Centre in both cities, and KPJ Ampang Puteri in Dhaka (N0040). MHTC works through Bangladeshi "healthcare facilitators" and travel partners (N0041).

**Partner in Bangladesh.** Dhaka Tribune (Sep 2026) names Irving Aviation Limited as MHTC's Bangladesh partner. MHTC's own releases name none (N0042). MHTC named GD Assist in 2022 (V0078).

**Need to know (still open):** MHTC's current authorised partners in Bangladesh. In the doc, report the Irving Aviation name only as "unofficial, not verified". Demand is mainly for cardiology, oncology, transplants and surgery (N0043; corridor context only).

## Employment Pass (Bangladeshi professionals)

A "significant number" of Bangladeshi professionals work in Malaysia on Employment Passes (N0037). They fear the 1 June 2026 thresholds (official: E0008-E0011) will push employers to keep only exceptionally qualified Bangladeshis (N0038). This is corridor context only; there are no numbers.

## MM2H

In 2024 a Bangladesh Bank spokesperson said the central bank has never approved sending money to Malaysia for MM2H (Malaysia My Second Home), so that money did not leave legally (N0051, N0052 (2024)). E0141 has 3,604 Bangladeshi MM2H holders (Jan 2024).

**Need to know:** an official Bangladesh Bank or Foreign Exchange Regulation rule on whether a Bangladeshi resident may legally transfer the USD 150,000 MM2H deposit and buy property abroad. This matters for the "how" of MM2H for Bangladeshis.

## Asylum

**Bangladeshis are not a listed refugee group.** UNHCR registered 225,740 refugees and asylum-seekers (Aug 2026); about 90% are from Myanmar, and the other named origins are Pakistan, Yemen, Afghanistan and Somalia. Bangladesh is not named (verified: N0044). UNHCR's figures grow only with its own registration capacity (N0045). The doc can use this for "written to discourage": there is no listed Bangladeshi refugee population [N0044].

**Fake UNHCR cards.**
- A Bangladeshi-run syndicate in Kulim sold fake work permits and UNHCR cards for RM80-250 each; 19 Bangladeshis were arrested (Jun-Jul 2026) (N0049).
- A KL ring sold forged e-PLKS work passes for RM150 and fake police reports for RM70; 27 Bangladeshis were detained (Sep 2026) (N0050).

**How to check a UNHCR card is real (official).** UNHCR cards carry holograms, barcodes and a QR code. Anyone can check them with the free UNHCR Verify Plus app from Google Play or the App Store only. "Incompatible QR Code" means the document is not genuine. UNHCR Malaysia is at 570 Jalan Bukit Petaling, KL, tel 03 2118 4800; detention hotline +6012 630 5060 (N0046-N0048; booklet version Dec 2024, uploaded Jun 2026, so possibly-outdated). Use with S0109 and S0143 (UNHCR services are free).

## Having a child

No new practical source specific to Bangladeshi parents was found. The general statelessness reporting is not Bangladesh-specific and isn't used. Existing official rows S0114-S0133 cover the procedure.

**Need to know (still open):** what happens to children of undocumented Bangladeshi parents (S part).

## Draft steps (additions to existing V/S/E steps)

**Tourist, Step 2 (apply):**
1. Apply only on malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my yourself. The High Commissioner told Bangladeshis to apply directly on the official portal [V0005, N0054].
2. Agents reportedly charge Tk 5,000-8,000 against official charges of RM20 + RM105 (unofficial, not verified) [V0016, V0017, N0056]. Past Dhaka processors took unreceipted mark-ups [N0021 (2015), N0023 (2016)].
3. If MyVISA is down, wait for the mission's notice of restoration; outages happened in Feb and Jul 2026 [N0001, N0005, N0006].

**Tourist, Step 4 (MDAC):** search nothing; type imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main or use MyNIISE [V0059, N0058]. Any site asking for payment is reported to be fake (unofficial, not verified) [V0065, N0057].

**Tourist, Step 5 (border):**
- Carry printed proof of a paid hotel booking, a return ticket and cash or a card for your stay [V0050, V0054].
- Expect questioning on early-morning Dhaka flights; refusals in 2025 were for fake bookings, no return ticket, too little money, or admitting a job search [N0007, N0011, N0013].
- If refused: you are held airside and the airline flies you back within about 24 hours; ask for a translator if needed [N0010, N0015 (2023), N0019 (2022)].

**Tourist, Scams:** a tourist visa with a "job" attached is a fraud. The BD High Commission KL has warned women and Sabah job-seekers [N0028, N0029]. A visit pass does not allow work [V0046].

**Student, Can students work?:** add that news guides overstate student work rights; the official rule is the one that applies [S0013, N0053].

**Medical, Step 2:** apply through an MHTC member hospital (V0073, V0076). **Need to know:** the current MHTC partner in Bangladesh; news names Irving Aviation (unofficial, not verified) [N0042].

**Asylum:** Bangladeshis are not among UNHCR Malaysia's listed nationalities [N0044]. Fake UNHCR cards are sold by syndicates and lead to arrest [N0049]. A genuine card can be checked with UNHCR Verify Plus [N0046, N0047].

**MM2H:** **Need to know:** Bangladesh Bank permission to move the deposit. In 2024 a BB spokesperson said no approval had ever been given (unofficial, not verified) [N0052 (2024)].

## Need to know (summary)

- The date MyVISA reopened for Bangladesh after the 10 Feb 2026 pause.
- The current authorised visa agencies or service provider in Dhaka and their fee.
- An official statement on whether MDAC is free.
- Consequences of a Not to Land notice for future entry; who pays the return flight.
- Official texts of the BD High Commission KL warnings (women on tourist visas, Jan 2025; Sabah, Aug 2025).

- Bangladesh Bank or foreign-exchange rule on outward transfers for MM2H.

# Findings, part O: student visa, tourist visa

Researcher: part O (rewrite of malaysia.src.md lines 177-266 into drafts/MY4.md). Accessed 7 Nov 2026. New ledger rows O0001-O0039 (ledger-parts/O.csv); 11 new sources registered.

## Re-verification of carried-over facts
- Every S/V/Z/C/K/R/N/L/B id kept in MY4 was re-read in ledger.csv (claim plus quote); quotes are tool-checked against raw copies. Spot-checked in raw: MyVISA FAQ fee table (0.8% card / 1.7% e-wallet; e-wallets are only Alipay and WeChat Pay, so Bangladeshis in practice pay by card), MyVISA T&C clauses 19-24 (interview online or physical; time resets after re-upload), IMI Student Pass part-time work section (documents list; no fee stated in that section).
- Dropped from the doc: the Prothom Alo 2025 "weekend work" claim (N0053, conflicts with official S0013/S0039; official rule kept); the Feb 2026 MyVISA "until further notice" pause (N0001, history; portal is running, V0025 notice shows Bangladesh hours); HC Dhaka's 30-day advance and 6-month bank statement + solvency letter (V0014, V0028; older/undated than MyVISA's 14 days and 3 months, V0013, V0027); HC Dhaka and IMI "3 months" SEV validity (V0039, C0002; superseded by MyVISA T&C 6 Oct 2026, R0005/R0006); T&C clause 4 "MEV for all nationalities" and IMI Types of Visas MEV page (Z0016, Z0068; superseded by FAQ V0009); 2017 RM20,000 agent fee (S0140) and 2017 Star series (S0138) as old; IMI "Types of visas" undated pages.
- Resolved "Need to know" items: (1) multiple-entry visa for a Bangladeshi tourist: MyVISA FAQ (6 Oct 2026) limits MEV from 1 Sep 2026 to Fly & Cruise, Business, Medical Treatment and Wedding Tourism for the 31 eVISA nationalities (Bangladesh is #3 on that list); ordinary tourists get SEV [V0009]. (2) When MyVISA came back after the Feb 2026 pause: not needed in a clean guide; the portal is operating. (3) Family members on one account: FAQ says one account holds 5 applications for max 5 individuals [V0023]; minors need a birth certificate [O0039]. (4) Whether MDAC is free: no official page says "free"; IMI's instructions/poster (raw/imi-notice-mdac-foreign-en, poster image viewed) list no fee, and IMI flags a paying site as fake [V0065]; wrote it that way, no Need to know. (5) Official list of private institutions allowed to take international students: FOUND. JPT page "Senarai Daftar dan Statistik IPT Swasta" links Google Drive folders: "Senarai Daftar IPTS Kelulusan KDN" (yearly folders 2019-2026, monthly AKTIF and TIDAK AKTIF PDFs; latest: up to 31 Jul 2026, updated 20 Aug 2026, saved as raw/jpt-ipts-kdn-active-2026-7 and -inactive-2026-7) and "Senarai IPTS Yang Telah Diluluskan Tindakan Pembatalan Kelulusan Penubuhan" (cancelled establishment). Column headers in the PDF are two-row and the group labels did not render (TARIKH MULA/TAMAT x2, SETARA, a rating such as "Berdaya Saing"); I did not interpret the date columns in the doc. Rendered page 1 image checked.

## New how-to detail
- IMI's checklist for every new Student Pass includes the institution's KDN approval letter still in force [O0006] - supports the active-list check.
- EMGS user guide PDF (created 19 Dec 2019, possibly outdated; mentions 6% tax and a physical VAL delivered only to the institution, both superseded): name as in passport MRZ, passport number must match offer letter, offer letter as one PDF (550 KB limit), payment via institution / telegraphic transfer (USD50 convenience fee) / card (3% fee), payment-problem email enquiry@emgs.com.my, institution must confirm the online application. Doc uses only the stable items and omits the 2019 fee amounts.
- EMGS contact page is only an enquiry form (name, email, phone, date of birth, gender, passport PDF); no phone or email published there.
- Students on MyVISA apply under VDR (C0006). Whether the RM105 processing charge applies to student VDR eVISAs: not stated on MyVISA FAQ/T&C or EMGS pages; kept as Need to know.

## Reality checks (student)
- Prothom Alo op-ed (USM senior lecturer, 19 Sep 2026): agents paid commission by institutions; free consultation = sales pitch; passports withheld for more payments; lapsed licences; work-on-student-visa lie; students caught working are deported, sometimes fined/jailed; verify offer letters with the international office by email/phone [O0017-O0020, S0134, S0135, S0137].
- Dhaka Post, 17 Oct 2026: three students, two agencies, about Tk 18.5 lakh; fake Genovasi offer letter; EMGS progress stuck 5-15% for three months; tuition not paid; moved to a Bangladeshi-owned English centre; two passports held by the agent (agent denies); families complained in writing to the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare (outcome not reported) [O0021-O0026]. Agency names are in the raw copy, not in the doc.
- Dhaka Tribune, 16 Jul 2026: Bhatara police case, court remand of education-consultancy officials over ~Tk 20 crore study-abroad fraud (91 students in one firm) [O0027].
- ApplyBoard guide (23 Sep 2026): tracker milestones 32-35% / 70% (eVAL downloadable) / 100%; says refusals can be appealed or re-applied via the institution (lead only, not official; doc keeps the appeal as Need to know) [O0013-O0016].
- 2017 R.AGE/Star series (Straits Times reprint saved raw/news-st-lured-students, not registered) and PIE News 2017 on EMGS pre-screening (raw/news-pie-emgs-visa-fraud, not registered): background only.
- Complaint channels: KPT SiSPAA at mohe.spab.gov.my (page updated 14 Jul 2025) [O0028]; MyGCC 24-hour call centre also listed on KPT contact page (raw/mohe-hubungi-sispaa, not logged). Bangladesh Ministry Complaint Resolution Cell minister.expat@gmail.com (R0012) is for Bangladeshis abroad; whether it acts on education-agent complaints is unknown. No evidence found either way on ILO/IOM/NGOs taking individual student-fraud cases.

## Reality checks (tourist)
- Migration Concern (27 Jul 2025): carry RM500-1,000; officers check hotel booking, purpose, sometimes phone photos; NTL list [O0029, O0030]. Existing: V0109, V0110, N0010, N0011, N0013, N0014, N0015, N0026.
- Bonik Barta (19 Sep 2026): Bangladesh immigration police stopped 81,500 travellers Jan 2021-Jun 2026 for document/visa/BMET-clearance mismatches and answers that don't match; Malaysia among 23 countries [O0031]. The figure is not in the doc.
- bdnews24 (13 Oct 2026): CID arrested Paltan travel-agency owner who promised work permits, sent two people on visit visas, held them, demanded Tk 5 lakh ransom (Tk 2 lakh paid); case under the money-laundering prevention act; CID asks people to report passport-holding and unusual visa demands [O0032, O0033]. CID contact details not in the article.
- Facebook/YouTube posts about tourist-visa job fraud and a Daily Star Bangla report on fake-visa makers arrested in Malaysia found but not used.

## Need to know still open (after search)
Student: late documents after EMGS return; appeal of refused Student Pass; RM105 charge for student VDR eVISA; eVAL authenticity check; part-time work permission fee and time; LTSVP for bachelor's student family; extra school documents and school-pass appeal; CID contact for reports; whether the Ministry acts on education-agent complaints; whether the High Commission helps recover passports held by agents; whether ILO/IOM/NGOs take student cases.
Tourist: genuine eVISA appearance (QR code, sticker number location; blocked, needs a sample); appeal for a visitor refused entry, official minimum funds, NTL consequences; whether ILO/IOM/NGOs take tourist-visa trafficking cases.

- eVAL delivery: EMGS 2019 guide says VAL delivered only to the institution; ApplyBoard 2026 says eVAL is a downloadable PDF in the student's portal at 70%. Newer (unofficial) used, cited as news/guide.
- Personal search searches done: Bangla and English news on student fraud and KLIA refusals; Malay "senarai IPTS dibenarkan mengambil pelajar antarabangsa"; EMGS advisory on impersonators (Facebook/Instagram only, blocked); MDAC free; eVISA QR; EMGS appeal.

# Findings, part P: family visit VDR, business, medical, Professional Visit Pass, transit, Employment Pass

Researched 7 Nov 2026 for draft chunk drafts/MY5.md (source lines 267-320). New ledger rows P0001-P0047 (ledger-parts/P.csv). The draft also cites existing verified/possibly-outdated rows from ledger.csv (V, C, Z, E, K, B, N series), each re-read against its claim and quote before reuse.

## Family visit VDR (social Visa Dengan Rujukan)

- IMI's 2023 checklist PDF (SENARAI-SEMAK-Borang-VDR.pdf, scanned, no text layer; rows logged with --no-quote-check, quotes transcribed from page images) contains the full kit: Imm.12 x2, passport copy, personal/security bond stamped RM10 at LHDN, sponsor IC (sponsor must attend), sponsor's declaration (Commissioner for Oaths), income proof RM2,000+/month, employer letter, sponsor information form, cover letter, relationship proof; 7 working days [P0010-P0012]. Same content as the IMI VDR web page [Z0025-Z0028, V0090].
- Security bond only for China and Nigeria nationals; everyone else, including Bangladeshis, gives a personal bond [P0006]. Personal bond form: sponsor binds himself to forfeit a sum deposited against a receipt [P0014]. The sponsor information form asks "if approved, are you willing to pay a deposit (wang cagaran)?" [P0012]. IMI's bond-rate table gives RM750 for Bangladesh [V0112]. Whether a cash deposit is actually taken on a family VDR for a Bangladeshi is not stated anywhere: kept as Need to know.
- Relationship-proof table: parents/parents-in-law (marriage cert, birth cert and passport of sponsor's spouse, notarial certificate endorsed by Malaysian mission abroad and Wisma Putra), child under 7, spouse of citizen [P0008].
- Processing: VDR page and checklist say 7 working days; IMI client charter says 5 working days [V0090, P0010 vs C0044]. Doc uses 7 (two sources, and charter applies only to normal cases). Not shown as conflict (doesn't change what anyone does).
- Where the sponsor submits: VDR is approved by IMI HQ; the charter lists it under the Visa, Pass and Permit Division [C0008, C0044]. IMI's LTSVP page says some related applications (work endorsement for spouses) can be made at HQ Putrajaya "or the nearest state Immigration office", and that China/Nigeria spouse applications go to HQ only; nothing says which for a social VDR. Searched: IMI VDR page, IMI forms page, IMI FAQ Visa/Pass/Permit (FAQ body is JS-loaded and empty in both curl and Firecrawl copies), HC Dhaka FAQ, web and Malay search ("permohonan VDR sosial ... pejabat imigresen negeri"). Kept as Need to know; HQ address and MyGCC phone 03-8000 8000 given [P0005].
- eVISA after VDR approval: MyVISA's VDR eVISA-exempt list (51 countries) was re-scraped with Firecrawl directQuote on 7 Nov and saved as raw/myvisa-faq-vdr-exempt-list.md; Bangladesh is not on it [P0015]. This resolves the earlier draft's doubt about the researcher-note list in raw/myvisa-faq.md. VDR eVISA needs passport biodata page (6+ months) and the eVAL [P0016]; mismatches must be fixed by the letter-issuing office before payment [P0017]. MyVISA's rate table (visa fee + RM105 + 0.8/1.7%) does not separate VTR from VDR, so the doc applies it to VDR [P0018].
- Appeal against a refused social VDR: nothing on IMI VDR page, checklist, MyVISA FAQ/terms (MyVISA only says missions may refuse without reasons [V0042], which is about eVISA). Kept as Need to know.
- Practice: no news found on family-visit VDR abuse specifically (searched English and Bangla). General visit-pass misuse arrests used instead [P0020, P0022].

## Business visit

- Multiple-entry eligibility: IMI/MyVISA say MEV is limited to 31 (IMI notice: 30) eligible nationalities without naming them [C0007, V0009, V0010, V0011]. VisasNews (4 Sep 2026) ran test applications on MyVISA for the 31 eVISA nationalities, Bangladesh among them, and the form allowed MEV for Business, Fly & Cruise, Medical, Wedding Tourism [P0001]; Observer (4 Sep 2026) says business/medical MEVs remain available to Bangladeshis [P0002]. Unofficial only: doc keeps one Need to know for an official statement. Jagonews24 English article (MEV tightened for Bangladesh and 30 others) returned 403; not needed after VisasNews.
- MEV validity conflict carried from part V: 6 months/30 days per entry (MyVISA terms, 2026) vs 3-12 months (undated IMI Types of Visas and HC Dhaka FAQ) [V0071 vs V0072, V0114]. Doc states only the current MyVISA rule.
- PLS@Xpats (new to the doc): IMI FAQ PDF (Lampiran B, Oct 2022) [P0024-P0031]: critical/emergency professional work on a social visit pass, max 30 days, approval valid 30 days single entry, no extension, free, 3 working days, ESD Putrajaya decides, company applies at mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats/main with company letter, full passport copy, e-SSM, schedule, return ticket; all nationalities except Israel, 18+, company need not be ESD-registered; visa-required nationals still need an eVISA; no entry without the approval letter; Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan only; no dependant pass. Charter confirms 3 working days [C0048]. 2022 PDF, so possibly-outdated; IMI home page still lists PLS@XPATS as a live service [E0072].
- Reality check: government directive 8 Jun 2026 for a nationwide crackdown on misuse of short-term passes including business travellers (EY alert) [P0021]; 79 Bangladeshis arrested in Perak glove factories for visit-pass misuse (Apr 2026) [P0020]; 173 foreign workers, 147 Bangladeshi, at Sepang construction sites, employers misusing others' PLKS and social visit passes (Sep 2026) [P0022]; 2024 syndicate bringing Bangladeshis on fake sports invitations, RM2,000-5,000 each [P0040]. KLIA "Not To Land" refusals of Bangladeshi visitors 2025 are in part V [V0109-V0111]; none of the reports found was about business visas specifically.
- Speed money: 2023 MACC arrest of an HC Dhaka enforcement officer for USD 300-500 bribes per visa [N0025]. Immigration DG (Aug 2025): matters only through JIM counters/official portal; fake ePASS sold for RM5,000; report to police or MACC [P0038, P0039].
- Complaint routes: Malaysia: police or MACC [P0039]; MyVISA Live Chat (24h) for payment problems [P0019]. Bangladesh: BMET complaint to DG (complaint cell, free, 30 working days) [B0034]; mobile courts under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013 [B0047]; full procedure lives in the PLKS Scams section (other agents' chunk). ILO/IOM don't take individual cases (part G, G0027) - not repeated in this chunk.

## Medical visit

- MHTC's Aug 2026 press release (carried by Utusan Malaysia) names Irving Aviation Ltd as "MHTC's local partner in Bangladesh", contact Abid Rahman Rafsan +8801762001467 [P0032]. Registered as official (MHTC is under the Ministry of Health and authored the release). The 2022 GD Assist appointment [V0078] and the Dhaka Tribune Sep 2026 report [N0042] are superseded in the doc. MHTC's Sep 2026 Healthcare Week release (Bernama/Macau Business copy) names no partner.
- Conflict that changes action: HC Dhaka (undated) says Bangladeshi medical visitors apply through MHTC's authorised partners [V0075]; MyVISA (Oct 2026) and MHTC's own steps describe applying online yourself [V0073, V0076]. Kept as one Need to know.
- MHTC procedure: appointment letter in English [P0034], MHTC support letter [V0076], Find A Hospital directory and WhatsApp concierge +60 18 870 0100 [P0035], print approval [P0036], Special Lane conditions [P0037], what to bring [P0046]. 91 member hospitals [P0033].
- Long-term medical pass: IMI 2023 checklist (Imm.38, Imm.12, hospital letter, 3 months' bank statements, relationship proof certified by Malaysian mission, MHTC letter, payment proof) [P0013]; LTSVP web page: government or specialist hospitals, one companion [C0018].
- Scams: searched Bangla ("মেডিকেল ভিসা মালয়েশিয়া ... দালাল প্রতারণা") and English. Results were about fake Indian-hospital appointments for Indian medical visas and hospital brokers in Bangladeshi public hospitals, nothing on the Malaysia route. Kept as Need to know.

## Professional Visit Pass

- PVP-Expert FAQ (ESD, Sep 2026): document list by category [P0041]; approval period depends on the task, max not automatic [P0042]; earlier rows E0070, V0092-V0094.
- ePASS covers EP, DP, LTSVP and PVP; endorsement steps (company submits and pays in ESD Online; KLIA ESC T1/T2 without appointment, or MYXpats Centre); corrections via MYXpats helpdesk 3-5 working days; i-KAD only for EP holders [P0043-P0045].
- Standard PVP processing time: client charter gives 5 days (fast-track PVP-Expert), artistes 5, missionaries 25, Sarawak manual 30 [C0048, C0047, Z0079]; nothing for a standard PVP via ESD. Kept as Need to know.
- "PVP sold to general workers": searched English ("professional visit pass misuse ... Bangladesh") and Bangla ("প্রফেশনাল ভিসা মালয়েশিয়া ... প্রতারণা"); found only PLKS/social-visit-pass misuse and fake e-PLKS rings [P0023, D0045]. Kept as Need to know.

## Transit

- Unchanged rules [V0079-V0082]. Only Malaysia's missions in India state the PNR/KLIA-KLIA2 rule [V0081]; no IMI or HC Dhaka statement for Bangladeshis. Third-party airport guides (klia2.com.my, visatraveler) are unofficial and disagree with each other; not used. Need to know kept.

## Employment Pass

- Bangladesh side added: a worker with an individually obtained visa applies for BMET emigration clearance himself at BMET's emigration branch (Dhaka) or Chattogram DEMO, 3 working days; fees Tk 4,500 welfare+insurance, Tk 250 smart card, source tax Tk 400/250 [B0029, B0030]. B0028 (same charter, conflict status in ledger) not cited.
- Section 60K timing conflict: ESD Aug 2026 booklet 10 working days [Z0086] vs JTKSM 2023 FAQ 14 working days [Z0085]. Doc uses the newer ESD figure only; the difference doesn't change what the worker does.
- Earlier draft's Need to know "what MSC Malaysia status is": ESD's own line names the Multimedia Development Corporation and ICT; MIDA's MSC page is 404 and MDEC's Malaysia Digital page is JavaScript-only (Firecrawl got only a heading). Doc now explains it as ICT companies with MSC Malaysia status approved by the Multimedia Development Corporation [E0007]; no separate Need to know.
- Earlier Need to know "nationality restriction for Bangladeshis": ESD's visa-charge table has a Bangladesh rate [E0045] and nothing found restricts Bangladeshis; dropped from the doc rather than kept as a gap.
- EP scams: no official ESD scam notice found (search "ESD scam alert fake Employment Pass"). Used the Aug 2025 fake-ePASS case [P0038]. The Indian High Commission KL advisory for professionals exists only as a 2017 Wayback copy (raw/hci-kl-advisory-professionals, not registered, not used).

- "The Malaysian High Commission in New Delhi tells Indian travellers..." phrasing replaced by the rule itself with citation.
- 2023 news on refused travellers' airlines etc. belongs to part V (Tourist visa).
- "IMI's VDR page says 7 days while client charter says 5" and "older official pages still describe 3 to 12 months" removed from the doc (research log).

- Cut per checker: P0021 crackdown line, N0025 HC Dhaka bribery anecdote, E0045 "visa-charge table" filler, "test applications" narration (P0001/P0002 no longer cited). Offence (false statement, K0031/K0030) now stated only in Tourist visa, Scams (existing text, outside this chunk); MY5 cross-refers to it from each visa.


# Findings, part Q: family passes, MM2H, DE Rantau, other passes

Rewrite of malaysia.src.md lines 321-376 (draft: drafts/MY6.md). Researched 7 Nov 2026. Ledger rows Q0001-Q0077 (ledger-parts/Q.csv).

## Family of an Employment Pass holder

- Dependant Pass duration follows the principal's pass (or the dependant's passport if shorter) [E0034]. This resolves the old draft's "how long a Dependant Pass lasts" gap.
- LTSVP duration still conflicts: ESD's LTSVP page says it follows the principal's pass [E0147], ESD's payment table (Jul 2025) caps approval at 12 months [E0148]. Kept as one Need to know.
- Parents for Dependant Pass: ESD's older EP page lists parents under DP [E0149]; the 2026 salary policy puts them under LTSVP [E0014]. Newer policy kept; conflict left out of the doc.
- Work: ESD's current Dependant Pass page [Q0001] and the 2025 ESD guidebook [E0145] both say a DP holder must get their own Employment Pass. IMI's 2021 FAQ [E0038] says a spouse can work with an endorsement. Kept the ESD rule (newer, two sources); the IMI 2021 line is dropped from the doc. Secondary sources (Speedhome, AHK, BAL) agree no current work right; BAL reports MDEC stopped "consent to work" for DP spouses (undated).
- New: Budget 2027 speech (9 Oct 2026, Malay text p.75): from 1 Jan 2027 spouses of Category I EP holders on a DP may work, "tertakluk kepada syarat ditetapkan" [Q0003]. The English speech text (raw/mof-budget-2027-speech-en.txt) does not contain the passage in its text layer; the Malay speech was fetched instead. Conditions not yet published (law-firm notes eiglaw.com, tandslaw.com, envoyglobal say the same; not saved).
- New: from 1 Nov 2026 DP spouses can apply online for Permission to Study via the ESD Online Sub-Product menu; counter submissions no longer accepted [Q0005].
- Appeal: ESD's Appeal Module covers only EP and PVP [Q0064]. No official text found on appealing a refused DP or LTSVP (searched ESD FAQ pages: MyXpats, Xpats Gateway, company registration; IMI FAQ). Need to know kept.
- Personal bond: the old draft asked whether an EP holder's family LTSVP needs a bond. ESD guidebook says LTSVP needs a personal bond stamped by IRB [E0031]; bond rate for Bangladeshis RM750 [E0144]. Gap closed.
- Certification: ESD wants certificates certified by the Malaysian mission in Bangladesh or the Bangladesh mission in Malaysia, dated in the current year [E0030]. BHC KL attestation fee: RM42 normal / RM63 urgent for certificates and affidavits from 16 Jan 2026 [Q0062]; the older BHC fee chart PDF (image, 2024 upload) gives RM45 for a marriage/nikahnama certificate [Q0077]; newer page used. Malaysian HC Dhaka pages saved (kln-mhc-dhaka-faqs, home, visa requirement, agencies) cover visas only, nothing on certifying family documents: Need to know kept.
- Scams: searched English and Bangla ("Malaysia family visa scam", "মালয়েশিয়া ফ্যামিলি ভিসা দালাল প্রতারণা"); found only work-permit and fake-visa rings (Daily Star Bangla news-540361, 358361; bribery at the Malaysian HC Dhaka news-501376), nothing on family passes. Need to know kept.

## Spouse of a Malaysian citizen

- Office: IMI's spouse-pass extension page says all pass applications can go to the office nearest the applicant's home [Z0046]; new-application page sends only Chinese and African nationals to HQ [Z0047]. Used as the rule; old Need to know dropped.
- Work-endorsement office conflict: one block of IMI's LTSVP page says the Visa, Pass and Permit Division in the state where you work [Z0097]; another says HQ Putrajaya or the nearest state office [Z0098]. The doc gives the state-of-work office (also S0099); conflict left out (doesn't change much: the state office is valid under both).
- Added checklist items from IMI's LTSVP page: sworn declaration with photo stamped by a Commissioner for Oaths, postcard-size wedding photo, divorce/death certificate, children's birth certificates, employer letter, 3 months' payslips/bank book/Form J [Q0006-Q0009].
- Fee: no official fee for a citizen's-spouse LTSVP found. Searched imi.gov.my (Kadar Bayaran Visa page lists only visa fees: Bangladesh RM20), WebSearch in Malay and English. Secondary sources (Santa Fe Relo, MDEC "new update from Immigration", expat forums) say the LTSVP fee moved from RM50 a month to RM90 a year; not official for this category. Need to know kept.
- Appeal for a refused spouse LTSVP: nothing official found. Need to know kept.
- Reality check:
  - Daily Star Bangla, 4 Nov 2023 [Q0010]: IMI DG Ruslin Jusoh (via Berita Harian) said PLKS holders are barred from marrying locals; a marriage in breach automatically cancels the permit and the worker is deported; foreigners must fill a special form with employer details. Old (2023) but consistent with IMI's PLKS page [E0139].
  - FMT, 13 Jul 2025 [Q0011, Q0012]: IMI DG Zakaria Shaaban: syndicates pay elderly Malaysian women monthly allowances to marry young foreign men; IMI won't approve LTSVPs for marriages of convenience; a syndicate selling spouse-category applications and JPN marriage registration was broken. Not specific to Bangladeshis.
  - No report found of Bangladeshi men specifically targeted by spouse-visa syndicates (English and Bangla searches).
- Permanent residence for spouses: now from IMI's Entry Permit e-book (see below): wife 3 years married + >12 months on spouse LTSVP; husband 5 years + 5 continuous years on the LTSVP [Q0016-Q0018].

## Residence Pass and other relatives

- Carried over unchanged in substance; all ids re-checked against claims.py output (Z0018, Z0048, Z0049, Z0069-Z0073, E0131-E0133; C0015-C0020, Z0029, Z0074-Z0076, Z0099, Z0100, S0007, E0057, K0031).
- Residence Pass status check is on IMI's myOnlinePasResiden [E0136].
- Residence Pass processing time: IMI's client charter (imi-client-charter) lists no Residence Pass line. Need to know kept. Appeal: nothing found.

## Complaint routes (Immigration)

- IMI SISPAA (imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do) confirmed by Firecrawl scrape: new complaint ("Aduan/Maklum Balas Baharu") and check status ("Semak Aduan/Maklum Balas") [Q0046]; linked from IMI's own site [Q0067].
- IMI Integrity Division complaints unit for officer misconduct or bribery: address, aduanintegriti@imi.gov.my, 03-8880 1246, QR online form, Whistleblower Protection Act 2010 protection if the complainant identifies themselves [Q0068-Q0070].
- Berita Harian (Apr 2026, via search snippet only, not saved) gives IMI's illegal-immigrant reporting line 03-8880 1374 and aduanpati_hq@imi.gov.my; not used.
- EAIC (Enforcement Agencies Integrity Commission) also takes complaints about enforcement officers including Immigration (search result only, eaic.gov.my not saved). Not used in the doc.
- No evidence found on how long SISPAA answers take, or that the Bangladesh High Commission helps with family-pass cases. Outside bodies (ILO, IOM, NGOs) have no role in non-worker pass applications in anything found.
- Note: raw/imi-faq-plsxpats-fc.md was logged with URL ".../soalan-lazim-plsxpats/#fc" because the plain URL was already fetched by curl (empty FAQ body); the scrape adds the SISPAA link and HQ phone.

## MM2H

- Medical check after approval at a MOTAC panel clinic; FD withdrawal up to 50%; OSC MM2H; renewal RM500/year + visa RM0-50; contacts [Q0039-Q0044].
- Fee conflict (not resolved): mm2h.gov.my gives RM5,000 processing per principal [E0107]; MOTAC's 1 Aug 2025 system notice gives "Fi proses: RM500 (tidak termasuk SST) bagi setiap permohonan" [E0118]. Possibly a separate system fee. Need to know kept.
- Child age conflict: mm2h.gov.my (Feb 2026) max 34 [E0104, E0110]; MOTAC "Insights on the Categories" PDF (Jan 2025) max 35 [E0111]. Newer official site used; conflict dropped from the doc.
- IMI's undated MM2H page still has part-time work guidelines [Z0089]; MOTAC's current site says only Platinum may work [E0112]. Current MOTAC rule used.
- MM2H and PVIP time does not count for permanent residence (Entry Permit e-book 2.5) [Q0015].
- Reality check:
  - DayakDaily, 18 Apr 2024 [Q0047, Q0048]: Tourism Minister Tiong: agents charged tens of thousands of ringgit, paying an agent doesn't guarantee approval, agents lost contact and clients couldn't recover money; some "agents" were shell entities leasing licences.
  - The Star, 17 Oct 2024 [Q0049]: all agents told to re-register; 229 recognised agent companies; Certificates of Good Conduct and police screening now mandatory; ~30-day review period. 34 of 249 applications approved by 14 Oct 2024 (statistic, not used).
  - The Star, 27 Aug 2026 [Q0050]: minister says MM2H agents must comply with the laws of the countries where they recruit; a Malaysian licence doesn't meet other countries' rules.
  - Bangladesh side: Desh Rupantor, Apr 2024 [N0051]: Bangladesh Bank spokesperson Mezbaul Haque said BB never approved remittances for MM2H, so the money did not go through legal channels; ACC lawyer says ACC can act only on a referral. TBS 2024: 3,604 Bangladeshi MM2H pass holders as of 31 Jan 2024 (statistic, not used). Daily Star Bangla (news-dailystar-bn-premium-visa-mm2h) on Bangladeshis as a top applicant group (not used).
  - Expat.com forum post (c. 2018, not saved): agent vanished after the visa fee and kept original documents.
- Bangladesh Bank rule: no official text found. Searched English and Bangla (GFET capital account, "বিদেশে বাড়ি কেনা বাংলাদেশ ব্যাংকের অনুমতি"), Firecrawl search; bb.org.bd GFET PDFs return HTML; US State Dept investment climate statement returned 403. Secondary sources: Capital Account Transaction (Equity Investment Abroad) Rules 2022 require BB permission for equity investment abroad (companies). Need to know kept; blocked list has the GFET request.
- MOTAC complaints: MOTAC SISPAA at motac.spab.gov.my confirmed by Firecrawl [Q0045]. News (Bernama/Sinar Harian, NST 2022/2025) shows MOTAC tells scam victims to file on SISPAA plus a police report, with receipts; not saved, not used. No evidence MOTAC has recovered money for MM2H applicants.
- MM2H contact email on mm2h.gov.my is Cloudflare-obfuscated; decodes to info@motac.gov.my, but the saved text copy doesn't contain it, so not cited.
- MOTAC's step-by-step MM2H guide PDF is broken/image-only (blocked list).

## DE Rantau

- FAQ v11 (1 Nov 2026): English only; approval letter valid 6 months; eVISA or VDR; normal counter, not autogate; tourist-pass holders must exit and re-enter; endorsement documents incl. LHDN tax registration slip; endorsement within 1 month of entry; stay until e-Pass [Q0051-Q0058].
- Document checklists (Nov 2025) [Q0072-Q0076]. Conflict: freelancer checklist allows "foreign or Malaysian clients" [Q0075] while the FAQ wording logged as Z0066 asks for contracts with foreign clients. Kept as a Need to know because it changes who can apply.
- Budget 2027: holders who reached the two-year limit get two more years, subject to conditions [Q0004]; consistent with the enhanced framework (48 months, E0086/E0098).
- MDEC's 2024 FAQ for China and Bangladesh (v4.0, 28 May 2024): bank guarantee requirement for these nationals waived [Q0071]. Superseded FAQ; not used in the doc. Bond for Bangladeshis RM750 in both.
- Scams: MDEC FAQ Q22 says no agent or MDEC can guarantee approval [E0094]; Q23 says legal threats don't change processing. No news found of DE Rantau scams aimed at Bangladeshis (English search). Sun report (undated, not saved): a holder told to leave after 2 months. MDEC complaint channel not found. Need to know kept.

## Other passes

- RP-T: TalentCorp page: register interest via contact form; EP with more than 3 months' validity; contacts [Q0059-Q0061]. No nationality restriction stated, so the old "whether Bangladeshis are eligible" gap was dropped. Fee, processing time and appeal not on the page.
- PLS@XPATS: IMI's FAQ page exists but has no questions (empty, dated 28 Oct 2022) [Q0067]. Eligibility and fee not found.
- Permanent residence: IMI's Entry Permit e-book (Google Drive folder linked from imi.gov.my; file "e-BOOK GARIS PANDUAN PERMOHONAN PERMIT MASUK (OKT '26).pdf"; cover says SEPT '25) fetched via drive.usercontent.google.com, 39 MB, text layer fine. Resolves the old Need to know on criteria, office, fee, time and appeal [Q0014-Q0038]:
  - Categories: wife, husband, biological child under 18 of a citizen, and fully foreign (WAS: investors, experts, professionals recognised by a regulating agency; 5 continuous years on EP/Student/PVP/Residence Pass).
  - PLKS, UNHCR card and IMM.13 holders excluded from the spouse categories; MM2H, PVIP, PLKS, UNHCR, IMM.13 excluded from WAS. This answers the mentor's "PR for PLKS workers: as far as I know, no": confirmed no.
  - Nearest Immigration office; interview; PDRM screening; status after 6 months online; fee RM120 cashless; deposit RM750 for Bangladeshis (WAS); appeal within 30 days to the Home Minister via KSU KDN; reapply after 1 year; processing now 6 months (DG's preface).
  - Copies of foreign documents: home MOFA or home embassy in Malaysia attestation, translation, then re-attestation by a Malaysian mission or Wisma Putra [Q0035].
  - The REP-specific guideline [Z0080-Z0083] is now covered by the general e-book (same appeal address), so REP detail was cut from the doc as not relevant to Bangladeshis.
- MTEP, Investor Pass: nationality restriction not checked in this pass; kept as Need to know.

## Fix round 2 (7 Nov 2026): checker failures on drafts/MY6.md

- Category III band: the pre-1 June 2026 band is RM3,000-4,999 [E0012]; E0019 gives no salary. Fixed.
- Spouse of a Category I holder: RM20,000 now cited to E0009.
- Permission to Study: counters closed to paper submissions from 1 Nov 2026 [Q0078]. No fee or charter time for PTS found in the ESD guidebook v6, the fee revision FAQ, the MyXpats FAQ or the key-services page: Need to know.
- Bangladesh-side attestation: read the BHC KL attestation checklist PDF (image-only, 2 pages) and the MoFA Dhaka citizen charter PDF (scanned, 12 pages; pp.1-6 read (items 1-32: attestation, then welfare services); pp.7-12 not read in this round). Logged Q0079-Q0084. MoFA Dhaka: family and marriage documents go via notary public and Ministry of Law, two sets, submit 8-12, collect 2-3 pm same day, free (charter dated 24 Aug 2022). BHC KL wants marriage certificates attested by both Law Ministry and MoFA Dhaka first; birth certificates attested in Dhaka or with online-verification proof; one extra set of copies. BHC KL counter hours and turnaround for attestation not published on the fee page, checklist or labour FAQ (the labour FAQ gives 9:30-1:00 only for travel permits): Need to know.
- Personal bond: ESD guidebook describes it as a written undertaking backed by a Malaysian company representative's pledge of a nationality-based sum [Q0086]; the IMI personal bond form says the sum is deposited with the Government and forfeited on default (raw/imi-personal-bond-form.txt), but nothing official says how an ESD dependant's LTSVP bond is paid or refunded. Need to know kept.

IMI's Facebook (Nov 2026, "PEMEGANG PLKS BOLEH BERKAHWIN DI MALAYSIA? JAWAPANNYA TIDAK") repeats the special-form rule but Facebook can't be scraped (Firecrawl unsupported, curl fails); Q0010 kept for the deportation consequence. BHC KL's requirement for a Bangladeshi marrying a Malaysian logged as Q0081.
- Spouse LTSVP: form links from IMI's new-spouse page [Q0111]; Naziran inspection [Q0089]. Fee: Firecrawl search of imi.gov.my found only RM90 for a dependant/guardian LTSVP (Student Pass page, S0007) and a Scribd copy of an old IMI document mentioning a deposit for citizen's spouses (not official host, not used). Need to know kept.

- DE Rantau: income "at least"; over-18 rule [E0084]; freelancer conflict reduced to one Need to know line; insurance requirement from the remote-worker checklist [Q0109] (no minimum cover or insurer list found in the FAQ v11, enhanced FAQ or checklists: Need to know); eVISA charges and time borrowed from the tourist section [V0005, V0006, V0012, V0016, V0017, V0019]; VDR cost not found: Need to know. Complaint route: MDEC's DE Rantau page gives expatctr@mdec.com.my and 03-8315 3106/3157 [Q0097]; MDEC contact form [Q0098]. MDEC's Facebook announcement (same email) could not be scraped.
- RP-T: fetched rpt.talentcorp.com.my fees (Firecrawl; curl copy near-empty) and FAQ (curl) [Q0099-Q0102, Q0110]. Old "register interest" step and Need to know replaced.

- Investor Pass: Xpats Gateway URL [Q0107]. PVIP: portal URL [Q0104], registration check [Q0105]; Firecrawl search found no published IMI list of appointed agencies. PLS@XPATS URL [Q0106].

- Not used: raw/talentcorp-rpt-fees.md and raw/mdec-contact-us.md (curl copies, empty; Firecrawl copies are the -fc files).

# Part R: re-check of leads and conflicts (6 Nov 2026)

Scope: every ledger row with status `lead` (23) or `conflict` (43) in ledger.csv as of 6 Nov 2026. New rows are R0001-R0012 in `ledger-parts/R.csv` (4 facts in `R.facts.csv`). No other part's rows were edited. Sources registered through `ledger.py source` (so sources.csv gained 6 entries): news-rtm-kesuma-osc-2026-07, mof-budget-2027-speech-en, bdhckl-consular-fees-2026-pdf, imi-p4833-overstay, probashi-malaysia-max-migration-cost-order-2022-07, probashi-special-notice-expatriates-2026-09-22 (and myvisa-faq/terms, mohr-dasar were already registered).

Where I searched for the two Ministry press releases (15 Oct and 31 Oct 2026):
- pressinform.gov.bd: its "তথ্যবিবরণী" list (`/pages/all-notes`, category 6922d29581fc96cef9e992c0) is empty for current items, and the archive only holds 2017 handouts. PID handouts for 2026 aren't on the portal.
- probashi.gov.bd: I searched notices, news, press-releases (one 2015 item only), miscellaneous-infos and office-orders for মালয়েশিয়া (both spellings), Malaysia, বিজ্ঞপ্তি, শূন্য, মেডিকেল and প্রেস. The newest Malaysia items are the 5 Nov 2026 BOESL DHL circular (W0001) and the 2025 press release and criteria (W0022, W0023). Neither October 2026 release is there.
- bmet.gov.bd, wewb.gov.bd, boesl.gov.bd and kualalumpur.mofa.gov.bd notices and news pages for Sep-Nov 2026: neither release is posted.
- Web search (English and Bangla, including Facebook and bssnews): only news copies turned up (UNB, TBS, Daily Star, Prothom Alo, bdnews24, Jagonews, Daily Sun, Dhaka Times, Jugantor, Desh Rupantor). I found no Facebook post. bssnews had no October item, and it would count as news anyway.

Totals: 23 leads. 4 confirmed fully or partly by a new official row (W0071, L0188, V0108, B0123). 1 contradicted by an official source (W0015). 1 partly confirmed and partly contradicted (W0069). 1 partly covered by existing official rows (L0189). 16 still news-only.

## Official versus news conflicts still open

- **OSC under KDN (official W0043; IMI slides Apr 2026) vs under KSM since 1 Jul 2026 (news R0001, RTM quoting the Human Resources Minister).** Under the source rule the official page is the rule. But the news is newer and quotes a Cabinet decision, and the other official page (W0044) also says KSM. The doc should show both. Need to know: MOHR's media statement (blocked).
- **FOMEMA medical: within 3 days of arrival (IMI slides, lead W0069) vs 30 days (IMI page W0047) / 1 month (MOHR policy).** The official rule is 30 days / 1 month.
- **Employment period: "10 + 3 years" (IMI slides, lead W0069) vs up to 10 years (official W0049, R0007).** The official rule is 10 years. The +3-year extension is reported only in the slides and in older news.
- **MoU date: 18 Dec 2022 (news W0015) vs 19 Dec 2021 (official W0027, R0010).** The official date is the rule (settled).
- **Tk 78,990 cost: news B0123 adds a Tk 37,000 agency service charge and employer-paid accommodation.** The official order (R0008, R0009) gives neither figure; it lists the agency service charge only as one of the items inside the Tk 78,990 cap. Don't write Tk 37,000 as fact.
